With the same notation as in Proposition 5.1 , we have $(1+2^{k})2^{k_{\sigma}}\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{B_{k,j}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{4}(m+j)},$ (5.33) for any fixed $\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0}$ , $(k,j),(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J}$ , and $m\in[0,L+1]\cap\mathbb{Z}$ , satisfying $j\geq\beta m/2+N^{\prime}_{0}k_{+}+D^{2},\qquad-10j\leq k_{1},\,k_{2}\leq j/N^{\prime}_{0},\qquad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\leq 10j.$ (5.34) 5.3. Proof of Proposition 5.4 In this subsection we will show that proving Proposition 5.4 can be further reduced
to proving Proposition 5.9 below. The arguments are more complicated than before, and we need to examine our bilinear operators
more carefully; however, in all cases discussed in this subsection we can still control the stronger $B^{1}_{k,j}$ norms. We notice that we are looking to prove the bound ( 5.33 ) for fixed $k,j,k_{1},j_{1},k_{2},j_{2},m$ .
We will consider several cases, depending on the relative sizes of these parameters. Lemma 5.5 . The bound ( 5.33 ) holds provided that ( 5.34 ) holds and, in addition, $j\geq\max(m+D,-k(1+\beta^{2})+D).$ (5.35) Proof of Lemma 5.5 . Using definition ( 4.4 ) it suffices to prove that $\begin{split}&(1+2^{k})(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})\cdot 2^{(1+\beta)j}\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&+(1+2^{k})(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})\cdot 2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}}\big\|\mathcal{F}[\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})]\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{4}(m+j)}.\end{split}$ (5.36) Assume first that $\min(j_{1},j_{2})\leq(1-\beta^{2})j.$ (5.37) By symmetry, we may assume that $j_{1}\leq(1-\beta^{2})j$ and write $\begin{split}&\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}(x)\cdot P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})(x)\\
&=c\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi_{k}(\xi)e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{is[\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta)]}q_{m}(s)\cdot\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta dsd\xi.\end{split}$ We examine the integral in $\xi$ in the formula above. We recall the assumptions ( 5.34 ), ( 5.35 ), and ( 5.37 ), and the last bound in ( 5.17 ). Notice that, using only the assumption ( 5.35 ) and the definition ( 3.12 ) (see also Lemma A.4 ), $\Big|\nabla_{\xi}\big[x\cdot\xi+s[\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta)]\big]\Big|\geq|x|-s\big|\nabla_{\xi}[\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)\big]\big|\geq 2^{j-10}.$ We apply Lemma A.2 (with $K\approx 2^{j}$ , $\epsilon\approx\min(2^{-j_{1}},2^{k})$ ) to conclude that $\big|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}(x)\cdot P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})(x)\big|\lesssim 2^{-10j}|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}(x)|,$ and the desired bounds ( 5.36 ) follow easily. Assume now that $\min(j_{1},j_{2})\geq(1-\beta^{2})j.$ (5.38) By symmetry, we may assume that $k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ . We prove first the bound on the second term in the left-hand side of ( 5.36 ): using ( 5.18 ) we estimate $\begin{split}&(1+2^{k})(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})\cdot 2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}}\|\mathcal{F}[\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})]\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim(1+2^{k})(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}}\cdot 2^{m}\sup_{s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]}\|f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim(1+2^{k})(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}}2^{j}\cdot(2^{\alpha k_{1}}+2^{10k_{1}})^{-1}2^{2\beta\widetilde{k_{1}}}2^{-(1-{\beta})j_{1}}\cdot(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}2^{2\beta\widetilde{k_{2}}}2^{-(1-{\beta})j_{2}}\\
&\lesssim(1+2^{k})2^{j}\cdot 2^{-\alpha k_{1}}\min(2^{(1+\beta)k_{1}},2^{-(1-\beta-\beta^{2})j})\cdot 2^{-(1-{\beta}-\beta^{2})j}.\end{split}$ This suffices to prove the desired bound in ( 5.36 ), as it can be easily seen by considering the cases $k_{1}\leq-j$ and $k_{1}\geq-j$ . Some more care is needed to prove the bound on the first term in the left-hand side of ( 5.36 ). We recall that $f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}=P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}(\widetilde{\varphi}_{j_{1}}^{(k_{1})}\cdot P_{k_{1}}f_{\mu})\quad\text{ and }\quad f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}=P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}(\widetilde{\varphi}_{j_{2}}^{(k_{2})}\cdot P_{k_{2}}f_{\nu}).$ Since $\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{j_{1}}^{(k_{1})}\cdot P_{k_{1}}f_{\mu}(s)\|_{B_{k_{1},j_{1}}}+\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{j_{2}}^{(k_{2})}\cdot P_{k_{2}}f_{\nu}(s)\|_{B_{k_{2},j_{2}}}\lesssim 1$ , see ( 5.15 ), we use ( 4.6 )–( 4.9 ) to decompose $\begin{split}&\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{1})}_{j_{1}}\cdot P_{k_{1}}f_{\mu}(s)=(2^{\alpha k_{1}}+2^{10k_{1}})^{-1}[g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)+h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)],\\
&g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)=g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{1})}_{[j_{1}-2,j_{1}+2]},\qquad h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)=h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{1})}_{[j_{1}-2,j_{1}+2]},\\
&2^{(1+{\beta})j_{1}}\|g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k_{1}}}\|\widehat{g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1,\\
&2^{(1-{\beta})j_{1}}\|h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}+2^{\gamma j_{1}}\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{-8|k_{1}|},\end{split}$ (5.39) and $\begin{split}&\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{2})}_{j_{2}}\cdot P_{k_{2}}f_{\nu}(s)=(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}[g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)+h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)],\\
&g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)=g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{2})}_{[j_{2}-2,j_{2}+2]},\qquad h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)=h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{2})}_{[j_{2}-2,j_{2}+2]},\\
&2^{(1+{\beta})j_{2}}\|g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k_{2}}}\|\widehat{g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1,\\
&2^{(1-{\beta})j_{2}}\|h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}+2^{\gamma j_{2}}\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{-8|k_{2}|}.\end{split}$ (5.40) Using these decompositions and recalling the definition ( 5.13 ), to prove the desired bound on the first term in the left-hand side of ( 5.36 ), it suffices to prove that for any $s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]$ $\begin{split}(1+2^{k})&(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})2^{(1+\beta)j}\cdot(2^{\alpha k_{1}}+2^{10k_{1}})^{-1}(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}2^{m}\\
\Big[&\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
+&\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
+&\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
+&\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\Big]\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{4}(m+j)}.\end{split}$ (5.41) Recall that we assumed $k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ ; therefore we may also assume that $k\leq k_{2}+4$ . Using ( 5.39 )–( 5.40 ) and recalling ( 5.38 ), we estimate $\begin{split}\big\|P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim\|\mathcal{F}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu})(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{3k_{1}/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j_{1}}2^{-(1+\beta)j_{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{3k_{1}/2}2^{-(2+2\beta)(1-\beta^{2})j},\end{split}$ $\begin{split}\big\|P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\gamma j_{1}}2^{-8|k_{1}|}2^{-(1-\beta)j_{2}}2^{-8|k_{2}|}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-8|k_{1}|}2^{-(2+2\beta)(1-\beta^{2})j},\end{split}$ $\begin{split}\big\|P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\widehat{g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\gamma j_{1}}2^{-8|k_{1}|}2^{-(1+\beta)j_{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-8|k_{1}|}2^{-(2+2\beta)(1-\beta^{2})j},\end{split}$ and $\begin{split}\big\|P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}&(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\min\big(2^{3k_{1}/2}\|\widehat{g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}},\|\widehat{g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\big)\\
&\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j_{1}}2^{-8|k_{2}|}\min\big(2^{-(1-\beta)j_{2}}2^{3k_{1}/2},2^{-\gamma j_{2}}\big)\\
&\lesssim 2^{-(2+2\beta)(1-\beta^{2})j}2^{3k_{1}/4}2^{-8|k_{2}|}.\end{split}$ Since $2^{m}\lesssim 2^{j}$ and $(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}\lesssim 1$ , the left-hand side of ( 5.41 ) is dominated by $C(1+2^{k})2^{(1+\beta)j}\cdot(2^{\alpha k_{1}}+2^{10k_{1}})^{-1}2^{j}\cdot 2^{-(2+2\beta)(1-\beta^{2})j}(2^{3k_{1}/2}+2^{3k_{1}/4}2^{-8|k_{2}|})\lesssim 2^{-2\beta j/3}(1+2^{k}),$ which suffices since $2^{k}\lesssim 2^{j/N^{\prime}_{0}}$ . This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎ Lemma 5.6 . The bound ( 5.33 ) holds provided that ( 5.34 ) holds and, in addition, $\max(m+D,j)\leq-k(1+\beta^{2})+D.$ (5.42) Proof of Lemma 5.6 . In view of the restrictions ( 5.42 ) and ( 5.34 ), we may assume that $k\leq-D^{2}/2$ . Using the definition, it is easy to see that $\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}h\|_{B^{1}_{k,j}}\lesssim(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})2^{(1+\beta)j}2^{3k/2}\|\widehat{P_{k}h}\|_{L^{\infty}}.$ (5.43) Therefore, it suffices to prove that $2^{k_{\sigma}}2^{\alpha k}2^{(1+\beta)j}2^{3k/2}\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{4}(m+j)}.$ (5.44) Using ( 5.18 ) and recalling $\alpha\leq 2\beta$ we estimate $\begin{split}\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},&f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\|f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\,ds\\
&\lesssim\|q_{m}\|_{L^{1}}\cdot(2^{\alpha k_{1}}+2^{10k_{1}})^{-1}2^{2\beta\widetilde{k_{1}}}2^{-(1-\beta)j_{1}}\cdot(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}2^{2\beta\widetilde{k_{2}}}2^{-(1-\beta)j_{2}}\\
&\lesssim\|q_{m}\|_{L^{1}}\min(1,2^{-5k_{1}})2^{-(1-\beta)j_{1}}\cdot\min(1,2^{-5k_{2}})2^{-(1-\beta)j_{2}}.\end{split}$ Recalling the definitions ( 4.1 ) and the assumptions, the desired bound ( 5.44 ) follows if $\sigma=i\,\,\,\text{ or }\,\,\,m=L+1\,\,\,\text{ or }\,\,\,m\leq(1-\beta)(j_{1}+j_{2})-(1/2-\beta)k.$ It remains to prove the bound ( 5.44 ) in the case $\sigma\in\{e,b\}\,\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\,m\in[1,L]\cap\mathbb{Z}\,\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\,m\geq-(1/2-\beta)k+(1-\beta)(j_{1}+j_{2}).$ (5.45) Since $j_{1}+k_{1}\geq 0$ , $j_{2}+k_{2}\geq 0$ , and $k\leq-D^{2}/2$ , the conditions ( 5.42 ) and ( 5.45 ) show that $k_{1},k_{2}\geq k/4$ and $|k_{1}-k_{2}|\leq 10$ . Using also ( 5.42 ), for ( 5.44 ) it suffices to prove that, assuming ( 5.45 ), $\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-k(1/2+\alpha-\beta-2\beta^{2})}.$ (5.46) To prove ( 5.46 ) we would like to integrate by parts in $\eta$ and $s$ in the formula ( 5.3 ). Recall the definitions $\mathcal{F}P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})(\xi)=\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}q_{m}(s)\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,$ where $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)=\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta).$ We decompose $\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})(\xi)=G(\xi)+H(\xi),\\
&G(\xi):=\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\varphi(2^{20D}(1+2^{k_{2}})\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))q_{m}(s)\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,\\
&H(\xi):=\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}[1-\varphi(2^{20D}(1+2^{k_{2}})\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))]q_{m}(s)\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds.\end{split}$ The function $H$ can be estimated using integration by parts in $s$ , ( 5.20 ), the assumptions ( 5.7 ), and the bounds ( 5.17 ). Indeed, $\begin{split}|H(\xi)|&\lesssim(1+2^{k_{2}})\sup_{s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]}\big[\big\|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\big\|_{L^{2}}\big\|\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&+2^{m}\big\|(\partial_{s}\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}})(s)\big\|_{L^{2}}\big\|\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\big\|_{L^{2}}+2^{m}\big\|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\big\|_{L^{2}}\big\|(\partial_{s}\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}})(s)\big\|_{L^{2}}\big]\\
&\lesssim\min(1,2^{-(N_{0}-10)k_{2}}).\end{split}$ Therefore, for ( 5.46 ) it suffices to prove that $\big\|G\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-k(1/2+\alpha-\beta-2\beta^{2})}.$ (5.47) Recall the definitions ( 5.14 ), $\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)=(\nabla_{\eta}\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(\xi,\eta)=-\iota_{1}\nabla\Lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(\eta-\xi)-\iota_{2}\nabla\Lambda_{\sigma_{2}}(\eta),$ (5.48) where $\mu=(\sigma_{1}\iota_{1}),\qquad\nu=(\sigma_{2}\iota_{2}),\qquad\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{i,e,b\},\qquad\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{+,-\}.$ For $l\in\mathbb{Z}$ let $\begin{split}G_{\leq l}(\xi):=\varphi_{k}(\xi)&\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi_{(-\infty,l]}(\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))\cdot e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\\
&\varphi(2^{20D}(1+2^{k_{2}})\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))q_{m}(s)\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds.\end{split}$ (5.49) Let $G_{l}:=G_{\leq l}-G_{\leq l-1}$ . In proving ( 5.47 ) we may assume that $j_{1}\leq j_{2}$ . If $l\geq l_{0}=-20D-4\max(k_{2},0)$ then we integrate by parts in $\eta$ , using Lemma A.2 with $K\approx 2^{m+l}$ and $\epsilon^{-1}\approx 2^{j_{2}}+2^{-\min(l,0)-\min(k_{2},0)}+2^{k_{2}}$ . Using also the last bound in ( 5.17 ), ( 5.42 ), and ( 5.45 ) to ensure $\epsilon K\geq 2^{\beta^{2}m}$ , it follows that $\sum_{l\geq l_{0}+1}\|G_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(1+2^{5k_{2}})^{-1}.$ (5.50) It remains to estimate $\|G_{\leq l_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}$ . Since $\sigma\neq i$ , it follows from Proposition B.2 that $G_{\leq l_{0}}\equiv 0$ . This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎ Lemma 5.7 . The bound ( 5.33 ) holds provided that ( 5.34 ) holds and, in addition, $j\leq m+D\quad\text{ and }\quad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\geq(1-\beta/10)m+k_{\sigma},$ (5.51) or $j\leq m+D\quad\text{ and }\quad\min(k_{1},k_{2})\leq-9m/10.$ (5.52) Proof of Lemma 5.7 . Assume first that ( 5.52 ) holds. We estimate, assuming $k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ and using ( 5.17 ), $\begin{split}(1+2^{k})2^{k_{\sigma}}&(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})2^{(1+\beta)j}2^{3k/2}\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{(1+\beta)j}2^{m}(1+2^{11k})2^{3k_{2}/2}\sup_{s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]}\|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{(2+\beta)m}(1+2^{11k})2^{3k_{2}/2}\cdot 2^{5k_{1}/2}2^{-k_{2}/2}.\end{split}$ The desired bound ( 5.33 ) follows using also ( 5.43 ). Assume now that ( 5.51 ) holds. Using definition ( 4.4 ), it suffices to prove that $\begin{split}&(1+2^{k})2^{k_{\sigma}}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})\cdot 2^{(1+\beta)j}\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&+(1+2^{k})2^{k_{\sigma}}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})\cdot 2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}}\big\|\mathcal{F}[\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})]\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{4}(m+j)}.\end{split}$ (5.53) By symmetry, we may assume $k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ . We prove first the bounds ( 5.53 ) in the case $k_{1}\leq-5m/6.$ (5.54) Using ( 5.17 ), for any $s\in[0,t]$ , $\|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{3k_{1}}\|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{(5/2-\alpha+\beta)k_{1}}.$ Therefore, using ( 5.17 ) again, it follows that $\begin{split}\big\|\mathcal{F}[T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})]\big\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{m}\sup_{s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]}\|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{m}2^{(5/2-\alpha+\beta)k_{1}}\min(2^{-(N_{0}-1)k_{2}},2^{(1+\beta-\alpha)k_{2}})\end{split}$ and $\begin{split}\big\|\mathcal{F}[T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim 2^{m}\sup_{s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]}\|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{m}2^{(5/2-\alpha+\beta)k_{1}}\cdot(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}2^{-(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k_{2}}}.\end{split}$ (5.55) Therefore, recalling ( 5.54 ), if $k\leq 0$ then the left-hand side of ( 5.53 ) is dominated by $C2^{(2+\beta)m}2^{(5/2-\alpha+\beta)k_{1}}\lesssim 2^{(-1/12+5\alpha/6+{\beta}/6)m},$ which suffices. Similarly, if $k\geq 0$ then the left-hand side of ( 5.53 ) is dominated by $C2^{(2+\beta)m}2^{(5/2-\alpha+\beta)k_{1}}2^{-(N_{0}-15)k}+C2^{k_{2}}2^{m}2^{(5/2-\alpha+\beta)k_{1}}\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}2^{(-1/12+5\alpha/6+{\beta}/6)m},$ which also suffices. To prove the bound ( 5.53 ) when $-5m/6\leq k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ we decompose, as in ( 5.39 )–( 5.40 ), for any $s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]$ , $\begin{split}&\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{1})}_{j_{1}}\cdot P_{k_{1}}f_{\mu}(s)=(2^{\alpha k_{1}}+2^{10k_{1}})^{-1}[g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)+h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)],\\
&g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)=g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{1})}_{[j_{1}-2,j_{1}+2]},\qquad h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)=h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{1})}_{[j_{1}-2,j_{1}+2]},\\
&2^{(1+{\beta})j_{1}}\|g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k_{1}}}\|\widehat{g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1,\\
&2^{(1-{\beta})j_{1}}\|h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}+2^{\gamma j_{1}}\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{-8|k_{1}|},\end{split}$ (5.56) and $\begin{split}&\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{2})}_{j_{2}}\cdot P_{k_{2}}f_{\nu}(s)=(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}[g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)+h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)],\\
&g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)=g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{2})}_{[j_{2}-2,j_{2}+2]},\qquad h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)=h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{2})}_{[j_{2}-2,j_{2}+2]},\\
&2^{(1+{\beta})j_{2}}\|g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k_{2}}}\|\widehat{g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1,\\
&2^{(1-{\beta})j_{2}}\|h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}+2^{\gamma j_{2}}\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{-8|k_{2}|}.\end{split}$ (5.57) We will prove now the $L^{2}$ bound $(1+2^{k})2^{k_{\sigma}}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})\cdot 2^{(2+\beta)m}\big\|P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}m},$ (5.58) for any $s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]$ , see ( 5.13 ) for the definition of the bilinear operators $\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ . In view of the assumption ( 5.51 ) this would clearly imply the desired $L^{2}$ bound in ( 5.53 ). Assume first that $\min(j_{1},j_{2})\leq(1-9\beta)m$ , i.e. $\min(j_{1},j_{2})\leq(1-15\beta)m,\qquad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\geq(1-\beta/10)m+k_{\sigma},\qquad k_{2}\geq k_{1}\geq-5m/6.$ (5.59) Using ( 5.18 ) and ( 5.19 ), $\begin{split}\|P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}&(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\min(\|Ef_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|Ef_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{2}},\|Ef_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|Ef_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}})\\
&\lesssim\min(2^{\beta k_{1}},2^{-6k_{1}})\min(2^{\beta k_{2}},2^{-6k_{2}})\cdot 2^{-m(5/4-10\beta)}2^{(1/4-11\beta)\min(j_{1},j_{2})}2^{-(1-\beta)\max(j_{1},j_{2})}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-k_{\sigma}}(1+2^{k_{2}})^{-6}2^{-(2+3\beta/2)m},\end{split}$ which suffices to prove ( 5.58 ). Assume now that $\min(j_{1},j_{2})\geq(1-15\beta)m$ , i.e. $\min(j_{1},j_{2})\geq(1-15\beta)m,\qquad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\geq(1-\beta/10)m+k_{\sigma},\qquad k_{2}\geq k_{1}\geq-5m/6.$ (5.60) We recall that $\begin{split}&f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}=P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}(\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{1})}_{j_{1}}\cdot P_{k_{1}}f_{\mu})=(2^{\alpha k_{1}}+2^{10k_{1}})^{-1}[P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}+P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}],\\
&f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}=P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}(\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{2})}_{j_{2}}\cdot P_{k_{2}}f_{\nu})=(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}[P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}+P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}],\end{split}$ (5.61) and use the bounds in ( 5.56 )–( 5.57 ). Then we estimate, using also ( 5.60 ), $\begin{split}\big\|P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),&P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\gamma j_{1}}2^{-(1-\beta)j_{2}}2^{-8|k_{1}|}2^{-8|k_{2}|}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-(\gamma+1-25\beta)m}2^{-6|k_{1}|}2^{-8|k_{2}|}\end{split}$ $\begin{split}\big\|P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),&P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\widehat{g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\gamma j_{1}}2^{-(1+\beta)j_{2}}2^{-8|k_{1}|}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-m(\gamma+1-25\beta)}2^{-6|k_{1}|},\end{split}$ $\begin{split}\big\|P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),&P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\widehat{g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j_{1}}2^{-\gamma j_{2}}2^{-8|k_{2}|}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-m(\gamma+1-25\beta)}2^{-8|k_{2}|}2^{-k_{\sigma}},\end{split}$ and, using also ( A.21 )–( A.25 ) (compare with the bounds ( 5.19 )), $\begin{split}&\big\|P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\min\big(\|e^{-is\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}}P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}(g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s))\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{2}},\|g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|e^{-is\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}}P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}(g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\\
&\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)\max(j_{1},j_{2})}\cdot 2^{-m(5/4-10\beta)}2^{(1/4-11\beta)\min(j_{1},j_{2})}(1+2^{4k_{2}})\\
&\lesssim 2^{-k_{\sigma}}(1+2^{4k_{2}})2^{-(2+19\beta/10)m}.\end{split}$ Therefore, using also $\alpha\in[0,{\beta}/2]$ and $k_{1}\geq-5m/6$ , the left-hand side of ( 5.58 ) is dominated by $C(1+2^{5k_{2}})2^{-\alpha k_{1}}2^{-9\beta m/10}\lesssim(1+2^{5k_{2}})2^{-29m\beta/60}.$ This completes the proof of ( 5.58 ). To complete the proof of ( 5.53 ) it remains to prove the $L^{\infty}$ bound.
This would follow from the estimate $(1+2^{k})2^{k_{\sigma}}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})\cdot 2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}}2^{m}\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}m}$ (5.62) for all $s\in[2^{m-2},2^{m+2}]$ .
If $k_{1}\leq-2m/5$ then, as in ( 5.55 ), $\begin{split}\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim\|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{(5/2-\alpha+\beta)k_{1}}(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}2^{-(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}_{2}},\end{split}$ and therefore the left-hand side of ( 5.62 ) is dominated by $C(1+2^{k})2^{k_{\sigma}}\cdot(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}\cdot 2^{(1/2-\beta)(\widetilde{k}-\widetilde{k}_{2})}\cdot 2^{m}2^{(5/2-\alpha+\beta)k_{1}},$ which is sufficient. We now assume that $-2m/5\leq k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ and we decompose $f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}$ as in ( 5.56 ), ( 5.57 ), ( 5.61 ). If $j_{1}\leq j_{2}$ , we estimate $\begin{split}\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}(g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)+h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)),&P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim\big(\|\widehat{g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\widehat{g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{(1+\beta)\widetilde{k}_{1}}2^{-(1+\beta)j_{2}},\end{split}$ and $\begin{split}\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}(g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)+h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)),&P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim\big(\|\widehat{g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k_{1}}}\cdot 2^{-8|k_{2}|}2^{-\gamma j_{2}}.\end{split}$ Since $-\widetilde{k_{1}}\leq 2m/5$ , $\alpha\leq\beta$ and $2^{j_{2}}\gtrsim 2^{m(1-\beta/10)}2^{k_{\sigma}}$ it follows that if $j_{1}\leq j_{2}$ then $\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)k_{\sigma}}2^{-(1+\beta)(1-\beta/10)m}\cdot(2^{\alpha k_{1}}+2^{10k_{1}})^{-1}(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}.$ (5.63) Similarly, if $j_{1}\geq j_{2}$ we estimate $\begin{split}\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),&P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}(g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)+h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)))\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim\|\widehat{g_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\big(\|\widehat{g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\big)\\
&\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j_{1}}2^{(1+\beta)\widetilde{k}_{2}},\end{split}$ and $\begin{split}\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),&P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}(g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)+h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)))\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim\|\widehat{h_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\big(\|\widehat{g_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\widehat{h_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\gamma j_{1}}2^{-6|k_{1}|}.\end{split}$ Since $2^{j_{1}}\gtrsim 2^{m(1-\beta/10)}2^{k_{\sigma}}$ it follows that if $j_{1}\geq j_{2}$ then $\big\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}\widetilde{T}_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s),f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s))\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)k_{\sigma}}2^{-(1+\beta)(1-\beta/10)m}\cdot(2^{\alpha k_{1}}+2^{10k_{1}})^{-1}(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}.$ (5.64) Using ( 5.63 ) and ( 5.64 ), the left-hand side of ( 5.62 ) is dominated by $C(1+2^{k})2^{-\alpha k_{1}}2^{-4\beta m/5},$ which suffices. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎ Now that we have identified $m$ as the largest parameter,
we may remove the non-resonant part of the nonlinearity. For any $\kappa\in(0,1]$ we define $\begin{split}T_{m}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g)&=R_{m,\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g)+N_{m}^{1;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g)+N_{m,\kappa}^{2;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g),\\
\mathcal{F}\big[N_{m}^{1;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g)\big](\xi)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\chi_{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)q_{m}(s)\cdot\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{g}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,\\
\chi_{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)&:=\varphi_{[1,\infty)}(2^{D^{2}+\max(0,k_{1},k_{2})}\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)),\\
\mathcal{F}\big[N_{m,\kappa}^{2;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g)\big](\xi)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\chi_{S}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)q_{m}(s)\cdot\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{g}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,\\
\chi_{S}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)&:=\varphi(2^{D^{2}+\max(0,k_{1},k_{2})}\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))\varphi_{[1,\infty)}(|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|/\kappa),\\
\mathcal{F}\big[R_{m,\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g)\big](\xi)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)q_{m}(s)\cdot\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{g}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,\\
\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)&:=\varphi(2^{D^{2}+\max(0,k_{1},k_{2})}\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))\varphi(|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|/\kappa).\end{split}$ (5.65) Our last lemma in this section shows that only the resonant part of the interaction $R_{m,\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ may produce more problematic outputs not in $B^{1}_{j,k}$ . Lemma 5.8 . Assume that $\sigma\in\{i,e,b\}$ , $\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0}$ , $(k,j),(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J}$ , $m\in[0,L+1]\cap\mathbb{Z}$ , and $\begin{split}&-9m/10\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq j/N_{0}^{\prime},\quad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\leq(1-\beta/10)m+k_{\sigma},\\
&\beta m/2+N_{0}^{\prime}k_{+}+D^{2}\leq j\leq m+D,\quad m\geq-k(1+\beta^{2}).\end{split}$ (5.66) Then, assuming $m\in[0,L]\cap\mathbb{Z}$ , $\begin{split}(1+2^{k})\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}N_{m}^{1;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})\|_{B^{1}_{k,j}}&\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}m},\\
(1+2^{k})\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}N_{m,\kappa}^{2;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})\|_{B^{1}_{k,j}}&\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}m},\end{split}$ (5.67) for any $\kappa\in(0,1]$ satisfying $2^{m}\kappa\geq 2^{\beta^{2}m}2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})},\qquad 2^{m}\kappa\geq 2^{\beta^{2}m}\kappa^{-1}2^{-\min(k_{1},k_{2},0)}2^{-D}.$ (5.68) Moreover, for $m=L+1$ , $(1+2^{k})\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}T_{L+1}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})\|_{B^{1}_{k,j}}\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}L}.$ (5.69) Proof of Lemma 5.8 . To prove the second inequality in ( 5.67 ) we use Lemma A.2 and the assumptions ( 5.68 ) to show that $|\mathcal{F}\big[N_{m,\kappa}^{2;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})\big](\xi)|\lesssim 2^{-10m}.$ (5.70) The second inequality in ( 5.67 ) follows easily using ( 5.66 ). To prove the first inequality in ( 5.67 ) when $m\leq L$ , we first integrate by parts in $s$ and obtain that $\begin{split}\mathcal{F}\big[N_{m}^{1;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g)\big](\xi)&=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\chi_{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}{i\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\cdot\partial_{s}\left[q_{m}(s)\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{g}(\eta,s)\right]\,d\eta ds.\\
\end{split}$ (5.71) Therefore $\begin{split}\mathcal{F}\big[N_{m}^{1;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})\big]&=i\left[N_{11}+N_{12}+N_{13}\right],\\
N_{11}(\xi)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\chi_{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}{\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}q^{\prime}_{m}(s)\cdot\widehat{f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,\\
N_{12}(\xi)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\chi_{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}{\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}q_{m}(s)\cdot(\partial_{s}\widehat{f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}})(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,\\
N_{13}(\xi)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\chi_{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}{\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}q_{m}(s)\cdot\widehat{f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta,s)(\partial_{s}\widehat{f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}})(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds.\end{split}$ We show first that $(1+2^{k})(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})2^{(1+\beta)m}\|P_{k}N_{m}^{1;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}m}.$ (5.72) We may assume $k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ . Using symbol type estimates, it is easy to see that $\Big\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left[\frac{\chi_{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}{\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\varphi_{[k-4,k+4]}(\xi)\varphi_{[k_{1}-4,k_{1}+4]}(\xi-\eta)\varphi_{[k_{2}-4,k_{2}+4]}(\eta)\right]\Big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim 2^{20\max(0,k_{2})}.$ (5.73) Using the decomposition ( 5.71 ), Lemma A.3 , and ( 5.73 ), we see that $\begin{split}&\|P_{k}N_{m}^{1;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{20\max(0,k_{2})}\sup_{s\in[2^{m-2},2^{m+2}]}\Big[\min\left\{\|Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}},\|f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|Ef^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\right\}\\
&+2^{m}\|Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|(\partial_{s}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})(s)\|_{L^{2}}+2^{m}\|(\partial_{s}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}})(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|Ef^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big].\end{split}$ (5.74) It follows from ( 5.17 ) and ( 5.20 ) that $2^{m}\|Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|(\partial_{s}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})(s)\|_{L^{2}}+2^{m}\|(\partial_{s}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}})(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|Ef^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-6\max(k_{2},0)}2^{-(1+2\beta)m}.$ Moreover, using ( 5.17 )–( 5.18 ), $\begin{split}\min\left\{\|Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}},\|f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|Ef^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\right\}\\
\lesssim 2^{-6\max(k_{2},0)}2^{-(1+\beta)m}2^{-(1-\beta)\max(j_{1},j_{2})}.\end{split}$ Finally, if $\max(j_{1},j_{2})\leq 2\beta m$ then, using ( 5.18 ) and ( 5.19 ), $\min\left\{\|Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}},\|f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|Ef^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\right\}\lesssim 2^{-6\max(k_{2},0)}2^{-(5/4-15\beta)m}.$ It follows from the last three bounds and ( 5.74 ) that $\|P_{k}N_{m}^{1;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{15\max(k_{2},0)}2^{-(1+2\beta)m},$ and the desired bound ( 5.72 ) follows since $2^{k}\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}\lesssim 2^{m/N^{\prime}_{0}}$ . We show now that $(1+2^{k})2^{(1/2-\beta)\tilde{k}}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}N_{m}^{1;\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}m}.$ (5.75) We may assume $k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ , and use the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, ( 5.17 ), and ( A.60 ) to see that $\begin{split}&\|N_{12}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|N_{13}\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{\max(0,k_{2})}2^{m}\sup_{s\in[2^{m-2},2^{m+2}]}\big[\|(\partial_{s}\widehat{f}^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}})(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{f}^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\widehat{f}^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|(\partial_{s}\widehat{f}^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})(s)\|_{L^{2}}\big]\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\beta m}(1+2^{(N_{0}-10)k_{2}})^{-1}.\end{split}$ This implies that $N_{12}$ and $N_{13}$ give acceptable contributions to ( 5.75 ).
Proceeding as above, using ( 5.18 ) we also get $\|N_{11}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(1+2^{k_{2}})2^{\beta\widetilde{k}_{1}}2^{-(1-\beta)j_{1}}\min(2^{-(N_{0}-5)k_{2}},2^{-(1-\beta)j_{2}}).$ Therefore, this gives an acceptable contribution to ( 5.75 ) unless $|k|+|k_{1}|+|k_{2}|+j_{1}+j_{2}\leq\beta^{2}m.$ (5.76) Assuming that ( 5.76 ) holds, we need to strenghten the $L^{\infty}$ bound on $N_{11}$ slightly. We decompose $\begin{split}N_{11}&=N_{11;1}+N_{11;2},\\
N_{11;1}(\xi)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\chi_{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}{\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\varphi(\delta^{-1}|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|)q^{\prime}_{m}(s)\cdot\widehat{f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,\\
N_{11;2}(\xi)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\chi_{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}{\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}[1-\varphi(\delta^{-1}|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|)]q^{\prime}_{m}(s)\cdot\widehat{f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,\\
\end{split}$ with $\delta:=2^{-m/3}$ . Applying Lemma A.2 with $K=2^{2m/3}$ , $\epsilon=2^{-m/3}$ , it is easy to see that $|N_{11;2}(\xi)|\lesssim 2^{-10m},$ provided that ( 5.76 ) holds, which is clearly sufficient. On the other hand, using the definition ( 5.14 ) and the bounds ( A.5 ), we observe that $\begin{split}|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|&\gtrsim|\nabla\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta)|\cdot\min(\big|(\xi-\eta)/|\xi-\eta|-\eta/|\eta|\big|,\big|(\xi-\eta)/|\xi-\eta|+\eta/|\eta|\big|)\\
&\gtrsim 2^{-\beta m}\min(\big|(\xi-\eta)/|\xi-\eta|-\eta/|\eta|\big|,\big|(\xi-\eta)/|\xi-\eta|+\eta/|\eta|\big|).\end{split}$ Consequently, if $|\xi|\in[2^{k-2},2^{k+2}]$ , $|\xi-\eta|\in[2^{k_{1}-2},2^{k_{1}+2}]$ and $|\eta|\in[2^{k_{2}-2},2^{k_{2}+2}]$ , and $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim 2^{-m/3}$ then $\min\big(|\eta/|\eta|-\xi/|\xi||,|\eta/|\eta|+\xi/|\xi||\big)\lesssim 2^{-m/4}.$ Then, a simple estimate using the $L^{\infty}$ bounds in ( 5.17 ) gives $|N_{11;1}(\xi)|\lesssim 2^{-m/6}$ , which is sufficient to finish the proof of ( 5.75 ). The first bound in ( 5.67 ) follows from ( 5.72 ) and ( 5.75 ). The bound ( 5.69 ) follows by a similar (in fact easier) argument; since $\|q_{L+1}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 1$ one does not need to integrate by parts in $s$ and one can simply estimate the appropriate $L^{2}$ and $L^{\infty}$ norms in the same way we estimated the contributions of the function $N_{11}$ in the argument above.
∎ We examine now the conclusions of Lemma 5.5 , Lemma 5.6 , Lemma 5.7 , and Lemma 5.8 . We notice that to complete the proof of Proposition 5.4 , it suffices to prove Proposition 5.9 below. Proposition 5.9 . Assume that $\sigma\in\{i,e,b\}$ , $\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0}$ , $(k,j),(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J}$ , $m\in[1,L]\cap\mathbb{Z}$ , and $\begin{split}&-9m/10\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq j/N_{0}^{\prime},\quad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\leq(1-\beta/10)m+k_{\sigma},\\
&\beta m/2+N_{0}^{\prime}k_{+}+D^{2}\leq j\leq m+D,\quad m\geq-k(1+\beta^{2}).\end{split}$ (5.77) Then there is $\kappa\in(0,1]$ , $\kappa\geq\max\big(2^{(\beta^{2}m-m)/2}2^{-\min(k_{1},k_{2},0)/2}2^{-D/2},2^{\beta^{2}m-m}2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})}\big)$ , such that $(1+2^{k})2^{k_{\sigma}}\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}R_{m,\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{B_{k,j}}\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}m}.$ (5.78) We prove this proposition in the next 3 sections. We consider several types of resonant interactions, which involve input and output frequencies located on spheres or at the origin, as well as the different phase functions $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ . We classify these interactions into 3 basic types, see Proposition B.2 , and analyze the contributions separately in the next 3 sections. The optimal value of $\kappa$ for which we prove ( 5.78 ) depends, of course, on all the other parameters. 6. Proof of Proposition 4.3 , II: Case A resonant interactions In this section we consider type A interactions, see Proposition B.2 , and prove the following proposition: Proposition 6.1 . Assume that $(k,j),(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J}$ , $m\in[1,L]\cap\mathbb{Z}$ , $\begin{split}\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{A}:=\{&\Phi^{i;e+,i-},\Phi^{i;b+,i-},\Phi^{i;b-,e+},\Phi^{i;b+,e-},\Phi^{e;e+,i+},\Phi^{e;b+,i+},\Phi^{e;b+,i-},\Phi^{e;b+,e-},\\
&\Phi^{b;e+,i+},\Phi^{b;b+,i+},\Phi^{b;e+,e+},\Phi^{b;b+,e+},\Phi^{b;b+,e-}\},\end{split}$ (6.1) and $-D/2\leq k,k_{1},k_{2}\leq D/2,\quad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\leq(1-\beta/10)m,\quad\beta m/2+N_{0}^{\prime}k_{+}+D^{2}\leq j\leq m+D.$ (6.2) Then there is $\kappa\in(0,1]$ , $\kappa\geq\max\big(2^{(\beta^{2}m-m)/2},2^{\beta^{2}m-m}2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})}\big)$ , such that $\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}R_{m,\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{B_{k,j}}\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}m}.$ (6.3) The phases in the set $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{A}$ are the same as the phases in the set $\mathcal{T}_{A}$ , after interchanging the last two indices. Without loss of generality we may assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{A}$ instead of $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\mathcal{T}_{A}$ . The rest of the section is concerned with the proof of Proposition 6.1 . The interactions corresponding to Case A are among the most difficult to control. In particular, they produce outputs which fail to belong to the “strong” $B^{1}_{k,j}$ spaces. A key element we need is a precise description of the sizes of the various elements close to the resonant set. This is made possible by the fact that the Hessian of the phases is nondegenerate. We refer to the introduction of [ 29 ] for more details. We define first the interaction functions for the space-resonant phases in $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{A}$ given in ( 6.1 ), the functions $p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ and $q^{\mu,\nu}$ defined below. They help us to characterize the vanishing set for $\Xi^{\mu,\nu}$ through the equality ( 6.4 ). Only the functions $p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ will play an essential role, but the functions $q^{\mu,\nu}$ appear as simpler natural intermediate functions. Our goal is to define these functions such that $\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta),\eta)=0=\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi)),$ (6.4) where the first equality holds for all $\eta$ and the second equality holds for all $\xi$ where $p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi)$ is well defined. For this we first define $p^{b;e+,e+}(\xi):=\xi/2,\quad q^{e+,e+}(\eta):=2\eta,\qquad t^{e,e}(r):=r.$ (6.5) The other functions require a little more care. We first define $q^{\mu,\nu}$ and then invert the process. We define the real-valued functions $t^{ei},t^{bi},t^{be}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ by the relation $\lambda_{e}^{\prime}(t^{ei}(r))=\lambda_{b}^{\prime}(t^{bi}(r))=\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r),\quad\lambda_{b}^{\prime}(t^{be}(r))=\lambda_{e}^{\prime}(r).$ Since $\lambda_{e}^{\prime}$ and $\lambda_{b}^{\prime}$ are injective (see Lemma A.4 ) and using also ( B.14 ), these functions are well defined.
We can directly see that $t^{bi}(r)\leq t^{ei}(r)$ , $t^{be}(r)\leq r$ and since $\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r)\in[\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r_{\ast}),\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(0)]\subseteq\Big[\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r_{\ast}),\frac{\sqrt{1+T}}{\sqrt{1+\varepsilon}}\Big],\qquad\text{ for any }r\in[0,\infty),$ we get from Lemma A.4 that $\sqrt{\varepsilon}\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r_{\ast})/(2T)\leq t^{ei}(r)\leq\sqrt{3\varepsilon/T},\quad\sqrt{\varepsilon}\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r_{\ast})/C_{b}\leq t^{bi}(r)\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon/C_{b}},\quad 0\leq t^{be}(r)\leq\frac{\sqrt{T(1+\varepsilon)}}{\sqrt{C_{b}^{2}-TC_{b}}}.$ (6.6) More precisely we have $t^{bi}(r)=\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon)}}{\sqrt{C_{b}}}\frac{\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r)}{\sqrt{C_{b}-\varepsilon(\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r))^{2}}},\quad t^{be}(r)=\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon)}}{\sqrt{C_{b}}}\frac{\lambda_{e}^{\prime}(r)}{\sqrt{C_{b}-\varepsilon(\lambda_{e}^{\prime}(r))^{2}}},$ while $t^{ei}$ has a similar behavior. Note in particular that $T(t^{ei}(r))^{2}\leq 3\varepsilon$ , $C_{b}(t^{bi}(r))^{2}\leq\varepsilon$ , $C_{b}(t^{be}(r))^{2}\leq T(1+\varepsilon)/(C_{b}-T)$ . Let $(\partial t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}})(r):=\frac{dt^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r)}{dr},\qquad(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in\{(e,e),(e,i),(b,i),(b,e)\}.$ Using Lemma A.4 , $\begin{split}&\big|(\partial t^{ei})(r)\big|=\Big|\frac{\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)}{\lambda_{e}^{\prime\prime}(t^{ei}(r))}\Big|\leq|\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)|\frac{\varepsilon^{1/2}(1+Tt^{ei}(r)^{2})^{3/2}}{T(1-\sqrt{\varepsilon})}\leq\frac{8\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}(1+3\varepsilon)^{3/2}T}{(1-\sqrt{\varepsilon})T}\leq\frac{1}{2},\\
&\big|(\partial t^{bi})(r)\big|=\Big|\frac{\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{i}(r)}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{b}(t^{bi}(r))}\Big|\leq|\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{i}(r)|\frac{\varepsilon^{1/2}(1+\varepsilon+C_{b}t^{bi}(r)^{2})^{3/2}}{C_{b}(1+\varepsilon)}\leq\frac{8\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}(1+\varepsilon)^{1/2}TC_{b}^{1/2}}{(C_{b}-T)^{3/2}}\leq\frac{1}{2},\\
&C_{C_{b},\varepsilon}^{-1}\leq(\partial t^{be})(r)=\frac{\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r)}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{b}(t^{be}(r))}\leq\frac{(1+\sqrt{\varepsilon})T}{(1+Tr^{2})^{3/2}}\frac{(1+\varepsilon+C_{b}t^{be}(r)^{2})^{3/2}}{C_{b}(1+\varepsilon)}\leq\frac{(1+4\sqrt{\varepsilon})TC_{b}^{1/2}}{(C_{b}-T)^{3/2}}\leq\frac{1}{2}.\end{split}$ (6.7) We now define $q^{\mu,\nu}$ when $(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in\{(e,i),(b,i),(b,e)\}$ by the formula $q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta):=\eta+(\iota_{1}\cdot\iota_{2})t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(|\eta|)\frac{\eta}{|\eta|}=\tilde{t}^{\mu,\nu}(|\eta|)\frac{\eta}{|\eta|},$ such that $\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta),\eta)=0$ . Then we define the function $r^{\mu,\nu}(s)$ as the inverse function of $\widetilde{t}^{\mu,\nu}(r):=r+(\iota_{1}\cdot\iota_{2})t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r)$ . Therefore $r^{\mu,\nu}:[\iota_{1}\iota_{2}t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0),\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ is a well-defined increasing function, and $(\partial_{s}r^{\mu,\nu})(s)=\frac{1}{1+\iota_{1}\iota_{2}(\partial t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}})(r^{\mu,\nu}(s))},\qquad s\in[\iota_{1}\iota_{2}t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0),\infty).$ (6.8) We can now finally define the functions $p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ and $\chi_{A}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:[0,\infty)\to[0,1]$ : (a) if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{A}\setminus\{\Phi^{e;b+,i-}\}$ then we define $I^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:=[t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0),\infty),\quad p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi):=r^{\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)\xi/|\xi|\,\text{ for }\,|\xi|\in I^{\sigma;\mu,\nu},\quad\chi_{A}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:=\mathbf{1}_{(t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0)+2^{-2D},\infty)};$ (6.9) (b) if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}=\Phi^{e;b+,i-}$ then we define $I^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:=[0,t^{bi}(0)],\quad p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi):=-r^{\mu,\nu}(-|\xi|)\xi/|\xi|\,\text{ for }\,|\xi|\in I^{\sigma;\mu,\nu},\quad\chi_{A}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:=\mathbf{1}_{(0,t^{bi}(0)-2^{-2D})}.$ (6.10) In both cases we also define $r^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|):=p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi)\cdot\xi/|\xi|.$ (6.11) The functions $p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ are not defined (and not needed) outside the range specified above. These functions are the key to an efficient analysis of Case A through the use of the following lemma. Lemma 6.2 . Assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{A}$ , see ( 6.1 ), and $-D/2\leq k,k_{1},k_{2}\leq D/2$ . (i) Assume that $\delta\in[0,2^{-100D}]$ and assume that $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}$ is a point such that $\begin{split}&|\xi|\in[2^{k-4},2^{k+4}],\qquad|\eta|\in[2^{k_{2}-4},2^{k_{2}+4}],\qquad|\xi-\eta|\in[2^{k_{1}-4},2^{k_{1}+4}],\\
&|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq\delta,\qquad|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq 2^{-100D}.\end{split}$ (6.12) Then $\chi_{A}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)=1,\quad\big|\eta-p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi)\big|\leq 2^{40D}\delta\quad\text{ and }\quad\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi))=0,$ (6.13) and $\begin{split}&\min\big(|(\partial_{s}r^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(|\xi|)|,|1-(\partial_{s}r^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(|\xi|)|\big)\geq 2^{-4D},\\
&|(D^{\rho}_{s}r^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(|\xi|)|\leq 2^{20D},\qquad\rho=0,1,\ldots 4.\end{split}$ (6.14) Moreover, if $\sigma_{2}=i$ then $\big||\eta|-r_{\ast}\big|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1.$ (6.15) (ii) Let $\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:I^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\to\mathbb{R}$ be defined by $\begin{split}\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(s)&:=\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(se,r^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(s)e)=\lambda_{\sigma}(s)-\iota_{1}\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(|r^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(s)-s|)-\iota_{2}\lambda_{\sigma_{2}}(|r^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(s)|),\end{split}$ (6.16) for some $e\in\mathbb{S}^{2}$ (the definition, of course, does not depend on the choice of $e$ ). Then there is some constant $\widetilde{c}=\widetilde{c}(\sigma,\mu,\nu)\in\{-1,1\}$ with the following property: $\begin{split}&\text{ the set }\widetilde{I}_{k}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:=\{s\in[2^{k-4},2^{k+4}]\cap I^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:\,|\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(s)|\leq 2^{-110D}\}\text{ is an interval};\\
&\widetilde{c}\cdot(\partial_{s}\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(s)\geq 2^{-20D}\text{ for any }s\in\widetilde{I}_{k}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}.\end{split}$ (6.17) Proof of Lemma 6.2 . Since $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{A}$ , $q^{\mu,\nu}$ is well defined. We start from the elementary formula $\begin{split}|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|&=|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)-\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta),\eta)|\\
&\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}|\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}^{\prime}(|\xi-\eta|)-\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}^{\prime}(|q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta)-\eta|)||\\
&+\max(\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}^{\prime}(|\xi-\eta|),\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}^{\prime}(|q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta)-\eta|))\Big|\frac{\xi-\eta}{|\xi-\eta|}-\frac{q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta)-\eta}{|q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta)-\eta|}\Big|.\end{split}$ Since $\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}^{\prime}(r)\geq 2^{-2D}$ and $\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}^{\prime\prime}(r)\geq 2^{-2D}(1+r)^{-3}$ if $r\geq 2^{-D/2-10}$ , the condition $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq\delta$ shows that $\big||\xi-\eta|-|q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta)-\eta|\big|+\Big|\frac{\xi-\eta}{|\xi-\eta|}-\frac{q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta)-\eta}{|q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta)-\eta|}\Big|\leq 2^{10D}\delta.$ This shows that $|\xi-q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta)|\leq 2^{20D}\delta\quad\text{ and }\quad|f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\eta|)|\leq 2^{30D}\delta.$ (6.18) where $f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}$ is defined by $f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(r):=\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(q^{\mu,\nu}(re),re)=\lambda_{\sigma}(|\widetilde{t}^{\mu,\nu}(r)|)-\iota_{1}\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))-\iota_{2}\lambda_{\sigma_{2}}(r).$ (6.19) We turn now to the proof of the lemma. We observe first that ( 6.14 ) follows from the formula ( 6.8 ) and the bounds ( 6.15 ), ( 6.13 ), and ( 6.7 ). We note also that the conclusion that $\widetilde{I}_{k}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ is a closed interval in the first line of ( 6.17 ) is a consequence of the existence of a constant $\widetilde{c}$ satisfying the inequality $\widetilde{c}(\partial_{s}\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(s)\geq 2^{-20D}$ for any $s\in\widetilde{I}_{k}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ in the second line of ( 6.17 ). We prove the claims in the lemma by analyzing several cases. Case 1. $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{b;b+,e+},\Phi^{b;e+,e+}\}$ . In this case we have $\begin{split}&t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0)=0,\quad\chi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{A}=\mathbf{1}_{(2^{-2D},\infty)},\quad\widetilde{t}^{\mu,\nu}(r)=r+t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r),\quad r^{\mu,\nu}(s)\in[0,s],\\
&f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(r)=\lambda_{b}(r+t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))-\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))-\lambda_{e}(r),\\
&\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(s)=\lambda_{b}(s)-\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(s-r^{\mu,\nu}(s))-\lambda_{e}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s)),\\
&(\partial_{s}\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(s)=\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(s)-\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s)).\end{split}$ (6.20) The claims ( 6.13 ) and ( 6.17 ) with $\widetilde{c}=1$ follow easily (using for example ( B.5 )), and the claim ( 6.15 ) is trivial. Case 2. $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{e;e+,i+},\Phi^{e;b+,i+},\Phi^{b;e+,i+},\Phi^{b;b+,i+}\}$ . In this case we have $\begin{split}&t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1,\quad\chi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{A}=\mathbf{1}_{(t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0)+2^{-2D},\infty)},\quad\widetilde{t}^{\mu,\nu}(r)=r+t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r),\quad r^{\mu,\nu}(s)\in[0,s],\\
&f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(r)=\lambda_{\sigma}(r+t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))-\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))-\lambda_{i}(r),\\
&\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(s)=\lambda_{\sigma}(s)-\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(s-r^{\mu,\nu}(s))-\lambda_{i}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s)),\\
&(\partial_{s}\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(s)=\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(s)-\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s)).\end{split}$ (6.21) Notice that $(\partial_{r}f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(r)=[1+(\partial t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}})(r)][\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r+t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))-\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma_{1}}(t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))].$ Therefore, using also ( 6.7 ) and Lemma ( A.4 ), $(\partial_{r}f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(r)\geq_{C_{b},\varepsilon}r(1+r^{2})^{-3/2}$ and $f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(0)\geq 0$ if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{e;e+,i+},\Phi^{b;e+,i+},\Phi^{b;b+,i+}\}$ . Therefore the inequality $|f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\eta|)|\leq 2^{-20D}$ in ( 6.18 ) cannot be verified in these cases for any $(\xi,\eta)$ as in ( 6.12 ), and the conclusions of the lemma are trivial. On the other hand, if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}=\Phi^{e;b+,i+}$ then the claims in ( 6.13 ) follow easily, using ( 6.18 ) and the hypothesis of the lemma. To prove the remaining claims we show first that $|\eta|\leq 3T^{-1/2}/4\leq 3r_{\ast}/4.$ (6.22) Indeed, starting from the inequalities $|f^{e;b+,i+}(|\eta|)|\leq 2^{-20D}$ and $t^{bi}(r)\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon/C_{b}}$ (see ( 6.6 )), and using also ( A.7 ), it follows that $2^{-20D}\geq\lambda_{e}(|\eta|)-\varepsilon^{-1/2}\sqrt{1+2\varepsilon}-\lambda_{i}(|\eta|)\geq\varepsilon^{-1/2}\big(\sqrt{1+T|\eta|^{2}}-\sqrt{1+2\varepsilon}\big)-\sqrt{(T+1)(\varepsilon+1)}|\eta|.$ The desired bound ( 6.22 ) follows. This clearly implies the bound ( 6.15 ). Finally, to prove ( 6.17 ), we calculate $\begin{split}&\Psi^{e;b+,i+}(t^{bi}(0))=\lambda_{e}(t^{bi}(0))-\lambda_{b}(t^{bi}(0))\leq-C^{-1}_{C_{b},\varepsilon},\\
&(\partial_{s}\Psi^{e;b+,i+})(t^{bi}(0))=\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(t^{bi}(0))-\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(0)=\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(t^{bi}(0))-\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(t^{bi}(0))\leq-C^{-1}_{C_{b},\varepsilon},\\
&(\partial^{2}_{s}\Psi^{e;b+,i+})(s)=\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{e}(s)-(\partial_{s}r^{b+,i+})(s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{i}(r^{b+,i+}(s)).\end{split}$ (6.23) Therefore $(\partial^{2}_{s}\Psi^{e;b+,i+})(s)\geq C^{-1}_{C_{b},\varepsilon}$ for all $s\in[t^{bi}(0),\infty)$ for which $r^{b+,i+}(s)\leq r_{\ast}$ . On the other hand, as in the proof of ( 6.22 ), if $s\in[2^{k-4},2^{k+4}]$ has the property that $|\Psi^{e;b+,i+}(s)|\leq 2^{-20D}$ then $r^{b+,i+}(s)\leq 4r_{\ast}/5$ . The desired conclusion ( 6.17 ) follows with $\widetilde{c}=1$ by combining the inequalities in ( 6.23 ). Case 3. $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{i;b-,e+},\Phi^{i;b+,e-},\Phi^{e;b+,e-},\Phi^{b;b+,e-}\}$ . In this case we have $\begin{split}&t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0)=0,\quad\chi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{A}=\mathbf{1}_{(2^{-2D},\infty)},\quad\widetilde{t}^{\mu,\nu}(r)=r-t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r),\quad r^{\mu,\nu}(s)\in[s,\infty),\\
&f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(r)=\lambda_{\sigma}(r-t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))-\iota_{1}\lambda_{b}(t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))-\iota_{2}\lambda_{e}(r),\\
&\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(s)=\lambda_{\sigma}(s)-\iota_{1}\lambda_{b}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s)-s)-\iota_{2}\lambda_{e}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s)),\\
&(\partial_{s}\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(s)=\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(s)-\iota_{2}\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s)).\end{split}$ (6.24) The claims in ( 6.13 ) follow easily, using the hypothesis and ( 6.18 ). The claim ( 6.15 ) is trivial. The conclusion ( 6.17 ) also follows from the formulas above if $\iota_{2}=-$ , with $\widetilde{c}=1$ . It remains to prove ( 6.17 ) when $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}=\Phi^{i;b-,e+}$ , in which case we set $\widetilde{c}=-1$ . For $s\geq r_{\ast}$ we estimate $(\partial_{s}\Psi^{i;b-,e+})(s)=\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(s)-\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s))\leq 1-\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r_{\ast})\leq-1,$ which gives the desired conclusion ( 6.17 ) when $s\geq r_{\ast}$ . On the other hand, we calculate $\begin{split}&\Psi^{i;b-,e+}(0)=\lambda_{i}(0)+\lambda_{b}(0)-\lambda_{e}(0)=0,\\
&(\partial_{s}\Psi^{i;b-,e+})(0)=\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(0)-\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(0)\geq C^{-1}_{C_{b},\varepsilon},\\
&(\partial^{2}_{s}\Psi^{i;b-,e+})(s)=\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{i}(s)-(\partial_{s}r^{b-,e+})(s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r^{b-,e+}(s)).\end{split}$ Therefore $(\partial^{2}_{s}\Psi^{i;b-,e+})(s)\leq-C^{-1}_{C_{b},\varepsilon}$ for $s\in[0,r_{\ast}]$ , and the desired conclusion ( 6.17 ) with $\widetilde{c}=-1$ follows in this range as well. Case 4. $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{i;e+,i-},\Phi^{i;b+,i-}\}$ . In this case we have $\begin{split}&t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1,\quad\chi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{A}=\mathbf{1}_{(t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0)+2^{-2D},\infty)},\quad\widetilde{t}^{\mu,\nu}(r)=r-t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r),\quad r^{\mu,\nu}(s)\in[s,\infty),\\
&f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(r)=\lambda_{i}(|r-t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r)|)-\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))+\lambda_{i}(r),\\
&\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(s)=\lambda_{i}(s)-\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s)-s)+\lambda_{i}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s)),\\
&(\partial_{s}\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(s)=\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(s)+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r^{\mu,\nu}(s)).\end{split}$ (6.25) Recalling that $t^{\sigma_{1}i}(r)\leq\sqrt{3\varepsilon/T}$ and $\lambda_{i}(r)\leq\sqrt{1+r^{2}}$ for any $r\in[0,\infty)$ , see ( 6.6 ) and ( A.4 ), we estimate $\lambda_{i}(|r-t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r)|)-\lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(r))+\lambda_{i}(r)\leq-\varepsilon^{-1/2}+2\sqrt{1+r^{2}}$ for any $r\in[0,\infty)$ . The inequality $|f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\eta|)|\leq 2^{-20D}$ , see ( 6.18 ), then shows that $|\eta|\geq(3\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$ . Therefore $|q^{\mu,\nu}(\eta)|=|\eta|-t^{\sigma_{1}i}(|\eta|)\geq|\eta|/2$ , and the conclusions in ( 6.13 ) follow using also ( 6.18 ). The claim ( 6.15 ) follows from $|\eta|\geq(3\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$ . Finally, the conclusion ( 6.17 ) with $\widetilde{c}=1$ follows from last formula in ( 6.25 ). Case 5. $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}=\Phi^{e;b+,i-}$ . In this case we have $\begin{split}&t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1,\quad\chi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{A}=\mathbf{1}_{(0,t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0)-2^{-2D})},\quad\widetilde{t}^{\mu,\nu}(r)=r-t^{bi}(r),\quad r^{\mu,\nu}(s)\in[s,\infty),\\
&f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(r)=\lambda_{e}(|r-t^{bi}(r)|)-\lambda_{b}(t^{bi}(r))+\lambda_{i}(r),\\
&\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(s)=\lambda_{e}(s)-\lambda_{b}(r^{\mu,\nu}(-s)+s)+\lambda_{i}(r^{\mu,\nu}(-s)),\\
&(\partial_{s}\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(s)=\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(s)-\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(r^{\mu,\nu}(-s)+s).\end{split}$ (6.26) Clearly, $-f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(0)\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ . Extending $\lambda_{e}$ as an even function on $\mathbb{R}$ we calculate, for $r\geq 0$ , $\begin{split}(\partial_{r}f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(r)&=(1-(\partial t^{bi})(r))\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r-t^{bi}(r))-(\partial t^{bi})(r)\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(t^{bi}(r))+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r)\\
&=[1-(\partial t^{bi})(r)][\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(t^{bi}(r))+\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r-t^{bi}(r))].\end{split}$ Let $r_{0}\in[0,\infty)$ denote the unique number with the property that $r_{0}=t^{bi}(r_{0})$ . In view of ( 6.6 ), $r_{0}\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon/C_{b}}\leq r_{\ast}/2$ . Moreover, $r-t^{bi}(r)\geq 0$ if $r\geq r_{0}$ and $r-t^{bi}(r)\leq 0$ if $r\leq r_{0}$ . Therefore $(\partial_{r}f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(r)\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ if $r\geq r_{0}$ and $(\partial_{r}f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(r)\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}r$ if $r\in[0,r_{0}]$ . Moreover, $\begin{split}f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(r_{0})&=\lambda_{e}(0)-\lambda_{b}(r_{0})+\lambda_{i}(r_{0})=\int_{0}^{r_{0}}[\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(\rho)-\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(\rho)]\,d\rho\\
&\geq r_{0}\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r_{0})-r_{0}\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(r_{0})+\int_{0}^{r_{0}}[\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(r_{0})-\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(\rho)]\,d\rho\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1.\end{split}$ Therefore the strictly increasing function $f^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ has a unique zero in the interval $(2^{-D/2},r_{0}-2^{-D/2})$ . It follows from ( 6.18 ) that if $\eta=re$ then $r\in(2^{-D},r_{0}-2^{-D})$ and $|\xi-(r-t^{bi}(r))e|\leq 2^{20D}\delta$ . The conclusions in ( 6.13 ) follow. The conclusion ( 6.15 ) follows using also $r_{0}\leq r_{\ast}/2$ . The inequality ( 6.17 ) follows using, for example, ( B.5 ).
∎ Remark: The analysis in Case 2 in the proof of Lemma 6.2 shows that the phases $\Phi^{e;e+,i+}$ , $\Phi^{b;e+,i+}$ , and $\Phi^{b;b+,i+}$ are, in fact, nonresonant, in the sense that there are no points $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}$ satisfying ( 6.12 ). Therefore in the proof of Proposition we may assume that $\begin{split}\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{A}:=\{&\Phi^{i;e+,i-},\Phi^{i;b+,i-},\Phi^{i;b-,e+},\Phi^{i;b+,e-},\Phi^{e;b+,i+},\Phi^{e;b+,i-},\\
&\Phi^{e;b+,e-},\Phi^{b;e+,e+},\Phi^{b;b+,e+},\Phi^{b;b+,e-}\}.\end{split}$ (6.27) 6.1. Proof of Proposition 6.1 Once the functions $p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ have been created, the rest of the analysis follows similar lines to the analysis of [ 29 , Section 4] . The main ingredients we need come from the refined $B_{k,j}$ norms and additional $L^{2}$ orthogonality arguments. We prove Proposition 6.1 in two steps, see Lemma 6.3 and Lemma 6.4 below, depending on the maximum in the definition of $\kappa$ . Lemma 6.3 . The bound ( 6.3 ) holds provided that ( 6.2 ) and ( 6.27 ) hold and, in addition, $\max(j_{1},j_{2})\leq(m-\beta^{2}m)/2,$ (6.28) with $\kappa:=2^{(\beta^{2}m-m)/2}.$ (6.29) Proof of Lemma 6.3 . For simplicity of notation, let $\begin{split}G(\xi):=&\mathcal{F}[P_{k}R_{m,\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})](\xi),\\
=&\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)q_{m}(s)\cdot\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,\end{split}$ (6.30) where $\chi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{A}$ was defined in Lemma 6.2 , and, as before, $\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)=\varphi(2^{D^{2}+\max(0,k_{1},k_{2})}\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))\varphi(|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|/\kappa).$ Using the $L^{\infty}$ bounds in ( 5.17 ) and ( 6.13 ) (with $\delta=4\kappa$ ), we see easily that $\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\kappa^{3}\cdot 2^{m}\lesssim 2^{-m/2}2^{3\beta^{2}m/2}.$ (6.31) This suffices to prove ( 6.3 ) if, for example, $j\leq m(1/2-4\beta)$ . To cover the entire range $j\leq m+D$ we integrate by parts in $s$ . In the argument below we may assume that $G\neq 0$ ; in particular this guarantees that the main assumption ( 6.12 ) is satisfied. With $\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)=\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi))$ , defined as in ( 6.16 ),
assume that $2^{m}|\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)|\in[2^{l},2^{l+1}],\,\,l\in[\beta m,\infty)\cap\mathbb{Z}.$ (6.32) Then, using Lemma 6.2 , we see that if $|\eta-p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi)|\leq 2^{50D}\kappa$ then $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)-\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)|\leq|\eta-p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi)|\cdot\sup_{|\zeta-p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi)|\leq 2^{50D}\kappa}|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\zeta)|\leq 2^{60D}\kappa|\eta-p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi)|,$ since $\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,p^{\mu,\nu}(\xi))=0$ . Therefore $2^{m}|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\in[2^{l-3},2^{l+4}]\qquad\text{ if }\qquad\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\neq 0.$ After integration by parts in $s$ it follows that $\begin{split}|G(\xi)|\lesssim 2^{m-l}|\varphi_{k}(\xi)|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}&|\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\,|q^{\prime}_{m}(s)|\,|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,s)|\,|\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\eta,s)|\\
&+|\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\,|q_{m}(s)|\,|(\partial_{s}\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}})(\xi-\eta,s)|\,|\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\eta,s)|\\
&+|\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\,|q_{m}(s)|\,|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,s)|\,|(\partial_{s}\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}})(\eta,s)|\,d\eta ds.\end{split}$ We use now ( 5.7 ), the last bound in ( 5.17 ), ( 5.22 ), and Lemma 6.2 . It follows that $|G(\xi)|\lesssim 2^{m-l}|\varphi_{k}(\xi)|\chi_{A}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)\cdot\kappa^{3}\lesssim|\varphi_{k}(\xi)|\chi_{A}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)\cdot 2^{-l}2^{-m/2}2^{\beta m/5}$ (6.33) provided that ( 6.32 ) holds. We can now prove the desired bound ( 6.3 ). To make use of ( 6.32 )–( 6.33 ) we need a good description of the
level sets of the functions $\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ . Let $\begin{split}&l_{0}:=\lfloor\beta m+2\rfloor,\quad D_{l_{0}}:=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:2^{m}|\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)|\leq 2^{l_{0}}\,\text{ and }|\varphi_{k}(\xi)|\chi_{A}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)\neq 0\},\\
&D_{l}:=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:2^{m}|\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)|\in(2^{l-1},2^{l}]\,\text{ and }|\varphi_{k}(\xi)|\chi_{A}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(|\xi|)\neq 0\},\quad l\in[l_{0}+1,m-100D]\cap\mathbb{Z},\\
&G=\sum_{l=l_{0}}^{m-100D}G_{l},\qquad G_{l}(\xi):=G(\xi)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D_{l}}(\xi).\end{split}$ For ( 6.3 ) it remains to prove that for any $l\in[l_{0},m-100D]\cap\mathbb{Z}$ $\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}(G_{l})\|_{B_{k,j}}\lesssim 2^{-3\beta^{4}m}.$ (6.34) Using ( 6.17 ) in Lemma 6.2 , it follows that there is $\theta^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}=\theta^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\mu,\nu,\sigma,k,k_{1},k_{2},l)\in[2^{-D},\infty)$ with the property that $D_{l}\subseteq\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:\big||\xi|-\theta^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\big|\lesssim 2^{l-m}\}.$ (6.35) Therefore, using also ( 6.33 ) if $l\geq l_{0}+1$ and ( 6.31 ) if $l=l_{0}$ , $\begin{split}\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}(G_{l})\|_{B^{1}_{k,j}}&\lesssim 2^{(1+\beta)j}\|G_{l}\|_{L^{2}}+\|G_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{\beta m}2^{-l}2^{-m/2}2^{\beta m/5}\cdot\big(2^{(1+\beta)j}2^{(l-m)/2}+1)\\
&\lesssim 2^{j-m}2^{-l/2}2^{11\beta m/5}+2^{-l}2^{-m/2}2^{6\beta m/5}.\end{split}$ This clearly suffices to prove ( 6.34 ) if $l\geq 6\beta m$ or $j\leq m-3\beta m$ . It remains to prove ( 6.34 ) in the remaining case $l\in[l_{0},6\beta m]\cap\mathbb{Z}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad j\in[m-3\beta m,m+D]\cap\mathbb{Z}.$ (6.36) For this we need to use the norms $B^{2}_{k,j}$ defined in ( 4.5 ). Assume first that $l\geq l_{0}+1$ . As before we estimate easily $\begin{split}2^{(1-\beta)j}\|G_{l}\|_{L^{2}}+\|G_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim 2^{-l}2^{-m/2}2^{\beta m/5}\cdot\big(2^{(1-\beta)m}2^{(l-m)/2}+1)\\
&\lesssim 2^{-l/2}2^{-4\beta m/5}+2^{-l}2^{-m/2}2^{\beta m/5}.\end{split}$ Therefore, for ( 6.34 ) it suffices to prove that $2^{\gamma j}\sup_{R\in[2^{-j},2^{k}],\,\xi_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}R^{-2}\big\|\mathcal{F}\big[\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}(G_{l})\big]\big\|_{L^{1}(B(\xi_{0},R))}\lesssim 2^{-3\beta^{4}m}.$ (6.37) Since $\big|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j})(\xi)\big|\lesssim 2^{3j}(1+2^{j}|\xi|)^{-6}$ , it follows from ( 6.33 ) that $\begin{split}\big|\mathcal{F}\big[\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}(G_{l})\big](\xi)\big|&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|G_{l}(\xi-\eta)|\cdot 2^{3j}(1+2^{j}|\eta|)^{-6}\,d\eta\\
&\lesssim 2^{-l}2^{-m/2}2^{\beta m/5}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{D_{l}}(\xi-\eta)\cdot 2^{3j}(1+2^{j}|\eta|)^{-6}\,d\eta.\end{split}$ Therefore, using now ( 6.35 ), for any $R\in[2^{-j},2^{k}]$ and $\xi_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $R^{-2}\big\|\mathcal{F}\big[\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}(G_{l})\big]\big\|_{L^{1}(B(\xi_{0},R))}\lesssim 2^{-l}2^{-m/2}2^{\beta m/5}\cdot 2^{l-m}\lesssim 2^{-3m/2}2^{\beta m/5},$ and the bound ( 6.37 ) follows. Similarly, using ( 6.31 ) and ( 6.35 ), $2^{(1-\beta)j}\|G_{l_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|G_{l_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{(1-\beta)(j-m)}2^{-\beta m+l_{0}/2+3\beta^{2}m}+2^{-m/4}\lesssim 2^{-3\beta^{4}m}$ and $\begin{split}\big|\mathcal{F}\big[\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}(G_{l_{0}})\big](\xi)\big|&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|G_{l_{0}}(\xi-\eta)|\cdot 2^{3j}(1+2^{j}|\eta|)^{-6}\,d\eta\\
&\lesssim 2^{-m/2}2^{3\beta^{2}m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{D_{l_{0}}}(\xi-\eta)\cdot 2^{3j}(1+2^{j}|\eta|)^{-6}\,d\eta\end{split}$ from where we conclude that, for any $R\in[2^{-j},2^{k}]$ and $\xi_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $R^{-2}\big\|\mathcal{F}\big[\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}(G_{l_{0}})\big]\big\|_{L^{1}(B(\xi_{0},R))}\lesssim 2^{-m/2}2^{3\beta^{2}m}\cdot 2^{l_{0}-m}\lesssim 2^{-3m/2}2^{2\beta m}.$ The desired bound ( 6.34 ) follows when $l=l_{0}$ , which completes the proof of the lemma.
∎ Lemma 6.4 . The bound ( 6.3 ) holds provided that ( 6.2 ) and ( 6.27 ) hold and, in addition, $\max(j_{1},j_{2})\geq(m-\beta^{2}m)/2,$ (6.38) with $\kappa:=2^{\beta^{2}m}2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})-m}.$ (6.39) Proof of Lemma 6.4 . Using definition ( 4.4 ), it suffices to prove that $2^{(1+\beta)j}\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}R_{m,\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})\big\|_{L^{2}}+\big\|\mathcal{F}[\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}R_{m,\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})]\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}m}.$ (6.40) Let $G=\mathcal{F}P_{k}R_{m,\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu},f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu})$ be given as in ( 6.30 ).
In proving ( 6.40 ) we may assume that $G\neq 0$ ; in particular this guarantees that the main assumptions ( 6.12 ) of Lemma 6.2 are satisfied.
We prove first the $L^{\infty}$ bound in ( 6.40 ). Assume that $j_{1}\leq j_{2}$ (the case $j_{1}\geq j_{2}$ is similar). Then, see ( 5.17 ) and ( 4.6 )–( 4.8 ), $\begin{split}&\|\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1,\\
&\sup_{\xi_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(s)\|_{L^{1}(B(\xi_{0},R))}\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j_{2}}R^{3/2},\qquad\text{ for any }R\leq 1.\end{split}$ Using ( 6.13 ) in Lemma 6.2 it follows that $\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{m}\cdot 2^{-(1+\beta)j_{2}}\kappa^{3/2}\lesssim 2^{-m/2}2^{2\beta^{2}m}2^{(1/2-\beta)j_{2}}\lesssim 2^{-2\beta^{4}m},$ as desired. To get the $L^{2}$ bound in ( 6.40 ) it suffices to show that $2^{(2+2\beta)m}\|G\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-4\beta^{4}m}.$ (6.41) To prove this we need first an orthogonality argument. Let $\chi:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]$ denote a smooth function supported in the interval $[-2,2]$ with the
property that $\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(x-n)=1\qquad\text{ for any }x\in\mathbb{R}.$ We define the smooth function $\chi^{\prime}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to[0,1]$ , $\chi^{\prime}(x,y,z):=\chi(x)\chi(y)\chi(z)$ . Recall the functions $\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ defined in ( 6.16 ). We define, for any $v\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}$ and $n\in\mathbb{Z}$ , $\begin{split}G_{v,n}(\xi):=&\chi^{\prime}(\kappa^{-1}\xi-v)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\\
&\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\chi(2^{-m}\kappa^{-1}s-n)q_{m}(s)\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta ds,\end{split}$ (6.42) and notice that $G=\sum_{v\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}G_{v,n}$ . In view of Lemma 6.2 (i) we notice also that the functions $\widetilde{G}_{v,s}$ are trivial unless $v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:=\{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\kappa|w|\in[2^{k-4},2^{k+4}]\cap[t^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(0)+2^{-4D},\infty),\,|\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\kappa|w|)|\leq 2^{-200D}\}.$ (6.43) We show now that $\|G\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\|G_{v,n}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2^{-10m}.$ (6.44) This additional orthogonality in time allows us a crucial gain of $\kappa^{1/2}$ in the time integration with respect to the trivial bound. To prove this bound we estimate $\|G\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}}\Big\|\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}G_{v,n}\Big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\sum_{v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}|\langle G_{v,n_{1}},G_{v,n_{2}}\rangle|.$ Therefore, for ( 6.44 ) it suffices to prove that $|\langle G_{v,n_{1}},G_{v,n_{2}}\rangle|\lesssim 2^{-20m}\qquad\text{ if }v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\text{ and }|n_{1}-n_{2}|\geq 2^{100D}.$ (6.45) Let $w_{n}:=n\kappa 2^{m}\cdot(\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})^{\prime}(\kappa|v|)\cdot v/|v|$ and integrate by parts in $\xi$ using Lemma A.3 with $K\approx|x+w_{n}|,\,\quad\,\epsilon^{-1}\approx 2^{-\max(j_{1},j_{2})}.$ It follows that, for any $n\in\mathbb{Z}$ , $|\mathcal{F}^{-1}(G_{v,n})(x)|\lesssim|x+w_{n}|^{-200}\quad\text{ if }\quad|x+w_{n}|\geq 2^{50D}\kappa 2^{m}.$ Moreover, using Lemma 6.2 and ( 6.43 ) we conclude that $|(\Psi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})^{\prime}({\kappa}|v|)|\geq 2^{-20D}$ . Therefore if $|n_{1}-n_{2}|\geq 2^{100D}$ then $|w_{n_{1}}-w_{n_{2}}|\geq 2^{70D}\kappa 2^{m}$ and the bound ( 6.45 ) follows. This completes the proof of ( 6.44 ). In view of ( 6.44 ), for ( 6.41 ) it remains to prove that $2^{(2+2\beta)m}\sum_{v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu},\,n\in[2^{-10}\kappa^{-1},2^{10}\kappa^{-1}]}\|G_{v,n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-4\beta^{4}m}.$ (6.46) Let $\widetilde{G}_{v,s}(\xi):=\chi^{\prime}(\kappa^{-1}\xi-v)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\eta,s)\,d\eta,$ (6.47) such that $G_{v,n}(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{G}_{v,s}(\xi)\chi(2^{-m}\kappa^{-1}s-n)q_{m}(s)\,ds.$ Therefore, for any $(v,n)$ , $\|G_{v,n}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim 2^{m}\kappa\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\widetilde{G}_{v,s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\chi(2^{-m}\kappa^{-1}s-n)q_{m}(s)\,ds.$ Therefore, for ( 6.46 ) it suffices to prove that for any $s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]$ $2^{(4+2\beta)m}\kappa\sum_{v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}}\|\widetilde{G}_{v,s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-4\beta^{4}m}.$ (6.48) Assuming $v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ fixed, the variables in the definition of the function $\widetilde{G}_{v,s}$ are naturally restricted as follows: $|\xi-\kappa v|\lesssim\kappa,\qquad|\eta-p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\kappa v)|\lesssim\kappa,$ where $p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ is defined as in Lemma 6.2 . More precisely, we define the functions $f_{1}^{v}$ and $f_{2}^{v}$ by the formulas $\begin{split}&\widehat{f_{1}^{v}}(\theta,s):=\varphi(2^{-50D}\kappa^{-1}(\theta-\kappa v+p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\kappa v)))\cdot\widehat{f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}}(\theta,s),\\
&\widehat{f_{2}^{v}}(\theta,s):=\varphi(2^{-50D}\kappa^{-1}(\theta-p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\kappa v))\cdot\widehat{f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}}(\theta,s).\end{split}$ (6.49) Since $|p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\kappa v_{1})-p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\kappa v_{2})|\geq 2^{-80D}\kappa$ and $\big|[\kappa v_{1}-p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\kappa v_{1})]-[\kappa v_{2}-p^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\kappa v_{2})]\big|\geq 2^{-80D}\kappa$ whenever $|v_{1}-v_{2}|\gtrsim 1$ (these inequalities are consequences of the lower bounds in the first line of ( 6.14 ) in Lemma 6.2 ), it follows by orthogonality that, for any $s\in\mathbb{R}$ , $\begin{split}&\sum_{v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}}\|f_{1}^{v}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|f_{k_{1},j_{1}}^{\mu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim 2^{-2j_{1}+2\beta j_{1}},\\
&\sum_{v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}}\|f_{2}^{v}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|f_{k_{2},j_{2}}^{\nu}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim 2^{-2j_{2}+2\beta j_{2}}.\end{split}$ (6.50) For any $v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ and $g_{1},g_{2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})$ let $A_{v}(g_{1},g_{2})(\xi):=\chi^{\prime}(\kappa^{-1}\xi-v)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\chi_{R}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\mathcal{F}(P_{[k_{1}-4,k_{1}+4]}g_{1})(\xi-\eta)\mathcal{F}(P_{[k_{2}-4,k_{2}+4]}g_{2})(\eta)\,d\eta.$ (6.51) We observe that $\begin{split}&\widetilde{G}_{v,s}(\xi)=e^{is\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)}A_{v}[Ef_{1}^{v}(s),Ef_{2}^{v}(s)](\xi),\\
&Ef_{1}^{v}(s)=e^{-is\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}}f_{1}^{v}(s),\qquad Ef_{2}^{v}(s)=e^{-is\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}}f_{2}^{v}(s).\end{split}$ Therefore for ( 6.48 ) it suffices to prove that, for any $s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]$ , $2^{(4+2\beta)m}\kappa\sum_{v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}}\|A_{v}(Ef_{1}^{v}(s),Ef_{2}^{v}(s))\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-4\beta^{4}m}.$ (6.52) We notice now that if $p,q\in[2,\infty]$ , $1/p+1/q=1/2$ , then $\|A_{v}(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|g_{1}\|_{L^{p}}\|g_{2}\|_{L^{q}}.$ (6.53) Indeed, as in the proof of Lemma A.3 , we write $\mathcal{F}^{-1}(A_{v}(g_{1},g_{2}))(x)=c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}g_{1}(y)g_{2}(z)K_{v}(x;y,z)\,dydz,$ where $\begin{split}K_{v}(x;y,z):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}&e^{i(x-y)\cdot\xi}e^{i(y-z)\cdot\eta}\chi^{\prime}(\kappa^{-1}\xi-v)\varphi(\kappa^{-1}\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))\\
&\times\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi(2^{D^{2}+\max(0,k_{1},k_{2})}\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))\varphi_{[k_{1}-4,k_{1}+4]}(\xi-\eta)\varphi_{[k_{2}-4,k_{2}+4]}(\eta)\,d\xi d\eta.\end{split}$ We recall that $k,k_{1},k_{2}\in[-D/2,D/2]$ and integrate by parts in $\xi$ and $\eta$ . Using also Lemma 6.2 , it follows that $|K_{v}(x;y,z)|\lesssim\kappa^{3}(1+\kappa^{-1}|x-y|)^{-4}\cdot\kappa^{3}(1+\kappa^{-1}|y-z|)^{-4},$ and the desired estimate ( 6.53 ) follows. We can now prove the main estimate ( 6.52 ). Assume first that $\max(j_{1},j_{2})\leq(3/5-\beta)m.$ (6.54) By symmetry, we may assume again that $j_{1}\leq j_{2}$ and estimate $\|Ef_{1}^{v}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\widehat{f_{1}^{v}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\kappa^{3}.$ Therefore, using ( 6.53 ) and ( 6.50 ), the left-hand side of ( 6.52 ) is dominated by $C2^{4m+2\beta m}\kappa\sum_{v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}}\kappa^{6}\|Ef_{2}^{v}(s)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{4m+2\beta m}\kappa^{7}\cdot 2^{-2j_{2}+2\beta j_{2}}\lesssim 2^{-3(1-\beta)m}2^{(5+2\beta)j_{2}},$ and the desired bound ( 6.52 ) follows provided that ( 6.54 ) holds. Assume now that $\max(j_{1},j_{2})\geq(3/5-\beta)m,\quad\max(j_{1},j_{2})-\min(j_{1},j_{2})\geq 8\beta m.$ (6.55) By symmetry, we may assume that $j_{1}\leq j_{2}$ and estimate, using ( 6.15 ), ( 5.17 ), and either ( A.37 ), ( A.42 ), ( A.49 ) or ( A.55 ), $\|Ef_{1}^{v}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-3m/2}2^{(1/2+\beta)j_{1}}.$ Therefore, using ( 6.53 ) and ( 6.50 ), the left-hand side of ( 6.52 ) is dominated by $\begin{split}C2^{4m+2\beta m}\kappa\sum_{v\in Z_{\kappa}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}}2^{-3m}2^{(1+2\beta)j_{1}}\|Ef_{2}^{v}(s)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{m+2\beta m}\kappa\cdot 2^{(1+2\beta)j_{1}}2^{-2j_{2}+2\beta j_{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{j_{1}-j_{2}}2^{3\beta m}2^{2\beta j_{1}}2^{2\beta j_{2}},\end{split}$ and the desired bound ( 6.52 ) follows provided that ( 6.55 ) holds. Finally, assume that $\max(j_{1},j_{2})-\min(j_{1},j_{2})\leq 8\beta m\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\geq(3/5-\beta)m.$ (6.56) In this case we need the more refined decomposition in ( 4.6 )–( 4.8 ). More precisely, using the definitions, for $s\in[2^{m-1},2^{m+1}]$ fixed we decompose $f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)=P_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}(g_{1}+h_{1}),\qquad f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)=P_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}(g_{2}+h_{2}),$ where $g_{1}=g_{1}\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{1})}_{[j_{1}-2,j_{1}+2]},\quad g_{2}=g_{2}\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{2})}_{[j_{2}-2,j_{2}+2]},$ (6.57) and $\begin{split}&2^{(1+\beta)j_{1}}\|g_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+2^{(1-\beta)j_{1}}\|h_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+2^{\gamma j_{1}}\sup_{R\in[2^{-j_{1}},2^{k_{1}}],\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}R^{-2}\|\widehat{h_{1}}\|_{L^{1}(B(\theta_{0},R))}\lesssim 1,\\
