&\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}+2^{k_{2}-k}|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}.\end{split}$ From ( 8.43 ) and the assumption $j\geq m+k_{2}+D$ , it follows that if $|x|\geq 2^{j-4}$ , $s\leq 2^{m+4}$ , then $|\nabla_{\xi}\left[x\cdot\xi+s\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\right]|\geq|x|-2^{m+k_{2}+D/2}\geq 2^{j-8}.$ We may then use Lemma A.2 with $f=2^{-j}\left[x\cdot\xi+s\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\right]$ , $K=2^{j}$ and $\epsilon=\min(2^{-\min(j_{1},j_{2}))},2^{2k})$ to conclude that $|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}(x)P_{k}Q^{0}_{\omega_{0}}R^{i;\mu,\nu}_{m,\kappa}(Q^{1}_{\omega_{1}}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},Q^{2}_{\omega_{2}}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})(x)|\lesssim 2^{-100m}$ from which ( 8.40 ) follows easily. Case 2: assume that $j\leq m+k_{2}+D.$ (8.44) In this case, we need a finer decomposition in order to obtain an efficient bilinear estimate in ( 8.49 ).
We define $\chi^{\mu,\nu}_{C}(\xi,\eta):=\varphi(2^{D^{2}}\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))\varphi(2^{D-k}|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi_{[k_{1}-2,k_{1}+2]}(\xi-\eta)\varphi_{[k_{2}-2,k_{2}+2]}(\eta)$ (8.45) and fix $\delta=2^{\max(k,k_{2})}$ in the definition ( 8.35 )-( 8.36 ). By ( 8.32 ), we see that $\delta\sim 2^{k_{1}}$ . An important property is that $\begin{split}\chi^{0}_{\omega}(\xi)\chi^{i+,i+}_{C}(\xi,\eta)=&\chi^{0}_{\omega}(\xi)\chi^{i+,i+}_{C}(\xi,\eta)\chi^{1}_{\omega}(\xi-\eta)\chi^{2}_{\omega}(\eta),\\
\chi^{0}_{\omega}(\xi)\chi^{i+,i-}_{C}(\xi,\eta)=&\chi^{0}_{\omega}(\xi)\chi^{i+,i-}_{C}(\xi,\eta)\chi^{1}_{-\omega}(\xi-\eta)\chi^{2}_{\omega}(\eta)+\chi^{0}_{\omega}(\xi)\chi^{i+,i-}_{C}(\xi,\eta)\chi^{1}_{\omega}(\xi-\eta)\chi^{2}_{-\omega}(\eta).\end{split}$ (8.46) Indeed, for $\chi_{C}^{i+,i+}$ , this follows from ( 8.41 ). For $\chi_{C}^{i+,i-}$ , we similarly see that $|\Xi^{i+,i-}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim\angle(\eta-\xi,\eta)$ and, using ( 8.34 ), $\begin{split}\sin(\angle(\xi,\eta))&=\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{|\xi|}\sin(\angle(\xi-\eta,\eta))\lesssim 2^{k_{1}},\quad\sin(\angle(\xi,\xi-\eta))=\frac{|\eta|}{|\xi|}\sin(\angle(\xi-\eta,\eta))\lesssim 2^{k_{1}}.\\
\end{split}$ Now, we perform an integration by parts in $s$ and obtain that $\begin{split}Q_{\omega}R^{i;i+,i-}_{m,\kappa}(f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})&=i\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[q_{m}^{\prime}(s)\Pi_{1,\omega}(s)+q_{m}(s)\Pi_{2,\omega}(s)+q_{m}(s)\Pi_{3,\omega}(s)\right]ds;\\
\widehat{\Pi_{1,\omega}}(\xi,s)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\chi_{\omega}(\xi)\chi_{C}^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}{\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\widehat{Q^{1}_{\omega}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{Q^{2}_{\omega}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta,s)d\eta ds,\\
\widehat{\Pi_{2,\omega}}(\xi,s)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\chi_{\omega}(\xi)\chi_{C}^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}{\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\widehat{Q^{1}_{\omega}(\partial_{s}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}})}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{Q^{2}_{\omega}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta,s)d\eta ds,\\
\widehat{\Pi_{3,\omega}}(\xi,s)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\chi_{\omega}(\xi)\chi_{C}^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}{\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\widehat{Q^{1}_{\omega}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{Q^{2}_{\omega}(\partial_{s}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})}(\eta,s)d\eta ds.\\
\end{split}$ Using ( A.3 ), ( A.49 ) and ( 8.49 ), we easily see that $\begin{split}\|\Pi_{1,\omega}(s)\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{-k-k_{1}-k_{2}}\min(\|EQ^{1}_{\omega}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|Q^{2}_{\omega}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}},\|Q^{1}_{\omega}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|EQ^{2}_{\omega}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}})\\
&\lesssim 2^{-k-k_{1}-k_{2}}2^{-3/2m}2^{3\min(j_{1},j_{2})/2}2^{k_{1}/2}\|Q^{1}_{\omega}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|Q^{2}_{\omega}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\end{split}$ and therefore, using orthogonality property ( 8.37 ), $\begin{split}&\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}^{\prime}(s)\cdot\sum_{\omega}\Pi_{1,\omega}(s)ds\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}^{\prime}(s)\|\sum_{\omega}\Pi_{1,\omega}(s)\|_{L^{2}}ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-k-k_{2}}2^{-k_{1}/2}2^{-3/2m}2^{3\min(j_{1},j_{2})/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}^{\prime}(s)\cdot\left(\sum_{\omega}\|Q^{1}_{\omega}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|Q^{2}_{\omega}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\right)ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-k-k_{2}}2^{-k_{1}/2}2^{-3/2m}2^{3\min(j_{1},j_{2})/2}\sup_{s\in[2^{m-4},2^{m+4}]}\|f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\end{split}$ and finally, with ( 5.18 ), ( 8.32 ) and ( 8.44 ), $\begin{split}&2^{(1+\alpha)k}2^{(1+\beta)j}\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}^{\prime}(s)\cdot\sum_{\omega}\Pi_{1,\omega}(s)ds\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{\alpha(k+k_{1}+k_{2})}2^{k+k_{2}}\cdot 2^{-k-k_{2}}2^{-k_{1}/2}2^{-(1/2-\beta)m}2^{3\min(j_{1},j_{2})/2}2^{-(1-\beta)(j_{1}+j_{2})}2^{2\beta(k_{1}+k_{2})}\\
&\lesssim 2^{\alpha(k+k_{1}+k_{2})}2^{-k_{1}/2}2^{-(1/2-\beta)m}2^{-(1/2-2\beta)\max(j_{1},j_{2})}2^{2\beta(k_{1}+k_{2})}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{4}m}.\end{split}$ (8.47) We now turn to $\Pi_{2,\omega}$ and $\Pi_{3,\omega}$ . Using ( A.23 ) and ( A.60 ), we see that for any $s\in[2^{m-4},2^{m+4}]$ and $j_{1}$ , $j_{2}$ satisfying ( 8.2 ), $\begin{split}\|Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(\partial_{s}f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}})\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{(1-5\beta)k_{1}}2^{-(1+\beta)m},\\
\|Ef^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(\partial_{s}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{(1-5\beta)k_{2}}2^{-(1+\beta)m}.\end{split}$ (8.48) Therefore, using ( 8.38 ), ( 8.46 ), ( 8.49 ) and Lemma A.3 , we see that $\begin{split}\|\sum_{\omega}\Pi_{2,\omega}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left(\sum_{\omega}\|\Pi_{2,\omega}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}&\lesssim 2^{-k-k_{1}-k_{2}}\left(\sum_{\omega}\|Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|Q^{2}_{\omega}(\partial_{s}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-k-k_{1}-k_{2}}\|Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|(\partial_{s}f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}})(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
\end{split}$ and therefore, using ( 8.48 ), $\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\|\sum_{\omega}\Pi_{2,\omega}(s)\|_{L^{2}}ds\lesssim 2^{-k}2^{-5\beta(k_{1}+k_{2})}2^{-(1+2\beta)m},$ so that, using ( 8.44 ), $2^{(1+\alpha)k}2^{(1+\beta)j}\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\sum_{\omega}\Pi_{2,\omega}(s)ds\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{\alpha k}2^{(1-10\beta)k_{2}}2^{-\beta m}$ which is sufficient. A similar bound holds for $\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\sum_{\omega}\Pi_{3,\omega}(s)ds\|_{L^{2}}$ . This gives ( 8.33 ) and finishes the proof.
∎ Lemma 8.8 . With $\chi_{C}^{\mu,\nu}$ defined as in ( 8.45 ), there holds that $\begin{split}\sup_{\omega}\|\mathcal{F}\left\{\frac{1}{\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\chi_{\omega}(\xi)\chi_{C}^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\right\}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}&\lesssim 2^{-(k+k_{1}+k_{2})}.\end{split}$ (8.49) Proof. We may assume that $\omega=e_{1}$ . We begin with the case $\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}=\Phi^{i;i+,i+}$ and we write $K(x,y):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-i(x\cdot\xi+y\cdot\eta)}\frac{1}{\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\chi_{\omega}(\xi)\chi_{C}^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)d\xi d\eta.$ The inequality ( 8.49 ) follows from ( 8.24 ) and the following bounds for $(\xi,\eta)$ in the support of $\chi^{0}_{e_{1}}\chi_{C}^{\mu,\nu}$ : $\begin{split}i)\quad|\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\cdot|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma}\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{\tau}\partial_{\eta_{j}}^{\upsilon}\frac{1}{\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}|&\lesssim 2^{-|\rho|k}2^{-|\sigma|(k+k_{1})}2^{-|\tau|k_{2}}2^{-|\upsilon|(k_{1}+k_{2})},\quad 2\leq i,j\leq 3,\\
ii)\quad|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma}\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{\tau}\partial_{\eta_{j}}^{\upsilon}\varphi(2^{-k}|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|)|&\lesssim 2^{-|\rho|k}2^{-|\sigma|(k+k_{1})}2^{-|\tau|k_{2}}2^{-|\upsilon|(k_{1}+k_{2})},\quad 2\leq i,j\leq 3,\\
\end{split}$ (8.50) Indeed, assuming ( 8.50 ), we deduce that, whenever $|x_{1}|\sim A$ , $|(x_{2},x_{3})|\sim B$ , $|y_{1}|\sim C$ and $|(y_{2},y_{3})|\sim D$ , there holds that $\begin{split}A^{p}B^{2q}C^{r}D^{2s}|K(x,y)|&\lesssim\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-i(x\cdot\xi+y\cdot\eta)}\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{p}\Delta_{x_{2},x_{3}}^{q}\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{r}\Delta_{y_{2},y_{3}}^{s}\left\{\frac{1}{\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)}\chi_{\omega}(\xi)\chi_{C}^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\right\}d\xi d\eta\right|\\
&\lesssim 2^{-(k+k_{1}+k_{2})}2^{k}2^{-pk}\cdot 2^{2(k+k_{1})}2^{-2q(k+k_{1})}\cdot 2^{k_{2}}2^{-rk_{2}}\cdot 2^{2(k_{1}+k_{2})}2^{-2s(k_{1}+k_{2})}\end{split}$ for $p,q,r,s\in\{0,2\}$ and therefore, $\begin{split}\|K\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}&\lesssim\sum_{A,B,C,D}\int_{|x_{1}|\sim A,\,|(x_{2},x_{3})|\sim B,\,|y_{1}|\sim C,\,|(y_{2},y_{3})|\sim D}|K(x,y)|dxdy\\
&\lesssim 2^{-(k+k_{1}+k_{2})}\sum_{A,B,C,D}\left[A2^{k}\right]^{1-p}\left[B2^{k+k_{1}}\right]^{2(1-q)}\left[C2^{k_{2}}\right]^{1-r}\left[D2^{k_{1}+k_{2}}\right]^{2(1-s)}\end{split}$ where the summation is over $A$ , $B$ , $C$ , $D$ dyadic numbers. Since the sum converges, this gives ( 8.49 ). It remains to prove ( 8.50 ). We start with $i)$ . Using ( 8.16 ), it suffices to obtain that $|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma}\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{\tau}\partial_{\eta_{j}}^{\upsilon}\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim 2^{k+k_{1}+k_{2}}2^{-|\rho|k}2^{-|\sigma|(k+k_{1})}2^{-|\tau|k_{2}}2^{-|\upsilon|(k_{1}+k_{2})},\quad 2\leq i,j\leq 3,\\
$ (8.51) Assume first that $\min(\rho+|\sigma|,\tau+|\upsilon|)\geq 1$ , then $|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma}\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{\tau}\partial_{\eta_{j}}^{\upsilon}\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|=|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma}\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{\tau}\partial_{\eta_{j}}^{\upsilon}\left[\Lambda_{i}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)-\iota_{2}\Lambda_{i}(\eta)\right]|=|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho+\tau}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma+\upsilon}\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)|.$ If $|\sigma|+|\upsilon|\geq 2$ we obtain the claim directly since $\partial^{\theta}_{\xi}\Lambda_{i}(|\xi-\eta|)\lesssim 2^{(1-|\theta|)k_{1}}$ . If $\rho+\tau=1=|\sigma|+|\upsilon|$ , then $|\partial_{\xi_{1}}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)|\leq|\lambda_{i}^{(2)}(|\xi-\eta|)\frac{\xi_{1}-\eta_{1}}{|\xi-\eta|}\frac{\xi_{i}-\eta_{i}}{|\xi-\eta|}|+\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(|\xi-\eta|)\frac{\xi_{1}-\eta_{1}}{|\xi-\eta|}\frac{\xi_{i}-\eta_{i}}{|\xi-\eta|^{2}}|\lesssim 1$ and the bound follows. If $\rho+\tau\geq 2$ , we compute that $\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{2}[\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)]=\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(|\xi-\eta|)\frac{(\xi_{1}-\eta_{1})^{2}}{|\xi-\eta|^{2}}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(|\xi-\eta|)}{|\xi-\eta|}\frac{|\xi_{\perp}-\eta_{\perp}|^{2}}{|\xi-\eta|^{2}},\quad\xi=\xi_{1}e_{2}+\xi_{\perp},\,\,\eta=\eta_{1}e_{1}+\eta_{\perp}.$ This can be bounded by $2^{k_{1}}$ and since any further derivative amounts to multiply by another factor of $2^{-k_{1}}$ , we obtain the claim in this case as well. We now claim that, on the support of $\chi^{0}_{e_{1}}\chi_{C}^{i+,i+}$ , $|\partial^{\rho}_{\xi_{1}}\partial^{\sigma}_{\xi_{i}}\Phi^{i;i+,i+}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim 2^{k+k_{1}+k_{2}}2^{-\rho k}2^{-|\sigma|(k+k_{1})}.$ (8.52) Note that, on the support of $\chi_{C}^{i+,i+}$ , there holds that $|\eta|\lesssim\min(|\xi|,|\xi-\eta|)$ .
Applying the simple formula $\begin{split}\partial_{x_{i}}^{\rho}f(g(x))=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\rho_{1}|,\dots,|\rho_{k}|\geq 1\\
\rho_{1}+\dots+\rho_{k}=\rho\end{subarray}}c_{\rho_{1},\dots,\rho_{k}}f^{(k)}(g)\cdot\partial_{x_{i}}^{\rho_{1}}g\dots\partial_{x_{i}}^{\rho_{k}}g,\end{split}$ for appropriate constants $c_{\rho_{1},\dots,\rho_{k}}$ , which follows by induction, with functions $f(r)=\lambda_{i}(r)$ and $g(\xi)=|\xi|$ , we see that ( 8.52 ) will follow from the following bounds: $\begin{split}|\lambda_{i}^{(\rho)}(|\xi|)-\lambda_{i}^{(\rho)}(|\xi-\eta|)|\lesssim|\eta|2^{2k_{1}}\cdot|\xi|^{-\rho}&\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}2^{(2-\rho)k_{1}},\quad\quad 1\leq\rho\leq 6\\
|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma}|\xi|-\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma}|\xi-\eta||\lesssim|\eta|2^{(2-|\sigma|)k_{1}}\cdot|\xi|^{-\rho-|\sigma|}&\lesssim 2^{k_{2}}2^{(2-\rho-2|\sigma|)k_{1}},\quad 0\leq\rho\leq 2,0\leq|\sigma|\leq 4,\,\rho+|\sigma|\geq 1\\
|\lambda_{i}^{(|\sigma|)}(|\xi|)|+|\lambda_{i}^{(|\sigma|)}(|\xi-\eta|)|+|\partial^{\sigma}_{\xi}|\xi||+|\partial_{\xi}^{\sigma}|\xi-\eta||\lesssim|\xi|^{1-|\sigma|}&\lesssim 2^{(1-|\sigma|)k_{1}},\quad\quad 1\leq|\sigma|\leq 6.\\
\end{split}$ The first line follows directly from the Taylor expansion of $\lambda_{i}$ . The second and third lines are straightforward unless $|\sigma|\leq 1$ . When $\sigma=0$ , $1\leq\rho\leq 2$ or $\sigma=1$ , $\rho=0$ , the bound $|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\left[|\xi|-|\xi-\eta|\right]|\lesssim|\eta|2^{2k_{1}}\cdot|\xi|^{-\rho},\quad|\partial_{\xi_{j}}\left[|\xi|-|\xi-\eta|\right]|\lesssim|\eta|2^{k_{1}}\cdot|\xi|^{-1}$ follows from the formulas 11 11 11 Here for a choice of $1\leq i\leq 3$ , we write $\xi=\xi_{i}+\xi_{\perp}$ , $e_{i}\cdot\xi_{\perp}=0$ . $\begin{split}\partial^{\rho}_{i}|\xi|=\begin{cases}|\xi|^{-3}\xi_{\perp}^{2}&,\rho=2\\
|\xi|^{-5}\xi_{\perp}^{2}\xi_{i}&,\rho=3.\end{cases}\end{split}$ The other cases follow from similar computations. We now claim that $|\partial^{\rho}_{\eta_{1}}\partial^{\sigma}_{\eta_{i}}\Phi^{i;i+,i+}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim 2^{k+k_{1}+k_{2}}2^{-\rho k_{2}}2^{-|\sigma|(k_{1}+k_{2})}.$ (8.53) Again, if $|\sigma|\geq 2$ , this follows from the bound $|\partial^{\theta}_{\eta}\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)|+|\partial^{\theta}_{\eta}\Lambda_{i}(\eta)|\lesssim|\eta|^{1-|\theta|}.$ If $\sigma=0$ , we may simply compute that $|\partial_{\eta_{1}}\Phi^{i;i+,i+}(\xi,\eta)|=|e_{1}\cdot\Xi^{i+,i+}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim|\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(|\xi-\eta|)-\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(|\eta|)|+[\angle(\xi-\eta,\eta)]^{2}\lesssim 2^{k_{1}+k}$ while the other bounds follow solely from the estimate $\begin{split}|\lambda_{i}^{(\rho)}(|\xi-\eta|)|+|\lambda_{i}^{(\rho)}(|\eta|)|+|\partial_{\eta_{1}}|\eta||+|\partial_{\eta_{1}}|\xi-\eta||&\lesssim 2^{2k_{1}}|\eta|^{(1-\rho)k_{2}},\quad\rho\geq 2.\end{split}$ If $\sigma=1$ , then $|\partial_{\xi_{j}}\Phi^{i;i+,i+}(\xi,\eta)|\leq|\Xi^{i+,i+}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim 2^{k}$ and if $\rho\geq 1$ , the claim follows from the bounds above and $|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{j}}|\eta||+|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{j}}|\xi-\eta||\lesssim 2^{k_{1}}|\eta|^{-\rho k_{2}},\quad\rho\geq 1.$ We now prove ( 8.50 ) $ii)$ . Since we can easily see that $\begin{split}&\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma}\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{\tau}\partial_{\eta_{j}}^{\upsilon}\varphi(2^{-k}|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|)\\
&=\sum_{|\theta_{1}|,\dots|\theta_{d}|\geq 1,\,\,\theta_{1}+\dots+\theta_{d}=(\rho,\sigma,\tau,\upsilon)}c_{\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{d}}2^{-dk}\varphi^{(d)}(2^{-k}|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|)\partial^{\theta_{1}}\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\dots\partial^{\theta_{d}}\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\end{split}$ for some appropriate coefficients $c_{\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{d}}$ . Therefore, we see that it suffices to show that $|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma}\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{\tau}\partial_{\eta_{j}}^{\upsilon}\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim 2^{k}2^{-|\rho|k}2^{-|\sigma|(k+k_{1})}2^{-|\tau|k_{2}}2^{-|\upsilon|(k_{1}+k_{2})}$ but this follows from ( 8.51 ). In case $\Phi^{i;\mu,\nu}=\Phi^{i;i+,i-}$ , we decompose $\begin{split}\chi^{0}_{\omega}(\xi)\chi^{i+,i-}_{C}(\xi,\eta)&=\chi^{0}_{\omega}(\xi)\chi^{i+,i-}_{C}(\xi,\eta)\chi^{1}_{-\omega}(\xi-\eta)\chi^{2}_{\omega}(\eta)+\chi^{0}_{\omega}(\xi)\chi^{i+,i-}_{C}(\xi,\eta)\chi^{1}_{\omega}(\xi-\eta)\chi^{2}_{-\omega}(\eta)\\
&=\chi^{i+,i-;1}_{C,\omega}(\xi,\eta)+\chi^{i+,i-;2}_{C,\omega}(\xi,\eta).\end{split}$ For $\chi^{i+,i-;2}_{C,\omega}(\xi,\eta)$ , changing variable $(\xi,\eta)\to(\widetilde{\xi},\widetilde{\eta})$ , where $\widetilde{\xi}=\xi-\eta$ and $\widetilde{\eta}=-\eta$ if $k_{2}\leq k$ and $\widetilde{\eta}=\xi$ if $k\leq k_{2}$ , the previous analysis for $\Phi^{i;i+,i+}$ applies. For $\chi^{i+,i-;1}_{C,\omega}(\xi,\eta)$ , using that, on its support, $|\xi-\eta|\simeq|\eta|\gtrsim|\xi|$ and $\begin{split}|\Phi^{i;i+,i-}(\xi,\eta)|&\gtrsim 2^{k},\\
|\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{\rho}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\sigma}\partial_{\eta_{1}}^{\tau}\partial_{\eta_{j}}^{\upsilon}\Xi^{i+,i-}(\xi,\eta)|&\lesssim 2^{k}2^{-|\rho|k}2^{-|\sigma|(k+k_{1})}2^{-|\tau|k_{2}}2^{-|\upsilon|(k_{1}+k_{2})}\end{split}$ we easily obtain ( 8.50 ). This ends the proof.
∎ Appendix A General estimates and the functions $\Lambda_{i},\Lambda_{e},\Lambda_{b}$ In this section we summarize the linear and the bilinear estimates we use in the paper. We also provide precise descriptions of the eigenvalues $\Lambda_{i},\Lambda_{e},\Lambda_{b}$ defined in ( 3.12 ). We note first that Calderon–Zygmund operators are compatible with the spaces constructed in Definition 4.1 . More precisely: Lemma A.1 . Assume $q\in\mathcal{S}^{10}$ , see ( 3.23 ), and $T_{q}f:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(q\cdot\widehat{f})$ . Then $\|T_{q}f\|_{Z}\lesssim\|q\|_{\mathcal{S}^{10}}\|f\|_{Z},\qquad\text{ for any }f\in Z.$ We omit the proof of this lemma, since it is identical to the proof of Lemma 5.1 in [ 29 ] . The following general oscillatory integral estimate is used repeatedly in the proofs. Lemma A.2 . Assume that $0<\epsilon\leq 1/\epsilon\leq K$ , $N\geq 1$ is an integer, and $f,g\in C^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{n})$ . Then $\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}e^{iKf}g\,dx\Big|\lesssim_{N}(K\epsilon)^{-N}\big[\sum_{|\rho|\leq N}\epsilon^{|\rho|}\|D^{\rho}_{x}g\|_{L^{1}}\big],$ (A.1) provided that $f$ is real-valued, $|\nabla_{x}f|\geq\mathbf{1}_{{\mathrm{supp}}\,g},\quad\text{ and }\quad\|D_{x}^{\rho}f\cdot\mathbf{1}_{{\mathrm{supp}}\,g}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{N}\epsilon^{1-|\rho|},\,2\leq|\rho|\leq N.$ (A.2) Proof of Lemma A.2 . We localize first to balls of size $\approx\epsilon$ . Using the assumptions in ( A.2 ) we may assume that inside each small ball, one of the directional derivatives of $f$ is bounded away from $0$ , say $|\partial_{1}f|\gtrsim_{N}1$ . Then we integrate by parts $N$ times in $x_{1}$ , and the desired bound ( A.1 ) follows.
∎ We will also use repeatedly the following simple bilinear estimate: Lemma A.3 . Assume $p,q\in[2,\infty]$ satisfy $1/p+1/q=1/2$ , and $m\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})$ . Then, for any $f,g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})$ , $\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\cdot\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{g}(\eta)\,d\eta\Big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|f\|_{L^{p}}\|g\|_{L^{q}}.$ (A.3) Proof of Lemma A.3 . Let $K(x,y):=(\mathcal{F}^{-1}m)(x,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{iy\cdot\eta}\,d\xi d\eta.$ Then, for any $h\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})$ we estimate $\begin{split}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta)\cdot\widehat{g}(\eta)\widehat{h}(\xi)\,d\xi d\eta\Big|&=C\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(x)g(y)h(z)\cdot K(-x-z,x-y)\,dxdydz\Big|\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f(x)g(x-v)h(-x-u)|\cdot|K(u,v)|\,dxdudv\\
&\lesssim\|K\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\cdot\|f\|_{L^{p}}\|g\|_{L^{q}}\|h\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}$ and the desired bound ( A.3 ) follows.
∎ Recall the functions $\Lambda_{i},\Lambda_{e},\Lambda_{b}$ defined in ( 3.12 ). Let $\lambda_{i},\lambda_{e},\lambda_{b}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ , $\begin{split}&\lambda_{i}(r):=\varepsilon^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)+(T+\varepsilon)r^{2}-\sqrt{\left((1-\varepsilon)+(T-\varepsilon)r^{2}\right)^{2}+4\varepsilon}}{2}},\\
&\lambda_{e}(r):=\varepsilon^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)+(T+\varepsilon)r^{2}+\sqrt{\left((1-\varepsilon)+(T-\varepsilon)r^{2}\right)^{2}+4\varepsilon}}{2}},\\
&\lambda_{b}(r):=\varepsilon^{-1/2}\sqrt{1+\varepsilon+C_{b}r^{2}},\end{split}$ (A.4) such that $\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)=\lambda_{\sigma}(|\xi|)$ , $\sigma\in\{i,e,b\}$ . We also define $c_{\sigma}=\lim_{r\to+\infty}\lambda_{\sigma}^{\prime}(r),\quad c_{i}=1,\,\,c_{e}=\sqrt{T/\varepsilon},\,\,c_{b}=\sqrt{C_{b}/\varepsilon}.$ Lemma A.4 . (i) The functions $\lambda_{i},\lambda_{e},\lambda_{b}$ are smooth on $[0,\infty)$ and satisfy $\begin{split}&\lambda_{i}(0)=0,\qquad\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{i}(0)=0,\qquad\lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i}(0)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}-1,\qquad\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1\,\,\text{ for any }\,\,r\in[0,\infty),\\
&\lambda_{e}(0)=\sqrt{\varepsilon^{-1}+1},\qquad\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(0)=0,\qquad\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}(1+r^{2})^{-3/2}\,\,\text{ for any }\,\,r\in[0,\infty),\\
&\lambda_{b}(0)=\sqrt{\varepsilon^{-1}+1},\qquad\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(0)=0,\qquad\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{b}(r)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}(1+r^{2})^{-3/2}\,\,\text{ for any }\,\,r\in[0,\infty).\end{split}$ (A.5) In addition, there is a constant $r_{\ast}\in(T^{-1/2},4T^{-1/2}+4T^{-1/4})$ such that, $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r_{\ast})=0,\qquad|\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{i}(r)|\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}\min(r,r^{-3})\,\,\text{ if }\,\,|r-r_{\ast}|\geq 2^{-D},\qquad|\lambda^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i}(r)|\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1\,\,\text{ if }\,\,|r-r_{\ast}|\leq 2^{-D/2}.$ (A.6) Moreover, $\begin{split}&r\leq\lambda_{i}(r)\leq\sqrt{(T+1)(\varepsilon+1)}r,\quad r\geq 0,\\
&\max(\lambda_{\sigma}(0),c_{\sigma}r)\leq\lambda_{\sigma}(r)\leq\lambda_{\sigma}(0)+c_{\sigma}r,\quad\sigma\in\{e,b\},\,\,r\geq 0.\end{split}$ (A.7) (ii) Letting $h_{\varepsilon}(r):=\varepsilon^{-1/2}\sqrt{1+Tr^{2}}$ , we have $|D^{\rho}_{r}(\lambda_{e}-h_{\varepsilon})(r)|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}|D_{r}^{\rho}h_{\varepsilon}(r)|,\quad 0\leq\rho\leq 2.$ (A.8) (iii) We have $\lambda_{i}(r)=rq_{i}(r)$ for some $1\leq q_{i}(r)\leq\sqrt{(1+T)/(1+\varepsilon)}$ , $q_{i}(r)\to 1$ as $r\to+\infty$ such that $q_{i}^{\prime}(r)\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{T^{2}r}{\left[1+T+Tr^{2}\right]^{2}}.$ (A.9) Moreover $|\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)|\leq 8\sqrt{2}T\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)\leq 10r^{-3}\text{ for }r\geq 4T^{-1/2}+4T^{-1/4}.$ (A.10) Proof of Lemma A.4 . (i) Recall the assumptions ( 1.9 ), which are used implicitly many times in this lemma. The claims in ( A.5 ) and ( A.7 ) are straightforward consequences of the definitions. To prove ( A.6 ), we use first the formula $\sqrt{\varepsilon}\lambda_{e}(r)\lambda_{i}(r)=r\left[1+T+Tr^{2}\right]^{1/2}$ to see that one can extend $\lambda_{i}(r)$ into a smooth odd function of $r$ . Starting from the relation $\lambda_{i}^{2}(r)=\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\left[1+\varepsilon+(T+\varepsilon)r^{2}-\sqrt{u^{2}+4\varepsilon}\right],\quad u=1-\varepsilon+(T-\varepsilon)r^{2},$ (A.11) and deriving up to three times, we find that $\begin{split}2\lambda_{i}(r)\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r)&=\frac{T+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}r-\frac{T-\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}ru(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-1/2},\\
2(\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r))^{2}+2\lambda_{i}(r)\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)&=\frac{T+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}-\frac{T-\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}u(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-1/2}-8(T-\varepsilon)^{2}r^{2}(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-3/2},\\
6\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r)\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)+2\lambda_{i}(r)\lambda_{i}^{(3)}(r)&=-\frac{24(T-\varepsilon)^{2}r}{\left[u^{2}+4\varepsilon\right]^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left[(1+\varepsilon)^{2}-(T-\varepsilon)^{2}r^{4}\right]:=A(r).\end{split}$ (A.12) In particular, $\lambda_{i}^{\prime}>0$ .
Since $\lambda_{i}$ is odd, its even derivatives vanish at $0$ . Dividing by $r$ and letting $r\to 0$ in the first and third lines gives $(\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(0))^{2}=(1+T)/(1+\varepsilon),\quad 8\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(0)\lambda_{i}^{(3)}(0)=-24(T-\varepsilon)^{2}(1+\varepsilon)^{-3}.$ Since $\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(0)>0$ , we see that $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}<0$ on some interval $(0,\delta)$ . Let $R_{A}=\sqrt{(1+\varepsilon)/(T-\varepsilon)}$ be the positive root of $A(r)$ . We claim that $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}<0$ on $(0,R_{A})$ . Indeed, we see from ( A.12 ) that, on this interval, so long as $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)\geq A(r)/(12\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r))$ , $\lambda_{i}^{(3)}<0$ and $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}$ is decreasing. Hence $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(R_{A})\leq 0$ . If $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(R_{A})=0$ , using ( A.12 ), we see that $\lambda_{i}^{(3)}(R_{A})=0$ and $R_{A}$ is a single root for $\lambda_{i}^{(3)}$ and a double root for $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}$ . Dividing by $r-R_{A}$ and letting $r\to R_{A}$ , we therefore find that $2\lambda_{i}(R_{A})\lambda_{i}^{(4)}(R_{A})=\lim_{r\to R_{A}}\frac{A(r)}{r-R_{A}}>0.$ But then $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)>0$ for some $r<R_{A}$ , a contradiction. It is clear that the argument above can be made quantitative, and prove that $\text{ for any }\delta>0\text{ there is }\delta^{\prime}=\delta^{\prime}(\delta,T,\varepsilon)>0\text{ such that }\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)\leq-\delta^{\prime}\text{ for any }r\in[\delta,R_{A}].$ This suffices to prove the desired claim ( A.6 ) for $r\in[0,R_{A}]$ . We now claim that $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}$ vanishes exactly once on $(R_{A},+\infty)$ . Indeed, using again ( A.12 ), we see that if $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r_{\ast})=0$ , then $\lambda_{i}^{(3)}(r_{\ast})=A(r_{\ast})/(2\lambda_{i}(r_{\ast}))>0.$ Let $r_{\ast\ast}$ be the next zero of $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}$ . Since $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}\geq 0$ on $(r_{\ast},r_{\ast\ast})$ , we have that $\lambda_{i}^{(3)}(r_{\ast\ast})\leq 0$ . Plugging $r=r_{\ast\ast}$ in the third line of ( A.12 ) gives a contradiction. Finally, we remark that there exists such $r_{\ast}$ since we will show below that $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}>0$ for $r$ large enough. Indeed, using the second equation in ( A.12 ), $\lambda_{i}(r)\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)=1-(\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r))^{2}+\frac{T-\varepsilon}{2\varepsilon}[1-u(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-1/2}]-4(T-\varepsilon)^{2}r^{2}(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-3/2}.$ (A.13) Therefore, using ( A.11 ) and ( A.12 ) and letting $v:=(1-u(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-1/2})/(2\varepsilon)$ , $\begin{split}(\lambda_{i}(r))^{3}\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)&=(\lambda_{i}(r))^{2}-(\lambda_{i}(r))^{2}(\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r))^{2}+(\lambda_{i}(r))^{2}(T-\varepsilon)v-4(\lambda_{i}(r))^{2}(T-\varepsilon)^{2}r^{2}(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-3/2}\\
&=r^{2}+1-v(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{1/2}-r^{2}(1+(T-\varepsilon)v)^{2}\\
&+(\lambda_{i}(r))^{2}(T-\varepsilon)v-4(\lambda_{i}(r))^{2}(T-\varepsilon)^{2}r^{2}(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-3/2}.\end{split}$ Notice that $v\leq u^{-2}\leq(T-\varepsilon)^{-2}r^{-4}$ , $(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{1/2}\leq(T-\varepsilon)r^{2}+1+\varepsilon$ , and $(\lambda_{i}(r))^{2}\in[r^{2},r^{2}+1]$ . Therefore, if $(T-\varepsilon)r^{2}\geq 10(1+\sqrt{T})$ then $(\lambda_{i}(r))^{3}\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)\in[1/10,1],$ (A.14) and the desired conclusion ( A.6 ) follows. (ii) We calculate $h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)=\varepsilon^{-1/2}Tr(1+Tr^{2})^{-1/2},\qquad h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)=\varepsilon^{-1/2}T(1+Tr^{2})^{-3/2}.$ Starting from the formula $\lambda_{e}^{2}(r)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left[1+Tr^{2}+\frac{\sqrt{u^{2}+4\varepsilon}-u}{2}\right],$ where, as before, $u=1-\varepsilon+(T-\varepsilon)r^{2}$ . Therefore $\lambda_{e}(r)\geq h_{\varepsilon}(r)$ and $\lambda_{e}(r)-h_{\varepsilon}(r)=\frac{\lambda^{2}_{e}(r)-h^{2}_{\varepsilon}(r)}{\lambda_{e}(r)+h_{\varepsilon}(r)}\leq\frac{\sqrt{u^{2}+4\varepsilon}-u}{4\varepsilon h_{\varepsilon}(r)}\leq\frac{1}{2uh_{\varepsilon}(r)}.$ (A.15) The desired bound ( A.8 ) follows for $\rho=0$ . Using the formulas above, we also calculate $2\lambda_{e}(r)\lambda_{e}^{\prime}(r)=\frac{2Tr}{\varepsilon}-\frac{(T-\varepsilon)r}{\varepsilon}\frac{\sqrt{u^{2}+4\varepsilon}-u}{\sqrt{u^{2}+4\varepsilon}}.$ Therefore $\lambda_{e}^{\prime}(r)\leq h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)$ and, using also ( A.15 ), $h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)-\lambda_{e}^{\prime}(r)=\frac{2h_{\varepsilon}(r)h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)-2\lambda_{e}(r)\lambda_{e}^{\prime}(r)+2h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)(\lambda_{e}(r)-h_{\varepsilon}(r))}{2\lambda_{e}(r)}\leq\frac{2Tr}{u^{2}\lambda_{e}}.$ (A.16) The desired bound ( A.8 ) follows for $\rho=1$ . Finally, we calculate $2\lambda_{e}(r)\lambda_{e}^{\prime\prime}(r)+2(\lambda_{e}^{\prime}(r))^{2}=\frac{2T}{\varepsilon}+E\qquad\text{ where }\qquad E:=-\frac{(T-\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}\frac{\sqrt{u^{2}+4\varepsilon}-u}{\sqrt{u^{2}+4\varepsilon}}+\frac{8(T-\varepsilon)^{2}r^{2}}{(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{3/2}}.$ Therefore, using also ( A.15 ) and ( A.16 ), $|\lambda_{e}^{\prime\prime}(r)-h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)|\leq\frac{|E|+2|\lambda_{e}(r)-h_{\varepsilon}(r)|h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)+2|(h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r))^{2}-(\lambda_{e}^{\prime}(r))^{2}|}{2\lambda_{e}}\leq\frac{20(T+1)}{u^{2}\lambda_{e}}.$ The desired bound ( A.8 ) follows for $\rho=2$ . (iii) Starting from the formula $\sqrt{\varepsilon}\lambda_{e}(r)\lambda_{i}(r)=r\sqrt{1+T+Tr^{2}},$ we calculate $q_{i}(r)=\frac{\sqrt{1+T+Tr^{2}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}\lambda_{e}}=\Big[1-\frac{T}{1+T+Tr^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{u^{2}+4\varepsilon}-u}{2(1+T+Tr^{2})}\Big]^{-1/2},$ (A.17) and, with $v=(1-u(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-1/2})/(2\varepsilon)$ as before, $q_{i}^{\prime}(r)=-\Big[1-\frac{T}{1+T+Tr^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{u^{2}+4\varepsilon}-u}{2(1+T+Tr^{2})}\Big]^{-3/2}\Big[\frac{T^{2}r-Tr\varepsilon v\sqrt{u^{2}+4\varepsilon}-(1+T+Tr^{2})(T-\varepsilon)r\varepsilon v}{(1+T+Tr^{2})^{2}}\Big].$ This suffices to prove ( A.9 ). The second bound in ( A.10 ) follows from ( A.14 ). To prove the first bound in ( A.10 ), we notice that it follows from part (i) that there are two values $r_{\min}\in(0,r_{\ast})$ and $r_{\max}\in(r_{\ast},\infty)$ such that $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)\in[\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r_{\min}),\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r_{\max})]\qquad\text{ for any }r\in[0,\infty).$ Using the identity in the second line of ( A.12 ) it follows that $\lambda_{i}(r)\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)\leq 1$ for any $r\geq\sqrt{(1+\varepsilon)/(T-\varepsilon)}$ . Since $r_{\max}\geq r_{\ast}\geq\sqrt{(1+\varepsilon)/(T-\varepsilon)}$ , it follows that $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r_{\max})\leq 1/\lambda_{i}(r_{\max})\leq 1/r_{\max}\leq\sqrt{T}.$ (A.18) To estimate $|\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r_{\min})|$ we use ( A.13 ) and the observation $|\lambda_{i}^{\prime}(r)|\leq q_{i}(r)\leq\sqrt{(1+T)/(1+\varepsilon)}$ to write $\begin{split}\lambda_{i}(r)\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r)&\geq 1-\frac{1+T}{1+\varepsilon}+\frac{T-\varepsilon}{2\varepsilon}[1-u(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-1/2}]-4(T-\varepsilon)^{2}r^{2}(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-3/2}\\
&\geq\frac{T-\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}\Big[-1+\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2\varepsilon}(1-u(u^{2}+4\varepsilon)^{-1/2})\Big]-4(T-\varepsilon)^{3/2}r\\
&\geq-8(T-\varepsilon)^{3/2}r.\end{split}$ Moreover, since $\lambda_{i}^{(3)}(r_{\min})=0$ and $\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r_{\min})\leq 0$ , it follows from the identity in the last line of ( A.12 ) that $r_{\min}\leq R_{A}=\sqrt{(1+\varepsilon)/(T-\varepsilon)}$ . Therefore, using also the fact that $q_{i}$ is decreasing on $[0,\infty)$ , see ( A.9 ), and the identity ( A.17 ), it follows that $-\lambda_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r_{\min})\leq\frac{8(T-\varepsilon)^{3/2}r_{\min}}{\lambda_{i}(r_{\min})}\leq\frac{8(T-\varepsilon)^{3/2}}{q_{i}(R_{A})}\leq 8\sqrt{2}(T-\varepsilon).$ The desired estimate in ( A.10 ) follows using also ( A.18 ).
∎ Lemma A.5 . Assume $\|f\|_{Z}\leq 1$ , $t\in\mathbb{R}$ , $(k,j)\in\mathcal{J}$ , and let $\widetilde{k}=\min(k,0)$ and $f_{k,j}:=P_{[k-2,k+2]}[\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}f].$ (i) Then $\|f_{k,j}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})^{-1}\cdot 2^{2\beta\widetilde{k}}2^{-(1-{\beta})j}$ (A.19) and $\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big|D^{\rho}_{\xi}\widehat{f_{k,j}}(\xi)\big|\lesssim_{|\rho|}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})^{-1}\cdot 2^{-(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}}2^{|\rho|j}.$ (A.20) Moreover, if $k\leq 0$ and $\sigma\in\{e,b\}$ then $\big\|e^{it\Lambda_{\sigma}}f_{k,j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{(3/2-\alpha)k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}(1+2^{-j}|t|2^{k})^{-3/2+10\beta}.$ (A.21) If $k\geq 0$ and $\sigma\in\{e,b\}$ then $\big\|e^{it\Lambda_{\sigma}}f_{k,j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-6k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}(1+2^{-j}|t|)^{-3/2+10\beta}.$ (A.22) With $r_{\ast}$ defined as in ( A.6 ), let $k_{\ast}:=\log_{2}r_{\ast}$ . If $k\leq k_{\ast}-3$ and $\sigma=i$ then $\big\|e^{it\Lambda_{\sigma}}f_{k,j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{(3/2-\alpha)k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}(1+2^{-j}|t|2^{2k/3})^{-3/2+10\beta}.$ (A.23) If $k\in[k_{\ast}-3,k_{\ast}+3]$ and $\sigma=i$ then $\big\|e^{it\Lambda_{\sigma}}f_{k,j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j}(1+2^{-j}|t|)^{-5/4+10\beta}.$ (A.24) If $k\geq k_{\ast}+3$ and $\sigma=i$ then $\big\|e^{it\Lambda_{\sigma}}f_{k,j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-6k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}(1+2^{-j}|t|)^{-3/2+10\beta}.$ (A.25) (ii) As a consequence $\sum_{j\geq\max(-k,0)}\|f_{k,j}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\min(2^{(1+\beta-\alpha)k},2^{-10k})$ (A.26) and 12 12 12 In many places we will be able to use the simpler bound ( A.27 ), instead of the more precise bounds ( A.21 )–( A.25 ). , for any $\sigma\in\{i,e,b\}$ , $\sum_{j\geq\max(-k,0)}\big\|e^{it\Lambda_{\sigma}}f_{k,j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\min(2^{(1/2-\beta-\alpha)k},2^{-6k})(1+|t|)^{-1-\beta}.$ (A.27) Proof of Lemma A.5 . We start by decomposing, as in ( 4.6 )–( 4.8 ), $\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}f=(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})^{-1}(g_{1,j}+g_{2,j}),\qquad g_{1,j}=g_{1,j}\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{[j-2,j+2]},\qquad g_{2,j}=g_{2,j}\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{[j-2,j+2]},$ (A.28) such that $2^{(1+{\beta})j}\|g_{1,j}\|_{L^{2}}+2^{(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}}\|\widehat{g_{1,j}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1,$ (A.29) and $2^{(1-{\beta})j}\|g_{2,j}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\widehat{g_{2,j}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+2^{\gamma j}\sup_{R\in[2^{-j},2^{k}],\,\xi_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}R^{-2}\|\widehat{g_{2,j}}\|_{L^{1}(B(\xi_{0},R))}\lesssim 2^{-10|k|}.$ (A.30) The bound ( A.19 ) follows easily. To prove ( A.20 ) we use the formulas in ( A.28 ) to write, for $\mu=1,2$ , $\widehat{g_{\mu,j}}(\xi)=c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widehat{g_{\mu,j}}(\eta)\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{[j-2,j+2]})(\xi-\eta)\,d\eta.$ Therefore $D^{\rho}_{\xi}\widehat{g_{\mu,j}}(\xi)=c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widehat{g_{\mu,j}}(\eta)\mathcal{F}(x^{\rho}\cdot\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{[j-2,j+2]})(\xi-\eta)\,d\eta.$ (A.31) The desired bounds ( A.20 ) follow using the bounds $\|\widehat{g_{\mu,j}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}}$ , see ( A.29 )-( A.30 ). We consider now the $L^{\infty}$ bounds ( A.21 )-( A.25 ). Using ( A.28 )-( A.30 ), we have $\|\widehat{f_{k,j}}\|_{L^{1}(B(\xi_{0},R))}\lesssim(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})^{-1}\min(R^{3}2^{-(1/2-\beta)\widetilde{k}},R^{3/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}),$ for any $\xi_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ and $R\leq 2^{k}$ . Therefore, for any $k\in\mathbb{Z}$ and $\sigma\in\{i,e,b\}$ , $\big\|e^{it\Lambda_{\sigma}}f_{k,j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})^{-1}\cdot 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}.$ (A.32) Step 1. We consider first the simplest case $\sigma\in\{e,b\},\qquad k\leq 0,\qquad|t|\geq 2^{j-k+D},$ (A.33) and prove that, for any $x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{it\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)}\widehat{f_{k,j}}(\xi)\,d\xi\Big|\lesssim 2^{(3/2-\alpha)k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{1}^{3/2-10\beta},\qquad\rho_{1}:=2^{j-k}|t|^{-1}.$ (A.34) The bound ( A.21 ) would clearly follow from ( A.32 ) and ( A.34 ).
Using the decomposition ( A.28 ), it suffices to prove that, for $\mu\in\{1,2\}$ , $\begin{split}&\big\|g_{\mu,j}\ast K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{1}^{3/2-10\beta},\\
&K^{\sigma}_{k,t}(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{it\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)}\varphi_{[k-2,k+2]}(\xi)\,d\xi.\end{split}$ (A.35) Recall that the kernel of the operator on $\mathbb{R}^{3}$ defined by the radial multiplier $\xi\to p(|\xi|)$ is $K(x)=c\int_{0}^{\infty}p(s)s^{2}\frac{e^{is|x|}-e^{-is|x|}}{s|x|}\,ds.$ (A.36) We show that $\|K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim|t|^{-3/2}.$ (A.37) In view of ( A.36 ) it suffices to prove that $\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\varphi^{1}_{[k-2,k+2]}(s)e^{it\lambda_{\sigma}(s)}\frac{e^{isr}-e^{-isr}}{sr}\,ds\Big|\lesssim|t|^{-3/2},$ (A.38) for any $r\in(0,\infty)$ . Recall the assumption ( A.33 ), it particular $|t|\geq 2^{D-2k}$ . Since $\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(s)\approx\min(s,1)$ (see ( A.5 )), the bound ( A.38 ) follows by integration by parts unless $r\approx|t|2^{k}$ . On the other hand, if $r\approx|t|2^{k}$ then the bound ( A.38 ) follows by stationary phase, using $\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma}(s)\approx(1+s^{2})^{-3/2}$ , see ( A.5 ). In view of ( A.37 ) and the assumptions ( A.28 )–( A.30 ), it follows that $\big\|g_{1,j}\ast K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|g_{1,j}\|_{L^{1}}\|K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3j/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}|t|^{-3/2}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{1}^{3/2},$ and $\big\|g_{2,j}\ast K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|g_{2,j}\|_{L^{1}}\|K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3j/2}2^{-(1-\beta)j}2^{2\beta k}|t|^{-3/2}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{1}^{3/2}2^{2\beta(j+k)}.$ The bounds ( A.35 ) follow if $\mu=1$ or if $\mu=2$ and $2^{j+k}\leq\rho_{1}^{-5}$ . On the other hand, if $\mu=2$ and $2^{j+k}\geq\rho_{1}^{-5}$ then, using the $L^{1}$ bounds on $\widehat{g_{2,j}}$ in ( A.30 ), $\big\|g_{2,j}\ast K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\big\|\widehat{g_{2,j}}\big\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}2^{-(\gamma-\beta-1)(j+k)}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{1}^{5(\gamma-\beta-1)},$ which suffices to prove ( A.35 ) in this case as well. Step 2. We consider now the case $\sigma\in\{e,b\},\qquad k\geq 0,\qquad|t|\geq 2^{j+k+D},$ (A.39) and prove that, for any $x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{it\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)}\widehat{f_{k,j}}(\xi)\,d\xi\Big|\lesssim 2^{-6k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{2}^{3/2-10\beta},\qquad\rho_{2}:=2^{j}|t|^{-1}.$ (A.40) The bound ( A.22 ) would clearly follow from ( A.32 ) and ( A.40 ).
Using the decomposition ( A.28 ), it suffices to prove that, for $\mu\in\{1,2\}$ , $\big\|g_{\mu,j}\ast K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{4k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{2}^{3/2-10\beta},$ (A.41) where $K^{\sigma}_{k,t}$ is defined as in ( A.35 ). As before, we show that $\|K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim|t|^{-3/2}2^{3k}.$ (A.42) In view of ( A.36 ) it suffices to prove that $\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\varphi^{1}_{[k-2,k+2]}(s)e^{it\lambda_{\sigma}(s)}\frac{e^{isr}-e^{-isr}}{sr}\,ds\Big|\lesssim|t|^{-3/2}2^{3k},$ (A.43) for any $r\in(0,\infty)$ . Recall the assumption ( A.39 ), in particular $|t|\geq 2^{D+k}$ . Since $\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(s)\approx\min(s,1)$ (see ( A.5 )), the bound ( A.43 ) follows by integration by parts unless $r\approx|t|$ . On the other hand, if $r\approx|t|$ then the bound ( A.43 ) follows by stationary phase, using $\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma}(s)\approx(1+s^{2})^{-3/2}$ , see ( A.5 ). In view of ( A.42 ) and the assumptions ( A.28 )–( A.30 ), it follows that $\big\|g_{1,j}\ast K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|g_{1,j}\|_{L^{1}}\|K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3j/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}2^{3k}|t|^{-3/2}\lesssim 2^{3k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{2}^{3/2},$ and $\big\|g_{2,j}\ast K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|g_{2,j}\|_{L^{1}}\|K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3j/2}2^{-(1-\beta)j}2^{3k}|t|^{-3/2}\lesssim 2^{3k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{2}^{3/2}2^{2\beta j}.$ The bounds ( A.41 ) follow if $\mu=1$ or if $\mu=2$ and $2^{j}\leq\rho_{2}^{-5}$ . On the other hand, if $\mu=2$ and $2^{j}\geq\rho_{2}^{-5}$ then, using the $L^{1}$ bounds on $\widehat{g_{2,j}}$ in ( A.30 ), $\big\|g_{2,j}\ast K^{\sigma}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\big\|\widehat{g_{2,j}}\big\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j}2^{-(\gamma-\beta-1)j}\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{2}^{5(\gamma-\beta-1)},$ which suffices to prove ( A.41 ) in this case as well. An identical argument shows that $\text{ if }\quad k\geq k_{\ast}+3,\qquad|t|\geq 2^{j+k+D},$ (A.44) then, for any $x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{it\Lambda_{i}(\xi)}\widehat{f_{k,j}}(\xi)\,d\xi\Big|\lesssim 2^{-6k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}(2^{j}/|t|)^{3/2-10\beta}.$ (A.45) The bound ( A.25 ) clearly follows from ( A.32 ) and ( A.45 ). Step 3. We consider now the case $k\leq k_{\ast}-3,\qquad|t|\geq 2^{j-2k/3+D},$ (A.46) and prove that, for any $x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{it\Lambda_{i}(\xi)}\widehat{f_{k,j}}(\xi)\,d\xi\Big|\lesssim 2^{(3/2-\alpha)k}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{3}^{3/2-10\beta},\qquad\rho_{3}:=2^{j-2k/3}|t|^{-1}.$ (A.47) The bound ( A.23 ) would clearly follow from ( A.32 ) and ( A.47 ).
Using the decomposition ( A.28 ), it suffices to prove that, for $\mu\in\{1,2\}$ , $\begin{split}&\big\|g_{\mu,j}\ast K^{i}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{3}^{3/2-10\beta},\\
&K^{i}_{k,t}(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{it\Lambda_{i}(\xi)}\varphi_{[k-2,k+2]}(\xi)\,d\xi.\end{split}$ (A.48) As before, we show that $\|K^{i}_{k,t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{k/2}|t|^{-3/2}.$ (A.49) In view of ( A.36 ) it suffices to prove that $\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\varphi^{1}_{[k-2,k+2]}(s)e^{it\lambda_{i}(s)}\frac{e^{isr}-e^{-isr}}{sr}\,ds\Big|\lesssim 2^{k/2}|t|^{-3/2},$ (A.50) for any $r\in(0,\infty)$ . Recall the assumption ( A.33 ), it particular $|t|\geq 2^{D-5k/3}$ . Since $\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(s)\approx 1$ (see ( A.5 )), the bound ( A.50 ) follows by integration by parts unless $r\approx|t|$ . On the other hand, if $r\approx|t|$ then the bound ( A.50 ) follows by stationary phase, using $\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma}(s)\approx s$ , see ( A.6 ). As before, we can now prove ( A.48 ). Using ( A.37 ) and ( A.28 )–( A.30 ), it follows that $\big\|g_{1,j}\ast K^{i}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|g_{1,j}\|_{L^{1}}\|K^{i}_{k,t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3j/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}2^{k/2}|t|^{-3/2}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{3}^{3/2},$ and $\big\|g_{2,j}\ast K^{i}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|g_{2,j}\|_{L^{1}}\|K^{i}_{k,t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3j/2}2^{-(1-\beta)j}2^{2\beta k}2^{k/2}|t|^{-3/2}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{3}^{3/2}2^{2\beta(j+k)}.$ The bounds ( A.48 ) follow if $\mu=1$ or if $\mu=2$ and $2^{j+k}\leq\rho_{3}^{-5}$ . On the other hand, if $\mu=2$ and $2^{j+k}\geq\rho_{3}^{-5}$ then, using the $L^{1}$ bounds on $\widehat{g_{2,j}}$ in ( A.30 ), $\big\|g_{2,j}\ast K^{i}_{k,t}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\big\|\widehat{g_{2,j}}\big\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}2^{-(\gamma-\beta-1)(j+k)}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{3}^{5(\gamma-\beta-1)},$ which suffices to prove ( A.48 ) in this case as well. Step 4. Finally, we consider the case $k\in[k_{\ast}-3,k_{\ast}+3],\qquad|t|\geq 2^{j+4D},$ (A.51) and prove that, for any $x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{it\Lambda_{i}(\xi)}\widehat{f_{k,j}}(\xi)\,d\xi\Big|\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{2}^{5/4-10\beta},\qquad\rho_{2}=2^{j}|t|^{-1}.$ (A.52) The bound ( A.24 ) would clearly follow from ( A.32 ) and ( A.52 ). Using the decomposition ( A.28 )–( A.30 ), it suffices to prove that, for $\mu\in\{1,2\}$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{it\Lambda_{i}(\xi)}\varphi_{[k-2,k+2]}(\xi)\big[1-\varphi[(|\xi|-r_{\ast})/\rho_{2}^{1/2}]\big]\widehat{g_{\mu,j}}(\xi)\,d\xi\Big|\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{2}^{5/4-10\beta},$ (A.53) and $\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{it\Lambda_{i}(\xi)}\varphi[(|\xi|-r_{\ast})/\rho_{2}^{1/2}]\widehat{g_{\mu,j}}(\xi)\,d\xi\Big|\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{2}^{5/4-10\beta}.$ (A.54) Letting $K_{k,t;\delta}(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{it\Lambda_{i}(\xi)}\varphi_{[k-2,k+2]}(\xi)\big[1-\varphi[(|\xi|-r_{\ast})/\delta]\big]\,d\xi$ and arguing as in the proof of ( A.49 ), it is easy to see that $\|K_{k,t;\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim|t|^{-3/2}\delta^{-1/2}$ (A.55) provided that $\delta\in[|t|^{-1/2},2^{-D}]$ . As before, this suffices to prove the bounds ( A.53 ). To prove ( A.54 ), we may assume, without loss of generality, that $x/t=(-z_{1},0,0)$ for some $z_{1}\in[0,\infty)$ . The formula ( A.31 ), together with the bounds in ( A.29 ) and ( A.30 ) show that $\begin{split}&2^{(1+\beta)j}\|D^{\rho}_{\xi}\widehat{g_{1,j}}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}+\|D^{\rho}_{\xi}\widehat{g_{1,j}}(\xi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{|\rho|j},\\
&2^{(1-\beta)j}\|D^{\rho}_{\xi}\widehat{g_{2,j}}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}+\|D^{\rho}_{\xi}\widehat{g_{2,j}}(\xi)\|_{L^{\infty}}+2^{\gamma j}\sup_{R\in[2^{-j},1],\,\xi_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}R^{-2}\|D^{\rho}_{\xi}\widehat{g_{2,j}}(\xi)\|_{L^{1}(B(\xi_{0},R))}\lesssim 2^{|\rho|j}.\end{split}$ (A.56) With $\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})=(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})$ and $l\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-\infty,D/10]$ we define $I^{\mu}_{\leq l}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi(|\xi^{\prime}|/2^{l})e^{it(\Lambda_{i}(\xi)-z_{1}\xi_{1})}\varphi[(|\xi|-r_{\ast})/\rho_{2}^{1/2}]\widehat{g_{\mu,j}}(\xi)\,d\xi,\qquad I^{\mu}_{l}:=I^{\mu}_{\leq l}-I^{\mu}_{\leq l-1}.$ We fix $l_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}$ such that $2^{l_{0}}\leq\rho_{2}+|t|^{-1/2}\leq 2^{l_{0}+1}.$ We use ( A.56 ) with $|\rho|=0$ to estimate, if $2^{j}\geq|t|^{1/2}$ , $\begin{split}&|I^{1}_{\leq l_{0}}|\lesssim 2^{l_{0}}\rho_{2}^{1/4}\|\widehat{g_{1,j}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j}2^{l_{0}}\rho_{2}^{1/4},\\
&|I^{2}_{\leq l_{0}}|\lesssim\frac{\rho_{2}^{1/2}}{2^{l_{0}}}\cdot 2^{2l_{0}}2^{-\gamma_{j}}\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j}2^{l_{0}}\rho_{2}^{1/2}.\end{split}$ On the other hand, if $2^{j}\leq|t|^{1/2}$ then $|I^{1}_{\leq l_{0}}|+|I^{2}_{\leq l_{0}}|\lesssim 2^{2l_{0}}\rho_{2}^{1/2}(\|\widehat{g_{1,j}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\widehat{g_{2,j}}\|_{L^{\infty}})\lesssim 2^{2l_{0}}\rho_{2}^{1/2}.$ Therefore, in both cases, $|I^{1}_{\leq l_{0}}|+|I^{2}_{\leq l_{0}}|\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{2}^{5/4}.$ (A.57) To estimate $|I_{l}^{\mu}|$ for $l\geq l_{0}+1$ we integrate by parts in $\xi^{\prime}$ , using Lemma A.2 with $K\approx|t|2^{l}$ and $\epsilon^{-1}\approx 2^{j}+2^{-l}+2^{l}\rho_{2}^{-1/2}$ . Arguing as before, we estimate, if $2^{j}=\max(2^{j},2^{-l},2^{l}\rho_{2}^{-1/2})$ , $\begin{split}&|I^{1}_{l}|\lesssim 2^{2j}/(|t|^{2}2^{2l})\cdot 2^{l}\rho_{2}^{1/4}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\lesssim\rho_{2}^{5/4}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\cdot\rho_{2}2^{-l},\\
&|I^{2}_{l}|\lesssim 2^{2j}/(|t|^{2}2^{2l})\cdot 2^{l}(2^{l}+\rho_{2}^{1/2})2^{-\gamma j}\lesssim 2^{-\gamma j}\rho_{2}^{2}+\rho_{2}^{3/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j}\cdot\rho_{2}2^{-l}.\end{split}$ On the other hand, if $2^{-l}=\max(2^{j},2^{-l},2^{l}\rho_{2}^{-1/2})$ then $|I^{1}_{l}|+|I^{2}_{l}|\lesssim 2^{-2l}/(|t|^{2}2^{2l})\cdot 2^{2l}\rho_{2}^{1/2}\lesssim 2^{-2l}|t|^{-2}\rho_{2}^{1/2}.$ Finally, if $2^{l}\rho_{2}^{-1/2}=\max(2^{j},2^{-l},2^{l}\rho_{2}^{-1/2})$ then $|I^{1}_{l}|+|I^{2}_{l}|\lesssim(2^{2l}\rho_{2}^{-1})/(|t|^{2}2^{2l})\cdot 2^{2l}\rho_{2}^{1/2}\lesssim 2^{2l}|t|^{-2}\rho_{2}^{-1/2}.$ Therefore $\sum_{l\geq l_{0}+1}|I^{1}_{l}|+|I^{2}_{l}|\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j}\rho_{2}^{5/4}.$ (A.58) The desired bound ( A.54 ) follows from ( A.57 ) and ( A.58 ).
∎ Our last lemma in this section is a bilinear estimate. Recall the operators $Q_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ defined in ( 5.4 ), $\mathcal{F}[Q_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g)](\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is[\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta)]}m_{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{g}(\eta)\,d\eta.$ Lemma A.6 . Assume $s\in\mathbb{R}$ , $\sigma\in\{i,e,b\}$ , $\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0}$ , and $\|f\|_{Z\cap H^{N_{0}}}\leq 1,\qquad\|g\|_{Z\cap H^{N_{0}}}\leq 1.$ (A.59) Then, for any $k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}$ , $\|P_{k^{\prime}}Q_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{k^{\prime}_{\sigma}}\min[(1+s)^{-1-\beta},2^{3k^{\prime}/2}]\cdot\min[1,2^{-(N_{0}-5)k^{\prime}}].$ (A.60) Moreover $\text{ if }\quad 2^{k^{\prime}}\in[2^{-D},2^{D}]\text{ and }\sigma\in\{e,b\}\quad\text{ or }\quad 2^{k^{\prime}}\in(0,2^{D}]\text{ and }\sigma=i,$ (A.61) then $\|\mathcal{F}P_{k^{\prime}}Q_{s}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(f,g)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(1+s)^{-1+\beta/10}2^{-k^{\prime}}.$ (A.62) Proof of Lemma A.6 . Clearly, the left-hand side of ( A.60 ) is dominated by $\begin{split}C\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|\varphi_{k^{\prime}}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-is[\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)+\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta)]}m_{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{P_{k_{1}}f}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{P_{k_{2}}g}(\eta,s)\,d\eta\Big\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}.\end{split}$ (A.63) Using ( A.26 )–( A.27 ) and the assumption ( A.59 ), $\begin{split}&\|P_{k^{\prime\prime}}f\|_{L^{2}}+\|P_{k^{\prime\prime}}g\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\min(2^{(1+\beta-\alpha)k^{\prime\prime}},2^{-N_{0}k^{\prime\prime}}),\\
&\|e^{-is\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}}P_{k^{\prime\prime}}f\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|e^{-is\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}}P_{k^{\prime\prime}}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\min(2^{(1/2-\beta-\alpha)k^{\prime\prime}},2^{-6k^{\prime\prime}})(1+s)^{-1-\beta},\end{split}$ (A.64) for any $k^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}$ . Using ( A.64 ) and the description of the symbols $m_{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ in Lemma 3.3 , the expression in ( A.63 ) is dominated by $\begin{split}C2^{k^{\prime}_{\sigma}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z},\,k_{1}\leq k_{2},\,k^{\prime}\leq k_{2}+4}\min(2^{(1+\beta-\alpha)k_{2}},2^{-(N_{0}-2)k_{2}})\cdot\min(2^{(1/2-\beta-\alpha)k_{1}},2^{-6k_{1}})(1+s)^{-1-\beta}\\
\lesssim 2^{k^{\prime}_{\sigma}}(1+s)^{-1-\beta}\min(1,2^{-(N_{0}-5)k^{\prime}}).\end{split}$ Moreover, if $k^{\prime}\leq 0$ , we use again ( A.64 ) and Lemma 3.3 to estimate the expression in ( A.63 ) by $\begin{split}&C\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{3k^{\prime}/2}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-is[\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)+\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta)]}m_{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{P_{k_{1}}f}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{P_{k_{2}}g}(\eta,s)\,d\eta\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{3k^{\prime}/2}2^{k^{\prime}_{\sigma}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\min(2^{(1+\beta-\alpha)k_{1}},2^{-(N_{0}-2)k_{1}})\cdot\min(2^{(1+\beta-\alpha)k_{2}},2^{-(N_{0}-2)k_{2}})\\
&\lesssim 2^{3k^{\prime}/2}2^{k^{\prime}_{\sigma}}.\end{split}$ The desired bound ( A.60 ) follows. To prove ( A.62 ) we use first Lemma 3.3 and Lemma A.1 and decompose the functions $f,g$ in suitable atoms. It suffices to prove that if $\|h_{1}\|_{Z\cap H^{N_{0}}}+\|h_{2}\|_{Z\cap H^{N_{0}}}\leq 1,$ (A.65) and we decompose $h_{i}=\sum_{(k_{i},j_{i})\in\mathcal{J}}h^{i}_{k_{i},j_{i}},\qquad h^{i}_{k_{i},j_{i}}:=P_{[k_{i}-2,k_{i}+2]}(\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k_{i})}_{j_{i}}\cdot P_{k_{i}}h_{i}),\qquad i=1,2,$ then $\sum_{(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J}}2^{k^{\prime}}\Big|\varphi_{k^{\prime}}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is[\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta)]}\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta)\,d\eta\Big|\lesssim(1+s)^{-1+\beta/10}2^{-k^{\prime}},$ (A.66) for any $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0}$ , $s\in\mathbb{R}$ , and $k^{\prime},\sigma$ as in ( A.61 ). We use first only the $L^{2}$ bounds $\|h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\min(2^{-N_{0}k_{1}},2^{(2\beta-\alpha)\widetilde{k_{1}}}2^{-(1-\beta)j_{1}}),\quad\|h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\min(2^{-N_{0}k_{2}},2^{(2\beta-\alpha)\widetilde{k_{2}}}2^{-(1-\beta)j_{2}}),$ (A.67) see ( A.65 ) and ( A.19 ). The full bound ( A.66 ) follows easily if $s^{1-\beta/10}\leq 2^{D^{2}}2^{-2k^{\prime}}$ . Assuming $s^{1-\beta/10}\geq 2^{D^{2}}2^{-2k^{\prime}}$ we estimate easily $\sum_{((k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2}))\in J_{1}}2^{k^{\prime}}\Big|\varphi_{k^{\prime}}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is[\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta)]}\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta)\,d\eta\Big|\lesssim s^{-1}2^{-k^{\prime}},$ where $J_{1}:=\{((k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2}))\in\mathcal{J}\times\mathcal{J}:2^{\max(k_{1},k_{2})}\geq s^{2/N_{0}}\text{ or }2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})}\geq s^{1+4\beta}2^{k^{\prime}}\}.$ Let $J_{2}:=\{((k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2}))\in\mathcal{J}\times\mathcal{J}:\,2^{\max(k_{1},k_{2})}\leq s^{2/N_{0}}\text{ and }2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})}\leq s^{1+4\beta}2^{k^{\prime}}\},$ and notice that $J_{2}$ has at most $C(\ln s)^{4}$ elements. Therefore, for ( A.66 ) it suffices to prove that $\Big|\varphi_{k^{\prime}}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-is[\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)+\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta)]}\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta)\,d\eta\Big|\lesssim 2^{-2k^{\prime}}s^{-1+\beta/11},$ (A.68) provided that $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0}$ , $k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-\infty,D]$ , $s^{1-\beta/10}\geq 2^{D^{2}}2^{-2k^{\prime}}$ , and $((k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2}))\in J_{2}$ . Assume first that $2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})}\geq 2^{-D^{2}}s^{1-\beta/11}2^{k^{\prime}}.$ (A.69) Without loss of generality, in proving ( A.68 ) we may assume that $j_{1}\leq j_{2}$ . Then, using ( 4.6 ), ( 4.9 ) and the assumption ( A.65 ), we have $\|\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{-(1+\beta)j_{2}}2^{3k_{2}/2}(2^{\alpha k_{2}}+2^{10k_{2}})^{-1}.$ (A.70) Using ( A.20 ), $\|\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-\widetilde{k_{1}}/2}$ . Using also ( A.67 ) we estimate the left-hand side of ( A.68 ) by $C\min(\|\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}\|_{L^{1}},\|\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}\|_{L^{2}})\lesssim\min(2^{-\widetilde{k_{1}}/2}2^{-(1+\beta)j_{2}},2^{\widetilde{k_{1}}(1+\beta-\alpha)}2^{-(1-\beta)j_{2}})\lesssim 2^{-j_{2}}.$ The desired bound ( A.68 ) follows if we assume ( A.69 ). Assume now that $2^{2\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\leq 2^{D^{2}}2^{-2k^{\prime}}s^{-1+\beta/11}.$ (A.71) Without loss of generality, in proving ( A.68 ) we may assume that $k_{2}\leq k_{1}$ . Then, using ( A.70 ) we estimate the left-hand side of ( A.68 ) by $C\|\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{-k_{1}/2}2^{5k_{2}/2}\lesssim 2^{2k_{2}},$ as desired Finally it remains to prove ( A.68 ) assuming that $2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})}\leq 2^{-D^{2}}s^{1-\beta/11}2^{k^{\prime}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad 2^{2\min(k_{1},k_{2})}\geq 2^{D^{2}}2^{-2k^{\prime}}s^{-1+\beta/11}.$ (A.72) In this case we would like to integrate by parts in $\eta$ to estimate the integral in ( A.68 ).
Using the bounds ( A.1 ) and ( A.20 ), $\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[1-\varphi_{\leq 0}(\delta^{-1}\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta))]e^{-is[\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)+\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta)]}\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta)\,d\eta\Big|\lesssim s^{-2}$ (A.73) as long as $\delta\in(0,1],\quad s\delta\geq s^{\beta^{2}}2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})},\quad s\delta\geq s^{\beta^{2}}\delta^{-1}2^{-\min(k_{1},k_{2},0)}.$ (A.74) Therefore, letting $D(\xi,\delta):=\{\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:\,|\eta|\in[2^{k_{2}-4},2^{k_{2}+4}],\,|\xi-\eta|\in[2^{k_{1}-4},2^{k_{1}+4}],\,|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq 2\delta\},$ (A.75) for ( A.68 ) it remains to prove that, for some $\delta$ satisfying ( A.74 ), $\Big|\varphi_{k^{\prime}}(\xi)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{D(\xi,\delta)}(\eta)\big|\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta)\big|\,\big|\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta)\big|\,d\eta\Big|\lesssim 2^{-2k^{\prime}}s^{-1+\beta/11},$ (A.76) provided that $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0}$ , $k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-\infty,D]$ , and $((k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2}))\in J_{2}$ satisfies ( A.72 ). Without loss of generality, we may assume that $k_{2}\leq k_{1}$ . We examine now the sets $D(\xi,\delta)$ defined in ( A.75 ). Assume that $\mu=(\sigma_{1}\iota_{1})$ , $\nu=(\sigma_{2}\iota_{2})$ , $\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{i,e,b\}$ , $\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{+,-\}$ . Notice that $\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)=-\iota_{1}\frac{\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma_{1}}(|\eta-\xi|)}{|\eta-\xi|}(\eta-\xi)-\iota_{2}\frac{\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma_{2}}(|\eta|)}{|\eta|}\eta=A(\eta-\xi)+B\eta,$ where $A:=-\iota_{1}\frac{\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma_{1}}(|\eta-\xi|)}{|\eta-\xi|},\qquad B:=-\iota_{2}\frac{\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma_{2}}(|\eta|)}{|\eta|}.$ In view of Lemma A.4 (i), we have $\min(|A|,|B|)\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}2^{-\max(k_{1},0)}$ . Letting $\xi=se$ , $e\in\mathbb{S}^{2}$ , $s\in[2^{k^{\prime}-2},2^{k^{\prime}+2}]$ , and $\eta=re+\eta^{\prime}$ , $r\in\mathbb{R}$ , $\eta^{\prime}\cdot e=0$ , and assuming $\eta\in D(\xi,\delta)$ , it follows that $|(A+B)\eta^{\prime}|\leq 2\delta,\qquad|(A+B)r-As|\leq 2\delta.$ We let $\delta:=\max(s^{\beta^{2}-1}2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})},s^{(\beta^{2}-1)/2}2^{-\min(k_{2},0)/2})$ , such that ( A.74 ) is satisfied. In view of ( A.72 ), it follows that $|(A+B)r|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}2^{-\max(k_{1},0)}2^{k^{\prime}}$ , therefore $|A+B|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}2^{-\max(k_{1},0)}2^{k^{\prime}}2^{-k_{2}}$ . This shows that $|\eta^{\prime}|\lesssim 2^{\max(k_{1},0)}2^{-k^{\prime}}2^{k_{2}}\delta$ . In other words, we proved that if $\xi=se$ , $e\in\mathbb{S}^{2}$ , $s\in[2^{k^{\prime}-2},2^{k^{\prime}+2}]$ , then $D(\xi,\delta)\subseteq\{\eta=re+\eta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:|r|+|\eta^{\prime}|^{2}\leq 2^{k_{2}+4},\,\eta^{\prime}\cdot e=0,\,|\eta^{\prime}|\lesssim 2^{\max(k_{1},0)}2^{-k^{\prime}}2^{k_{2}}\delta\}.$ (A.77) Using ( A.77 ) and the $L^{\infty}$ bounds $\|\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-k_{1}/2}$ , $\|\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-k_{2}/2}$ , we can bound the left-hand side of ( A.76 ) by $C2^{-k_{1}/2}2^{-k_{2}/2}\cdot(2^{\max(k_{1},0)}2^{-k^{\prime}}2^{k_{2}}\delta)^{2}2^{k_{2}}\lesssim 2^{2\min(k_{2},0)}\delta^{2}2^{-2k^{\prime}}s^{8/N_{0}}.$ This suffices to prove ( A.76 ) if $2^{\max(j_{1},j_{2})}2^{\min(k_{2},0)}\leq s^{1/2}$ . On the other hand, if $j_{1}\leq j_{2}$ and $2^{j_{2}}2^{\min(k_{2},0)}\geq s^{1/2}$ then we estimate the left-hand side of ( A.76 ) by $C2^{-k_{1}/2}\|\mathbf{1}_{D(\xi,\delta)}(\eta)\cdot\widehat{h^{2}_{k_{2},j_{2}}}(\eta)\|_{L^{1}_{\eta}}\lesssim 2^{-k_{1}/2}\cdot 2^{k_{2}/2}2^{\max(k_{1},0)}2^{-k^{\prime}}2^{k_{2}}\delta\cdot 2^{-j_{2}}\lesssim 2^{-k^{\prime}}s^{-1+\beta/11},$ which also suffices to prove ( A.76 ). Finally, if $j_{1}\geq j_{2}$ and $2^{j_{1}}2^{\min(k_{2},0)}\geq s^{1/2}$ then we estimate the left-hand side of ( A.76 ) by $C2^{-k_{2}/2}\|\mathbf{1}_{D(\xi,\delta)}(\eta)\cdot\widehat{h^{1}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L^{1}_{\eta}}\lesssim 2^{-k_{2}/2}\cdot 2^{k_{2}/2}2^{\max(k_{1},0)}2^{-k^{\prime}}2^{k_{2}}\delta\cdot 2^{-j_{1}}\lesssim 2^{-k^{\prime}}s^{-1+\beta/11},$ which also suffices to prove ( A.76 ). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎ Appendix B Classification of resonances We define the order $i<e<b$ . Recall that we introduced a large number $D\gg(\varepsilon^{-1}+C_{b})^{10}$ , depending only on $\varepsilon$ and $C_{b}$ . For $\sigma\in\{i,e,b\}$ and $\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0}$ , see definition ( 3.21 ), $\mu=(\sigma_{1}\iota_{1}),\qquad\nu=(\sigma_{2}\iota_{2}),\qquad\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{i,e,b\},\qquad\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{+,-\},$ (B.1) recall the definitions of the smooth functions $\Lambda_{\sigma}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to(0,\infty)$ , $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}$ and $\Xi^{\mu,\nu}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , $\begin{split}&\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)=\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)-\iota_{1}\Lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(\xi-\eta)-\iota_{2}\Lambda_{\sigma_{2}}(\eta),\\
&\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)=(\nabla_{\eta}\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu})(\xi,\eta)=-\iota_{1}\nabla\Lambda_{\sigma_{1}}(\eta-\xi)-\iota_{2}\nabla\Lambda_{\sigma_{2}}(\eta).\end{split}$ (B.2) In this subsection we prove several lemmas describing the structure of almost resonant sets, which are the sets where both $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|$ and $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|$ are small. Recall the sets $\begin{split}\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}=\{(\xi,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}:&\,|\xi|\in[2^{k-4},2^{k+4}],\,|\xi-\eta|\in[2^{k_{1}-4},2^{k_{1}+4}],\,|\eta|\in[2^{k_{2}-4},2^{k_{2}+4}],\\
&\,|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq\delta_{1},\,|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq\delta_{2}\}.\end{split}$ (B.3) defined for $\sigma\in\{i,e,b\}$ , $\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0}$ , $k,k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}$ , $\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\in(0,\infty)$ . We define also $\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}:=\{(\xi,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}:\,|\xi|\in[2^{k-4},2^{k+4}],\,|\xi-\eta|\in[2^{k_{1}-4},2^{k_{1}+4}],\,|\eta|\in[2^{k_{2}-4},2^{k_{2}+4}]\}.$ Given a phase $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}$ and a set of phases $\mathcal{T}$ , we denote $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\mathcal{T}$ if either $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\mathcal{T}$ or $\Phi^{\sigma;\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{T}$ , and $\Phi^{\sigma\mu,\nu}\notin\notin\mathcal{T}$ if neither possibility holds. We show first that some phases do not contribute in the analysis of resonant interactions. We define the $39$ strongly elliptic phases, $\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{Sell}:=\{&\Phi^{i;i+,e+},\Phi^{i,i+,e-},\Phi^{i;i+,b+},\Phi^{i;i+,b-},\Phi^{i,i-,e-},\Phi^{i;i-,b-},\Phi^{i,e+,e+},\Phi^{i;e+,b+},\Phi^{i,e-,e-},\Phi^{i;e-,b-},\\
&\Phi^{i;b+,b+},\Phi^{i;b-,b-},\Phi^{e;i+,i-},\Phi^{e;i+,e-},\Phi^{e;i+,b-},\Phi^{e;i-,i-},\Phi^{e;i-,e-},\Phi^{e;i-,b-},\Phi^{e;e+,e+},\Phi^{e;e+,e-},\\
&\Phi^{e;e+,b+},\Phi^{e;e+,b-},\Phi^{e;e-,e-},\Phi^{e;e-,b-},\Phi^{e;b+,b+},\Phi^{e;b-,b-},\Phi^{b;i+,i-},\Phi^{b;i+,e-},\Phi^{b;i+,b-},\Phi^{b;i-,i-},\\
&\Phi^{b;i-,e-},\Phi^{b;i-,b-},\Phi^{b;e+,e-},\Phi^{b;e+,b-},\Phi^{b;e-,e-},\Phi^{b;e-,b-},\Phi^{b;b+,b+},\Phi^{b;b+,b-},\Phi^{b;b-,b-}\}.\end{split}$ We also define $4$ additional nonresonant phases $\mathcal{T}_{NR}=\{\Phi^{e;i+,i+},\Phi^{e;b+,b-},\Phi^{b;i+,i+},\Phi^{b;i-,e+}\}.$ Lemma B.1 . Assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\mathcal{T}_{Sell}\cup\mathcal{T}_{NR}$ . If $\delta_{1}\leq 2^{-D}2^{-4\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)},\,\,\delta_{2}\leq 2^{-D}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)}\quad\hbox{then}\quad\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}=\emptyset.$ Proof of Lemma B.1 . We claim that if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\mathcal{T}_{Sell}$ , then we have $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)}.$ (B.4) This would clearly suffice to prove the claim for the strongly elliptic phases. If $(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})=(-,-)$ , the proof of ( B.4 ) is a direct consequence of the fact that $\lambda_{b}\geq\lambda_{e}\geq 1$ . To deal with the remaining $22$ phases in $\mathcal{T}_{Sell}$ we observe that, as a consequence of Lemma A.4 , we have, for any $r\in[0,\infty)$ , $r\leq\lambda_{i}(r)\leq\lambda_{e}(r)\leq\lambda_{b}(r),\qquad\lambda_{b}(r)-\lambda_{e}(r)\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}r,\qquad\lambda_{e}(r)-\lambda_{i}(r)\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1+r.$ (B.5) In addition, for any $r_{1},r_{2}\in[0,\infty)$ , $\begin{split}&\lambda_{i}(r_{1})+\lambda_{i}(r_{2})-\lambda_{i}(r_{1}+r_{2})\geq 0,\\
&\lambda_{e}(r_{1})+\lambda_{e}(r_{2})-\lambda_{e}(r_{1}+r_{2})\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}(1+\min(r_{1},r_{2}))^{-1},\\
&\lambda_{b}(r_{1})+\lambda_{b}(r_{2})-\lambda_{b}(r_{1}+r_{2})\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}(1+\min(r_{1},r_{2}))^{-1}.\end{split}$ (B.6) Indeed, the first bound in ( B.6 ) follows from the formula $\lambda_{i}(r)=rq_{i}(r)$ in Lemma A.4 , and the fact that $q_{i}$ is decreasing. For the second bound in ( B.6 ) we use the fact that the function $r\to\lambda_{e}(r)-h_{\varepsilon}(r)$ is nonnegative and decreasing on $[0,\infty)$ (see ( A.15 )), therefore $\lambda_{e}(r_{1})+\lambda_{e}(r_{2})-\lambda_{e}(r_{1}+r_{2})\geq h_{\varepsilon}(r_{1})+h_{\varepsilon}(r_{2})-h_{\varepsilon}(r_{1}+r_{2})\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}(1+\min(r_{1},r_{2}))^{-1}.$ The third bound follows directly from the definition. Using ( B.5 ), ( B.6 ), and the monotonicity of the functions $\lambda_{i},\lambda_{e},\lambda_{b}$ on $[0,\infty)$ , we can now prove lower bounds for the absolute values of the 22 phases in $\mathcal{T}_{Sell}$ , which correspond to $(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})=(+,+)$ , $\begin{split}-\Phi^{i;i+,e+}(\xi,\eta)&=[-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)+\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{e}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
-\Phi^{i;i+,b+}(\xi,\eta)&=[-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)+\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
-\Phi^{i;e+,e+}(\xi,\eta)&=[-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)+\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)]+[\Lambda_{e}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
-\Phi^{i;e+,b+}(\xi,\eta)&=[-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)+\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
-\Phi^{i;b+,b+}(\xi,\eta)&=[-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)+\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\xi-\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
-\Phi^{e;e+,e+}(\xi,\eta)&=[-\Lambda_{e}(\xi)+\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{e}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)},\\
-\Phi^{e;e+,b+}(\xi,\eta)&=[-\Lambda_{e}(\xi)+\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{e}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\eta)-\Lambda_{e}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)},\\
-\Phi^{e;b+,b+}(\xi,\eta)&=[-\Lambda_{b}(\xi)+\Lambda_{b}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{b}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\xi)-\Lambda_{e}(\xi)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)},\\
-\Phi^{b;b+,b+}(\xi,\eta)&=[-\Lambda_{b}(\xi)+\Lambda_{b}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{b}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)},\end{split}$ or $(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})=(+,-)$ , $\begin{split}\Phi^{i;i+,e-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{i}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{e}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
\Phi^{i;i+,b-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{i}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
\Phi^{e;i+,i-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{i}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{e}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
\Phi^{e;i+,e-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{i}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{e}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)]+[\Lambda_{e}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
\Phi^{e;i+,b-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{i}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{e}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
\Phi^{e;e+,e-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{e}(\xi)-\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{e}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)},\\
\Phi^{e;e+,b-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{e}(\xi)-\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{e}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\eta)-\Lambda_{e}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)},\\
\Phi^{b;i+,i-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{i}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
\Phi^{b;i+,e-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{i}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)]+[\Lambda_{e}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
\Phi^{b;i+,b-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{i}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\xi)-\Lambda_{i}(\xi)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\eta)-\Lambda_{i}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,\\
\Phi^{b;e+,e-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{e}(\xi)-\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{e}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\xi)-\Lambda_{e}(\xi)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)},\\
\Phi^{b;e+,b-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{b}(\xi)-\Lambda_{b}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{b}(\eta)]+[\Lambda_{b}(\xi-\eta)-\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)},\\
\Phi^{b;b+,b-}(\xi,\eta)&=[\Lambda_{b}(\xi)-\Lambda_{b}(\xi-\eta)+\Lambda_{b}(\eta)]\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-\max(k_{1},k_{2},0)}.\end{split}$ The desired lower bound ( B.4 ) follows for all phases $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\mathcal{T}_{Sell}$ . We now consider the phases $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\mathcal{T}_{NR}$ . Assume first $\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}=i$ . Then, since $\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ , we see that smallness of $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|$ implies that $(\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta\geq 0$ . But then $|\xi|\geq\max(|\xi-\eta|,|\eta|)$ and, using ( A.7 ), $\min[\Phi^{e;i+,i+}(\xi,\eta),\Phi^{b;i+,i+}(\xi,\eta)]\geq\lambda_{e}(|\xi|)-2\lambda_{i}(|\xi|)\geq h_{\varepsilon}(|\xi|)-2\sqrt{(T+1)(\varepsilon+1)}|\xi|\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}1,$ and the desired conclusion $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;i+,i+}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}=\emptyset$ , $\sigma\in\{e,b\}$ , follows. Assume now that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{e;b+,b-}\}$ . If $\max(k_{1},k_{2})\leq-D/10$ , then, using ( A.5 ), $\Phi^{e;b+,b-}(\xi,\eta)\geq 1$ . On the other hand, if $\max(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D/10$ , we see from smallness of $|\Xi^{b+,b-}(\xi,\eta)|$ that $|\xi|\leq 2^{-D}$ . But in this case, $|\lambda_{b}(|\xi-\eta|)-\lambda_{b}(|\eta|)|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}|\xi|\leq\lambda_{e}(0)/2,$ hence $\Phi^{e;b+,b-}(\xi,\eta)\geq 1$ . Finally, assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{b;i-,e+}\}$ . By symmetry we may assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)=\Phi^{b;i-,e+}(\xi,\eta)=\lambda_{b}(|\xi|)+\lambda_{i}(|\xi-\eta|)-\lambda_{e}(|\eta|).$ The condition $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq 2^{-D}$ and Lemma A.4 (i) show that $2^{k_{2}}\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ . The condition $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq 2^{-D}$ then shows that $|\eta|\geq|\xi|$ . Since $\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r)\leq\frac{\sqrt{1+T}}{\sqrt{1+\varepsilon}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r)\geq\frac{(1-\sqrt{\varepsilon})Tr}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}\sqrt{1+Tr^{2}}},$ for any $r\in[0,\infty)$ , see Lemma A.4 (ii) and (iii), the restriction $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq 2^{-D}$ shows that $|\eta|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon/T}$ . Therefore $|\xi|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon/T}$ and $|\xi-\eta|\leq 2\sqrt{\varepsilon/T}$ . Since $r_{\ast}\geq T^{-1/2}$ , see Lemma A.4 (i), it follows that $|\xi-\eta|\leq r_{\ast}/2$ . Therefore $\lambda^{\prime}_{i}$ is decreasing on the interval $[0,|\xi-\eta|]$ and we estimate, recalling that $2^{-D}\geq\big|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\big|\geq\big|\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(|\xi-\eta|)-\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(|\eta|)\big|$ , $\begin{split}\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)&=\int_{0}^{|\xi-\eta|}\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(s)\,ds+\int_{0}^{|\xi|}\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(s)\,ds-\int_{0}^{|\eta|}\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(s)\,ds\\
&\geq C_{C_{b},\varepsilon}^{-1}+|\xi-\eta|\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(|\xi-\eta|)-(|\eta|-|\xi|)\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(|\eta|)\\
&\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1.\end{split}$ This provides the contradiction.
∎ We consider now the remaining 20 phases, and define three sets of phases $\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{A}:=&\{\Phi^{i;i-,e+},\Phi^{i;i-,b+},\Phi^{i;e+,b-},\Phi^{i;e-,b+},\Phi^{e;i+,e+},\Phi^{e;i+,b+},\Phi^{e;i-,b+},\Phi^{e;e-,b+},\\
&\,\,\Phi^{b;i+,e+},\Phi^{b;i+,b+},\Phi^{b;e+,e+},\Phi^{b;e+,b+},\Phi^{b;e-,b+}\},\\
\mathcal{T}_{B}:=&\{\Phi^{e;i+,e+},\Phi^{e;i-,e+},\Phi^{b;i+,b+},\Phi^{b;i-,b+}\},\\
\mathcal{T}_{C}:=&\{\Phi^{i;i+,i+},\Phi^{i;i+,i-},\Phi^{i;i-,i-},\Phi^{i;e+,e-},\Phi^{i;e+,b-},\Phi^{i;e-,b+},\Phi^{i;b+,b-}\}.\end{split}$ (B.7) Notice that some phases, such as $\Phi^{e;i+,e+}$ , belong to more than one set. The set $\mathcal{T}_{A}$ corresponds to phases having nondegenerate stationary points on spheres, while the sets $\mathcal{T}_{B},\mathcal{T}_{C}$ consist of phases with degenerate behavior around $0$ (in $\eta$ , $\xi-\eta$ , or $\xi$ ). More precisely: Proposition B.2 . Assume $k,k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}$ and that there is a point $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ satisfying $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq\delta_{1}=2^{-10D}2^{-4\max(0,k_{1},k_{2})},\quad|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq\delta_{2}=2^{-10D}2^{-\max(0,k_{1},k_{2})}.$ Then one of the three following possibilities holds: Case A : $-D/2\leq k,k_{1},k_{2}\leq D/2$ and $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\mathcal{T}_{A}$ . Case B : $\min(k_{1},k_{2})\leq-D/3$ , $k\geq-D/4$ and $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\mathcal{T}_{B}$ . Case C : $k\leq-D/4$ and $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\mathcal{T}_{C}$ . Proof of Proposition B.2 . We divide the proof in several steps: Step 1. Assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{i;i+,i+},\Phi^{i;i+,i-},\Phi^{i;i-,i-}\}.$ (B.8) These phases are only in the set $\mathcal{T}_{C}$ , and we have to prove that if $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ then $k\leq-D/2.$ (B.9) Assume for contradiction that $k\geq-D/2$ . Using Lemma A.4 (iii) it is easy to see that if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{i;i-,i-}\}$ then $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}\max(|\xi|,|\eta|,|\xi-\eta|)\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}2^{-D/2}$ , which is not possible. On the other hand, using Lemma A.4 (iii), for any $r,s\in[0,\infty)$ $\lambda_{i}(r)+\lambda_{i}(s)-\lambda_{i}(r+s)=r(q_{i}(r)-q_{i}(r+s))+s(q_{i}(s)-q_{i}(r+s))\gtrsim_{\varepsilon,C_{b}}\frac{\min(r,s)(r+s)^{2}}{(1+(r+s)^{2})(1+\min(r,s)^{2})}.$ Therefore if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{i;i+,i+},\Phi^{i;i+,i-}\}$ , $k\geq-D/2$ , and $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ , then $\min(k_{1},k_{2})\leq-5D$ . On the other hand, if $\min(k_{1},k_{2})\leq-5D$ and $\max(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D/2-10$ , then we use the bound $\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r)-\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(s)\geq q_{i}(0)-C_{C_{b},\varepsilon}r-q_{i}(s)\geq 2^{-2D},$ whenever $0\leq r\leq 2^{-4D}$ and $s\geq 2^{-D}$ , which is a consequence of Lemma A.4 (iii). Therefore, in this case $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\geq 2^{-2D}$ for all $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ , which provides the contradiction. Step 2. Assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{i;e+,e-},\Phi^{i;b+,b-}\}.$ (B.10) These phases are only in the set $\mathcal{T}_{C}$ , and it suffices to prove that if $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ then $k\leq-D/2.$ (B.11) Assume for contradiction that $k\geq-D/2$ . Since $\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(0)=0$ , $\sigma\in\{e,b\}$ , the restriction $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq\delta_{1}$ shows that $\min(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D$ . On the other hand, if $\min(k,k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D$ then, for any $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ we have $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}\big|\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(|\eta-\xi|)-\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(|\eta|)\big|+\max[\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(|\eta-\xi|),\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(|\eta|)]\Big|\frac{\eta-\xi}{|\eta-\xi|}-\frac{\eta}{|\eta|}\Big|\geq 2^{D}\delta_{1},$ which provides a contradiction. Step 3. Assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{i;e+,b-},\Phi^{i;e-,b+}\}.$ (B.12) These phases are in the sets $\mathcal{T}_{C}$ and $\mathcal{T}_{A}$ , and we have to prove that if $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ then $\text{ either }\qquad k\leq-D/4\qquad\text{ or }\qquad-D/2\leq k,k_{1},k_{2}\leq D/2.$ This is equivalent to proving that $\text{ if }\quad k\geq-D/4\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset\quad\text{ then }\quad-D/2\leq k,k_{1},k_{2}\leq D/2.$ (B.13) Since $\lim_{r\to\infty}\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r)=1,\qquad\lim_{r\to\infty}\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r)=\sqrt{T/\varepsilon},\qquad\lim_{r\to\infty}\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(r)=\sqrt{C_{b}/\varepsilon},$ (B.14) it is easy to see that the condition $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ implies that $\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\leq D/4$ . Since $k\geq-D/4$ it follows that $\max(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D/4-10$ . Recall that $\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(0)=0$ and $\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma}(r)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}(1+r^{2})^{-3/2}$ , $\sigma\in\{e,b\}$ (see Lemma A.4 (i)). Since $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq\delta_{1}$ for some $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ it follows that $\min(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D/2$ as desired. Step 4. Assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{e;i-,e+},\Phi^{b;i-,b+}\}.$ (B.15) These phases are only in the set $\mathcal{T}_{B}$ , and we have to prove that if $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ then $k\geq-D/4\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\min(k_{1},k_{2})\leq-D/3.$ (B.16) It is easy to see that $\max(k_{1},k_{2},k)\geq-D/10$ ; otherwise $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\geq 2^{-2D}$ for all $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ , in view of the fact that $\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(0)=\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(0)=0$ and $\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(0)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ . Therefore it remains to prove that if $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ then $\min(k_{1},k_{2})\leq-D/3.$ (B.17) Assume, for contradiction, that ( B.17 ) fails. We may assume, without loss of generality, that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)=\Phi^{\sigma;i-,\sigma+}(\xi,\eta)=\lambda_{\sigma}(|\xi|)+\lambda_{i}(|\xi-\eta|)-\lambda_{\sigma}(|\eta|),$ for $\sigma\in\{e,b\}$ . We argue as in the proof of Lemma B.1 , $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}=\Phi^{b;i-,e+}\in\mathcal{T}_{NR}$ . The conditions $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq 2^{-10D}$ and $k_{1}\geq-D/3$ show that $|\eta|\geq|\xi|$ . Since $\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r)\leq\frac{\sqrt{1+T}}{\sqrt{1+\varepsilon}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(r)\geq\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r)\geq\frac{(1-\sqrt{\varepsilon})Tr}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}\sqrt{1+Tr^{2}}},$ for any $r\in[0,\infty)$ , see Lemma A.4 (ii) and (iii), the restriction $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\leq 2^{-D}$ shows that $|\eta|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon/T}$ . Therefore $|\xi|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon/T}$ and $|\xi-\eta|\leq 2\sqrt{\varepsilon/T}$ . Since $r_{\ast}\geq T^{-1/2}$ , see Lemma A.4 (i), it follows that $|\xi-\eta|\leq r_{\ast}/2$ . Therefore $\lambda^{\prime}_{i}$ is decreasing on the interval $[0,|\xi-\eta|]$ and we estimate, recalling that $2^{-10D}\geq\big|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\big|\geq\big|\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(|\xi-\eta|)-\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(|\eta|)\big|$ and $|\xi-\eta|\geq 2^{-D/2}$ , $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)=\int_{0}^{|\xi-\eta|}\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(s)\,ds-\int_{|\xi|}^{|\eta|}\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(s)\,ds\geq 2^{-2D}+|\xi-\eta|\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(|\xi-\eta|)-(|\eta|-|\xi|)\lambda^{\prime}_{\sigma}(|\eta|)\geq 2^{-4D}.$ This provides the contradiction. Step 5. Assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{e;i+,e+},\Phi^{b;i+,b+}\}.$ (B.18) These phases are in the sets $\mathcal{T}_{B}$ and $\mathcal{T}_{A}$ , and we have to prove that if $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ then $\text{ either }\qquad k\geq-D/4,\,\min(k_{1},k_{2})\leq-D/3\qquad\text{ or }\qquad-D/2\leq k,k_{1},k_{2}\leq D/2.$ (B.19) It is easy to see that $\max(k_{1},k_{2},k)\geq-D/10$ ; otherwise $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\geq 2^{-2D}$ for all $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ , in view of the fact that $\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(0)=\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(0)=0$ and $\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(0)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ . Therefore, for ( B.19 ) it suffices to prove that $\text{ if }\qquad\min(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D/3\qquad\text{ then }\qquad-D/2\leq k,k_{1},k_{2}\leq D/2.$ (B.20) In view of ( B.14 ), it is clear that $\min(k_{1},k_{2})\leq D/10$ ; otherwise $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\geq 2^{-2D}$ for all $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ . On the other hand, if $k\geq D/4$ then $\max(k_{1},k_{2})\geq D/4-10$ , and one can use ( B.14 ) again to see easily that this in contradiction with the assumption $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ . Therefore $\max(k,k_{1},k_{2})\leq D/2$ , as desired. Finally, for ( B.20 ) it remains to prove that $k\geq-D/2$ .
