Assuming, for contradiction, that $k\leq-D/2$ and recalling that $\max(k_{1},k_{2},k)\geq-D/10$ , it follows that $\max(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D/10$ , $|k_{1}-k_{2}|\leq 10$ . Therefore $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}2^{-2D}$ , which provides a contradiction. Step 6. Assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{e;e-,b+},\Phi^{b;e+,e+},\Phi^{b;e+,b+},\Phi^{b;e-,b+}\}.$ (B.21) These phases are only in the set $\mathcal{T}_{A}$ , and we have to prove that if $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ then $-D/2\leq k,k_{1},k_{2}$ (B.22) and $k,k_{1},k_{2}\leq D/2.$ (B.23) We prove first ( B.22 ). We notice that $\max(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D/10$ ; otherwise $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ for any $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ , since $\lambda_{e}(0)=\lambda_{b}(0)=\sqrt{1+\varepsilon^{-1}}$ . This implies that $\min(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D/4$ ; otherwise $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}2^{-D}$ for any $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ , since $\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}\min(r,1)$ and $\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(r)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}\min(r,1)$ . To complete the proof of ( B.22 ), assume, for contradiction, that $k\leq-D/2$ , therefore $\max(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D/10$ , $|k_{1}-k_{2}|\leq 10$ . If $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{b;e+,e+},\Phi^{b;e+,b+}\}$ then $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ for any $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ , in contradiction with the assumption $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ . On the other hand, if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{e;e-,b+},\Phi^{b;e-,b+}\}$ then $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}2^{-2D}$ for any $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ , which is again in contradiction with the assumption $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ . This last bound is a consequence of the estimate $\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(r)-\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(r)\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}\min(1,r),\qquad\text{ for any }r\geq 0,$ (B.24) which follows from Lemma A.4 (ii). This completes the proof of ( B.22 ). We prove now ( B.23 ). We notice first that $\min(k,k_{1},k_{2})\leq D/10$ ; otherwise either $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ or $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ for any $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ , using ( B.14 ). Assuming, for contradiction, that ( B.23 ) fails, we need to consider two cases: $\begin{split}\text{ either }\qquad&k\leq D/10,\quad\max(k_{1},k_{2})\geq D/2,\quad|k_{1}-k_{2}|\leq 10,\\
\text{ or }\qquad&\min(k_{1},k_{2})\leq D/10,\quad\max(k,k_{1},k_{2})\geq D/2,\quad|k-\max(k_{1},k_{2})|\leq 10.\end{split}$ (B.25) In the first case, we use ( B.14 ) to see that $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ for any $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{e;e-,b+},\Phi^{b;e+,b+},\Phi^{b;e-,b+}\}$ . We also notice that $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ for any $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{b;e+,e+}\}$ , which completes the contradiction in this case. Assume now that the inequalities in the second line of ( B.25 ) hold. By symmetry we may assume that $k_{1}=\min(k_{1},k_{2})$ . In view of ( B.14 ) it is clear that $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ if $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ and $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{e;e-,b+},\Phi^{b;e+,b+},\Phi^{b;e-,b+}\}$ . Also, using ( B.5 ) it is clear that $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ if $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ and $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{e;,b+,e-},\Phi^{b;e+,e+},\Phi^{b;b+,e+},\Phi^{b;b+,e-}\}$ . This completes the contradiction in this case as well, and the desired bound ( B.23 ) follows. Step 7. Assume that $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{i;i-,e+},\Phi^{i;i-,b+},\Phi^{e;i+,b+},\Phi^{e;i-,b+},\Phi^{b;i+,e+}\}.$ (B.26) These phases are only in the set $\mathcal{T}_{A}$ , and we have to prove that if $\mathcal{L}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{k,k_{1},k_{2};\delta_{1},\delta_{2}}\neq\emptyset$ then $-D/2\leq k,k_{1},k_{2}$ (B.27) and $k,k_{1},k_{2}\leq D/2.$ (B.28) To prove ( B.28 ), assume, for contradiction, that $\max(k_{1},k_{2})\geq D/4$ . By symmetry, we may assume also $k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ . Using ( B.14 ) it is easy to see that $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ if $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ and $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{i;i-,e+},\Phi^{i;i-,b+},\Phi^{e;i+,b+},\Phi^{e;i-,b+},\Phi^{b;i+,e+}\}$ . On the other hand, if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{i;,e+,i-},\Phi^{i;b+,i-},\Phi^{e;b+,i+},\Phi^{e;b+,i-},\Phi^{b;e+,i+}\}$ then, using again ( B.14 ) and the smallness of $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|$ , we necessarily have $k_{1}\leq D/10$ , $|k-k_{2}|\leq 10$ . In this case, however, $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ , as a consequence of ( B.14 ). This completes the proof of the contradiction. We prove now ( B.27 ). We notice that $\max(k_{1},k_{2})\geq-D/10$ ; otherwise $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ for any $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ , since $\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(0)=\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(0)=0,\,\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(0)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ . Assume, for contradiction, that ( B.27 ) fails. We may assume by symmetry that $k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ and need to consider two cases: $\begin{split}\text{ either }\qquad&k_{1}\leq-D/2,\quad k_{2}\geq-D/10,\quad|k-k_{2}|\leq 10,\\
\text{ or }\qquad&k\leq-D/2,\quad k_{2}\geq-D/10,\quad|k_{1}-k_{2}|\leq 10.\end{split}$ (B.29) Assume first that the inequalities in the first line of ( B.29 ) hold. Since $\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(r)\approx_{C_{b},\varepsilon}1$ and $\lambda^{\prime}_{b}(0)=\lambda^{\prime}_{e}(0)=0$ , it is easy to see that $|\Xi^{\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}2^{-2D}$ if $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ and $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{i;e+,i-},\Phi^{i;b+,i-},\Phi^{e;b+,i+},\Phi^{e;b+,i-},\Phi^{b;e+,i+},\}.$ On the other hand, using ( B.5 ), $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}2^{-2D}$ if $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ and $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\{\Phi^{i;i-,e+},\Phi^{i;i-,b+},\Phi^{e;i+,b+},\Phi^{e;i-,b+},\Phi^{b;i+,e+},\}.$ The desired contradiction follows in this case. Finally, assume that the inequalities in the second line of ( B.29 ) hold. Using ( B.5 ) it is easy to see that $|\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)|\gtrsim_{C_{b},\varepsilon}2^{-2D}$ if $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}$ and $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{i;i-,e+},\Phi^{i;i-,b+},\Phi^{e;i+,b+},\Phi^{b;i+,e+}\}$ . On the other hand, if $\Phi^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}\in\in\{\Phi^{e;i-,b+}\}$ , then the contradiction follows by the same argument as in Step 4. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎ Appendix C The multipliers $m_{e;\mu,\nu}$ , $m_{i;\mu,\nu}$ , and $m_{b;\mu,\nu}$ For the sake of completeness, in this section we compute explicitly the multipliers $m_{e;\mu,\nu}$ , $m_{i;\mu,\nu}$ , and $m_{b,\alpha;\mu,\nu}$ . The precise formulas are not used at any other place in the paper; the information we use about these multipliers is summarized in Lemma 3.3 . Let $\begin{split}&U_{e+}:=U_{e},\quad U_{e-}:=\overline{U_{e}},\quad U_{i+}:=U_{i},\quad U_{i-}:=\overline{U_{i}},\quad[U_{b+}]_{\alpha}=[U_{b}]_{\alpha},\quad[U_{b-}]_{\alpha}=\overline{[U_{b}]_{\alpha}}\\
&\widetilde{U_{\mu}}:=(1+R^{2})^{-1/2}U_{\mu},\quad\mu\in\{e+,e-,i+,i-\}.\end{split}$ (C.1) It follows from ( 3.11 ) that $\begin{split}\varepsilon nR_{\alpha}h=&-i\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}+i\Big(\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}-R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\Big)+i\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\\
&-i\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}+i\Big(\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}-R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\Big)+i\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}\\
&+i\Big(\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}+R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\Big)+i\Big(-\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}+R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\Big)\\
&+i\Big(\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}-R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\Big)-i\Big(\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}+R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\Big),\end{split}$ and $\begin{split}\varepsilon R_{\alpha}hR_{\alpha}h=&-R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}+2R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}-R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\\
&-R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}+2R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}-R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}\\
&+2R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}-2R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}-2R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}+2R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}.\end{split}$ Moreover $\begin{split}\rho R_{\alpha}g=&-i\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}+i\Big(\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}-R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\Big)+i\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}\\
&-i\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}+i\Big(\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}-R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\Big)+i\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\\
&-i\Big(\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}+R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\Big)+i\Big(\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}-R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\Big)\\
&+i\Big(-\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}+R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\Big)+i\Big(\frac{|\nabla|R}{\Lambda_{e}}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}+R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\frac{|\nabla|}{\Lambda_{i}}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\Big),\end{split}$ and $\begin{split}R_{\alpha}gR_{\alpha}g=&-R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}+2R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}-R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}\\
&-R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}+2R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}-R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\\
&-2R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}+2R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}+2R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}-2R_{\alpha}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}.\end{split}$ We also have the terms involving the magnetic field: $\begin{split}nA_{\alpha}&=-\varepsilon^{-1/2}|\nabla|\Lambda_{e}^{-1}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}-\varepsilon^{-1/2}|\nabla|\Lambda_{e}^{-1}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&-\varepsilon^{-1/2}|\nabla|\Lambda_{e}^{-1}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}-\varepsilon^{-1/2}|\nabla|\Lambda_{e}^{-1}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&+\varepsilon^{-1/2}|\nabla|\Lambda_{i}^{-1}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+\varepsilon^{-1/2}|\nabla|\Lambda_{i}^{-1}R\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&+\varepsilon^{-1/2}|\nabla|\Lambda_{i}^{-1}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+\varepsilon^{-1/2}|\nabla|\Lambda_{i}^{-1}R\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha},\end{split}$ and $\begin{split}\rho A_{\alpha}&=|\nabla|\Lambda_{e}^{-1}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+|\nabla|\Lambda_{e}^{-1}R\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&+|\nabla|\Lambda_{e}^{-1}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+|\nabla|\Lambda_{e}^{-1}R\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&+|\nabla|\Lambda_{i}^{-1}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+|\nabla|\Lambda_{i}^{-1}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&+|\nabla|\Lambda_{i}^{-1}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+|\nabla|\Lambda_{i}^{-1}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha},\end{split}$ and $\begin{split}(R_{\alpha}h)\cdot A_{\alpha}=&i\varepsilon^{-1/2}R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+i\varepsilon^{-1/2}R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&-i\varepsilon^{-1/2}R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}-i\varepsilon^{-1/2}R_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&-i\varepsilon^{-1/2}RR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}-i\varepsilon^{-1/2}RR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&+i\varepsilon^{-1/2}RR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+i\varepsilon^{-1/2}RR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha},\\
\end{split}$ while $\begin{split}(R_{\alpha}g)\cdot A_{\alpha}=&-iRR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}-iRR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&+iRR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+iRR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{e-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&-iR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}-iR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i+}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&+iR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+iR_{\alpha}\widetilde{U_{i-}}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}.\end{split}$ Finally, $\begin{split}A_{\alpha}\cdot A_{\alpha}&=\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}+2\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b+}\right]_{\alpha}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}+\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}\cdot\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\left[U_{b-}\right]_{\alpha}.\end{split}$ Let $\begin{split}\mathcal{I}:=&\big\{(e+,e+),(e+,e-),(e-,e-),(i+,i+),(i+,i-),(i-,i-),(b+\alpha,b+\beta),(b+\alpha,b-\beta),(b-\alpha,b-\beta)\\
&(e+,i+),(e+,i-),(e-,i+),(e-,i-),(e+,b+\alpha),(e+,b-\alpha),(e-,b+\alpha),(e-,b-\alpha),\\
&(i+,b+\alpha),(i+,b-\alpha),(i-,b+\alpha),(i-,b-\alpha),\,1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 3\big\}.\end{split}$ (C.2) The nonlinearities $\mathcal{N}_{e},\mathcal{N}_{i},\mathcal{N}_{b}$ can be written in the form $\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{N}_{e})(\xi,t)=c\sum_{(\mu,\nu)\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m_{e;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{U_{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,t)\widehat{U_{\nu}}(\eta,t)\,d\eta,\\
&\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{N}_{i})(\xi,t)=c\sum_{(\mu,\nu)\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m_{i;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{U_{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,t)\widehat{U_{\nu}}(\eta,t)\,d\eta\\
&\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{N}_{b,\alpha})(\xi,t)=c\sum_{(\mu,\nu)\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m_{b,\alpha;\mu,\nu}(\xi,\eta)\widehat{U_{\mu}}(\xi-\eta,t)\widehat{U_{\nu}}(\eta,t)\,d\eta.\end{split}$ (C.3) Letting $\begin{split}&T_{e;e,e}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{i[\varepsilon^{-1/2}-R(\xi)R(\xi-\eta)R(\eta)]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi)^{2})(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})(1+R(\eta)^{2})}},\\
&T_{e;i,i}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{i[\varepsilon^{-1/2}R(\xi-\eta)R(\eta)-R(\xi)]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi)^{2})(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})(1+R(\eta)^{2})}},\\
&T_{e;e,i}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{i[\varepsilon^{-1/2}R(\eta)+R(\xi)R(\xi-\eta)]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi)^{2})(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})(1+R(\eta)^{2})}},\\
&T_{e;e,b}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{i[-\varepsilon^{-1}+R(\xi)R(\xi-\eta)]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi)^{2})(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})}\Lambda_{b}(\eta)},\\
&T_{e;i,b}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{i[\varepsilon^{-1}R(\xi-\eta)+R(\xi)]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi)^{2})(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})}\Lambda_{b}(\eta)},\\
\end{split}$ (C.4) the multipliers $m_{e;\mu,\nu}$ are given by $\begin{split}&m_{e;e+,e+}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;e,e}(\xi,\eta)\Big[+\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}+\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{4|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{e;e+,e-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;e,e}(\xi,\eta)\Big[-\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}+\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\eta)}-\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{e;e-,e-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;e,e}(\xi,\eta)\Big[-\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}+\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{4|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{e;i+,i+}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;i,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[+\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}+\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{4|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{e;i+,i-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;i,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[-\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}+\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}-\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{e;i-,i-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;i,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[-\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}+\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{4|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{e;e+,i+}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;e,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[-\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}-\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}-\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{e;e+,i-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;e,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[+\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}-\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}+\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{e;e-,i+}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;e,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[-\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}+\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}+\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{e;e-,i-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;e,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[+\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}+\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}-\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big].\\
\end{split}$ (C.5) We also have the magnetic terms $\begin{split}&m_{e;e+,b\pm,\alpha}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;e,b}(\xi,\eta)\Big[\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}}{|\xi|}+\frac{1}{2}|\xi|\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\alpha}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big]\\
&m_{e;e-,b\pm,\alpha}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;e,b}(\xi,\eta)\Big[\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}}{|\xi|}-\frac{1}{2}|\xi|\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\alpha}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big]\\
&m_{e;i+,b\pm,\alpha}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;i,b}(\xi,\eta)\Big[\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}}{|\xi|}+\frac{1}{2}|\xi|\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\alpha}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big]\\
&m_{e;i-,b\pm,\alpha}(\xi,\eta)=T_{e;i,b}(\xi,\eta)\Big[\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Lambda_{e}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}}{|\xi|}-\frac{1}{2}|\xi|\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\alpha}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big]\\
&m_{e;b+,\alpha,b+\beta}(\xi,\eta)=m_{e;b-,\alpha,b-,\beta}(\xi,\eta)=\frac{1}{2}m_{e;b+,\alpha,b-,\beta}(\xi,\eta)=\frac{i[\varepsilon^{-3/2}-R(\xi)]}{\sqrt{1+R(\xi)^{2}}}\frac{|\xi|}{4\Lambda_{b}(\xi-\eta)\Lambda_{b}(\eta)}\delta_{\alpha\beta}.\end{split}$ (C.6) Similarly, letting $\begin{split}&T_{i;e,e}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{i[\varepsilon^{-1/2}R(\xi)+R(\xi-\eta)R(\eta)]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi)^{2})(1+R(\eta)^{2})(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})}},\\
&T_{i;i,i}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{i[\varepsilon^{-1/2}R(\xi)R(\xi-\eta)R(\eta)+1]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi)^{2})(1+R(\eta)^{2})(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})}},\\
&T_{i;e,i}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{i[\varepsilon^{-1/2}R(\xi)R(\eta)-R(\xi-\eta)]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi)^{2})(1+R(\eta)^{2})(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})}},\\
&T_{i;e,b}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{i[\varepsilon^{-1}R(\xi)+R(\xi-\eta)]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi)^{2})(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2}}\Lambda_{b}(\eta)},\\
&T_{i;i,b}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{-i[\varepsilon^{-1}R(\xi)R(\xi-\eta)-1]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi)^{2})(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})}\Lambda_{b}(\eta)},\\
&T_{i;b,b}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{-i[\varepsilon^{-3/2}R(\xi)+1]}{\sqrt{1+R(\xi)^{2}}\Lambda_{b}(\xi-\eta)\Lambda_{b}(\eta)},\end{split}$ (C.7) the multipliers $m_{i;\mu,\nu}$ are given by $\begin{split}&m_{i;e+,e+}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;e,e}(\xi,\eta)\Big[-\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}-\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{4|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;e+,e-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;e,e}(\xi,\eta)\Big[+\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}-\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\eta)}+\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;e-,e-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;e,e}(\xi,\eta)\Big[+\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}-\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{4|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;i+,i+}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;i,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[-\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}-\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{4|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;i+,i-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;i,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[+\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}-\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}+\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;i-,i-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;i,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[+\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}-\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{4|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;e+,i+}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;e,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[+\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}+\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}+\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;e+,i-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;e,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[-\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}+\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}-\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;e-,i+}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;e,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[+\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}-\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}-\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;e-,i-}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;e,i}(\xi,\eta)\Big[-\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|(\xi\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi||\eta|\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}-\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\eta|(\xi\cdot(\xi-\eta))}{2|\xi||\xi-\eta|\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}+\frac{|\xi|((\xi-\eta)\cdot\eta)}{2|\xi-\eta||\eta|}\Big].\end{split}$ (C.8) Again, the magnetic terms give $\begin{split}&m_{i;e+,b\pm,\alpha}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;e,b}(\xi,\eta)\Big[\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}}{|\xi|}+\frac{1}{2}|\xi|\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\alpha}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;e-,b\pm,\alpha}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;e,b}(\xi,\eta)\Big[\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}}{|\xi|}-\frac{1}{2}|\xi|\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\alpha}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;i+,b\pm,\alpha}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;i,b}(\xi,\eta)\Big[\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}}{|\xi|}+\frac{1}{2}|\xi|\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\alpha}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;i-,b\pm,\alpha}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;i,b}(\xi,\eta)\Big[\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Lambda_{i}(\xi)|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}}{|\xi|}-\frac{1}{2}|\xi|\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\alpha}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big],\\
&m_{i;b+,\alpha,b+,\beta}(\xi,\eta)=m_{i;b-,\alpha,b-,\beta}(\xi,\eta)=\frac{1}{2}m_{i;b+,\alpha,b-,\beta}(\xi,\eta)=T_{i;b,b}(\xi,\eta)\frac{|\xi|}{4}\delta_{\alpha\beta}.\end{split}$ (C.9) Finally, we can decompose $\mathcal{N}_{b}$ . Letting $\begin{split}&T_{b;e,e}^{\alpha,\beta}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{iQ^{2}_{\alpha,\beta}(\xi)\left[-\varepsilon^{-1}+R(\xi-\eta)R(\eta)\right]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})(1+R(\eta)^{2})}}\\
&T_{b;i,i}^{\alpha,\beta}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{iQ^{2}_{\alpha,\beta}(\xi)\left[-\varepsilon^{-1}R(\xi-\eta)R(\eta)+1\right]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})(1+R(\eta)^{2})}}\\
&T_{b;e,i}^{\alpha,\beta}(\xi,\eta):=\frac{iQ^{2}_{\alpha,\beta}(\xi)\left[\varepsilon^{-1}R(\eta)+R(\xi-\eta)\right]}{\sqrt{(1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2})(1+R(\eta)^{2})}}\\
\end{split}$ (C.10) we get that $\begin{split}&m_{b,\alpha;e+,e+}(\xi,\eta)=T^{\alpha,\beta}_{b;e,e}(\xi,\eta)\frac{1}{2}\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\eta_{\beta}}{|\eta|}\\
&m_{b,\alpha;e+,e-}(\xi,\eta)=T^{\alpha,\beta}_{b;e,e}(\xi,\eta)\frac{1}{2}\Big[-\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\eta_{\beta}}{|\eta|}+\frac{|\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\eta)}\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\beta}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big]\\
&m_{b,\alpha;e-,e-}(\xi,\eta)=-T^{\alpha,\beta}_{b;e,e}(\xi,\eta)\frac{1}{2}\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\eta_{\beta}}{|\eta|}\\
&m_{b,\alpha;i+,i+}(\xi,\eta)=T^{\alpha,\beta}_{b;i,i}(\xi,\eta)\frac{1}{2}\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\eta_{\beta}}{|\eta|}\\
&m_{b,\alpha;i+,i-}(\xi,\eta)=-T^{\alpha,\beta}_{b;i,i}(\xi,\eta)\frac{1}{2}\Big[\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\xi\eta)}\frac{\eta_{\beta}}{|\eta|}-\frac{|\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\beta}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big]\\
&m_{b,\alpha;i-,i-}(\xi,\eta)=-T^{\alpha,\beta}_{b;i,i}(\xi,\eta)\frac{1}{2}\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\eta_{\beta}}{|\eta|}\\
&m_{b,\alpha;e+,i+}(\xi,\eta)=T^{\alpha,\beta}_{b;i,e}(\xi,\eta)\frac{1}{2}\Big[\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\eta_{\beta}}{|\eta|}+\frac{|\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\beta}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big]\\
&m_{b,\alpha;e+,i-}(\xi,\eta)=T^{\alpha,\beta}_{b;i,e}(\xi,\eta)\frac{1}{2}\Big[-\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\eta_{\beta}}{|\eta|}+\frac{|\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\beta}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big]\\
&m_{b,\alpha;e-,i+}(\xi,\eta)=T^{\alpha,\beta}_{b;i,e}(\xi,\eta)\frac{1}{2}\Big[\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\eta_{\beta}}{|\eta|}-\frac{|\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\beta}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big]\\
&m_{b,\alpha;e-,i-}(\xi,\eta)=-T^{\alpha,\beta}_{b;i,e}(\xi,\eta)\frac{1}{2}\Big[\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)}\frac{\eta_{\beta}}{|\eta|}+\frac{|\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\eta)}\frac{(\xi-\eta)_{\beta}}{|\xi-\eta|}\Big].\end{split}$ (C.11) Finally, we add the magnetic terms $\begin{split}&m_{b,\alpha;e\pm,b\pm,\beta}(\xi,\eta)=\frac{iQ^{2}_{\alpha,\beta}(\xi)\left[\varepsilon^{-3/2}-R(\xi-\eta)\right]}{2\sqrt{1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2}}}\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{e}(\xi-\eta)\Lambda_{b}(\eta)}\\
&m_{b,\alpha;i\pm,b\pm,\beta}(\xi,\eta)=-\frac{iQ^{2}_{\alpha,\beta}(\xi)\left[\varepsilon^{-3/2}R(\xi-\eta)+1\right]}{2\sqrt{1+R(\xi-\eta)^{2}}}\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\Lambda_{i}(\xi-\eta)\Lambda_{b}(\eta)}.\end{split}$ (C.12) Appendix D Other models The purpose of this section is to show how the results in the main body of this work extend to several other models. In particular we focus on two variants of ( 1.1 ) which are invariant under appropriate change of frame: the Euler-Poisson is invariant under Galilean transformation, while the relativistic model is invariant under Lorentz transformations. D.1. The Euler-Poisson model Formally taking $c\rightarrow\infty$ and $B\equiv 0$ in ( 1.1 ), we derive the Euler-Poisson system in the two-fluid theory, which is relevant when the magnetic forces can be neglected: $\begin{split}\partial_{t}n_{e}+\hbox{div}(n_{e}v_{e})&=0,\\
n_{e}m_{e}\left[\partial_{t}v_{e}+v_{e}\cdot\nabla v_{e}\right]+\nabla p_{e}&=n_{e}e\nabla\phi,\\
\partial_{t}n_{i}+\hbox{div}(n_{i}v_{i})&=0,\\
n_{i}M_{i}\left[\partial_{t}v_{i}+v_{i}\cdot\nabla v_{i}\right]+\nabla p_{i}&=-Zn_{i}e\nabla\phi,\\
-\Delta\phi&=4\pi e(Zn_{i}-n_{e}).\end{split}$ (D.1) Here the electromagnetic fields are given by $E=-\nabla\phi,\quad B=0.$ Galilean invariance. This system is left invariant by Galilean change of unknowns. More precisely, let $V\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$ be a fixed vector and let $\begin{split}&x\to x^{\prime}:=x+Vt,\quad t\to t^{\prime}:=t\\
&n_{e}\to n_{e}^{\prime}:=n_{e},\quad v_{e}\to v_{e}^{\prime}:=v_{e}-V,\quad n_{i}\to n_{i}^{\prime}:=n_{i},\quad v_{i}\to v_{i}^{\prime}:=v_{i}-V,\quad\phi\to\phi^{\prime}:=\phi,\end{split}$ then, we observe that $(n_{e},v_{e},n_{i},v_{i},\phi)(x,t)$ solves ( D.1 ) if and only if $(n_{e}^{\prime},v_{e}^{\prime},n_{i}^{\prime},v_{i}^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})(x^{\prime},t^{\prime})$ does. Arbitrary pressures. 13 13 13 The analysis of the arbitrary pressure here carries over directly to the Euler-Maxwell case in ( 1.1 ). We consider arbitrary smooth pressure laws of the form $p_{i}=p_{i}(n_{i}),\quad p_{e}=p_{e}(n_{e}),\quad\text{ with}\quad p_{e}^{\prime}(n_{0})>0\hbox{ and }p_{i}^{\prime}(n_{0}/Z)>0.$ (D.2) Defining $P_{i}=Zp_{i}^{\prime}(n_{0}/Z)/n_{0},\quad P_{e}=p_{e}^{\prime}(n_{0})/n_{0},$ and using the rescaling ( 1.7 ), we have the Taylor expansions 14 14 14 The enthalpies given by $h_{e}^{\prime}(x)=p_{e}^{\prime}(x)/x$ and $h_{i}^{\prime}(x)=p_{i}^{\prime}(x)/x$ are also important for the relativistic model later on. The functions $q_{i}$ and $q_{e}$ are essentially the internal specific energies , $\epsilon_{i}=n_{i}h_{i}-p_{i}$ and $\epsilon_{e}=n_{e}h_{e}-p_{e}$ . $\frac{p_{i}^{\prime}(n_{i})}{n_{i}}=P_{i}\left[1+P_{i}^{1}\rho+\rho^{2}\cdot c_{i}(\rho)\right]:=P_{i}q_{i}^{\prime}(\rho)\nabla\rho,\quad\frac{p_{e}^{\prime}(n_{e})}{n_{e}}=P_{i}\left[T+P_{e}^{1}n+n^{2}\cdot c_{e}(n)\right]:=P_{i}q_{e}^{\prime}(n)\nabla n,$ (D.3) for some constants $P_{i}^{1},P_{e}^{1}$ and some smooth functions $c_{i}$ and $c_{e}$ . After rescaling as in ( 1.7 ), this gives the new system $\displaystyle\partial_{t}n+\hbox{div}((n+1)v)$ $\displaystyle=$ $\displaystyle 0,$ (D.4) $\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(\partial_{t}v+v\cdot\nabla v\right)+T\nabla n-\nabla\phi$ $\displaystyle+$ $\displaystyle P_{e}^{1}n\nabla n+n^{2}c_{e}(n)\nabla n$ $\displaystyle=$ $\displaystyle 0,$ (D.5) $\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho+\hbox{div}((\rho+1)u)$ $\displaystyle=$ $\displaystyle 0,$ (D.6) $\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla u\right)+\nabla\rho+\nabla\phi$ $\displaystyle+$ $\displaystyle P_{i}^{1}\rho\nabla\rho+\rho^{2}c_{i}(\rho)\nabla\rho$ $\displaystyle=$ $\displaystyle 0,$ (D.7) $\displaystyle-\Delta\phi-\rho+n$ $\displaystyle=$ $\displaystyle 0,$ (D.8) The analysis developed in the main body of this work applies directly to the Euler-Poisson system and gives: Theorem D.1 . Assume $\varepsilon\leq 10^{-3}$ and $T\in[1/10,100]$ . Let $N_{0}=10^{4}$ and assume that $\begin{split}&\|(n^{0},v^{0},\rho^{0},u^{0})\|_{H^{N_{0}}}+\|(n^{0},v^{0},\rho^{0},u^{0})\|_{Z}=\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta},\\
&\nabla\times v^{0}=\nabla\times u^{0}=0,\end{split}$ (D.9) where $\overline{\delta}=\overline{\delta}(T,\varepsilon)>0$ is sufficiently small, and the $Z$ norm is defined in Definition 4.1 . Then there exists a unique global solution $(n,v,\rho,u)\in C([0,\infty):{H^{N_{0}}})$ of the system ( D.4 )–( D.8 ) with initial data $(n(0),v(0),\rho(0),u(0))=(n^{0},v^{0},\rho^{0},u^{0})$ . Moreover, for any $t\in[0,\infty)$ , $\nabla\times v(t)=\nabla\times u(t)\equiv 0,\text{ (irrotationality) }$ (D.10) and, with $\beta:=1/100$ , $\begin{split}&\|(n(t),v(t),\rho(t),u(t))\|_{H^{N_{0}}}+\sup_{|\alpha|\leq 4}(1+t)^{1+\beta/2}\|D_{x}^{\alpha}(n(t),v(t),\rho(t),u(t))\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\delta_{0}.\end{split}$ (D.11) D.1.1. Derivation and main steps of the proof. The proof of this theorem is very similar to the proof of Theorem 1.1 . Proposition 2.1 remains essentially unchanged using the energy $\mathcal{E}_{N}:=\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big[q_{e}^{\prime}(n)|D_{x}^{\gamma}n|^{2}+\varepsilon(1+n)|D_{x}^{\gamma}v|^{2}+q_{i}^{\prime}(\rho)|D_{x}^{\gamma}\rho|^{2}+(\rho+1)|D_{x}^{\gamma}u|^{2}+|D_{x}^{\gamma}\nabla\phi|^{2}\big]\,dx.$ Note that $q_{e}^{\prime}>0$ and $q_{i}^{\prime}>0$ for small perturbations, as follows from ( D.2 ) and ( D.3 ) and that $q_{e}^{\prime}(0)=T$ , $q_{i}^{\prime}(0)=1$ .
This gives local existence of smooth solutions.
Using that $\begin{split}\partial_{t}\left[\nabla\times v\right]&=\nabla\times\left[v\times\left[\nabla\times v\right]\right]\\
\partial_{t}\left[\nabla\times u\right]&=\nabla\times\left[u\times\left[\nabla\times u\right]\right]\end{split}$ we see that smooth solutions with irrotational initial data remain irrotational for all time. We can therefore write $v_{\alpha}=R_{\alpha}h,\,\,u_{\alpha}=R_{\alpha}g,\quad\hbox{or}\quad h:=-|\nabla|^{-1}\hbox{div}(v),\,\,g:=-|\nabla|^{-1}\hbox{div}(u)$ and we then see that ( D.4 )-( D.8 ) becomes $\begin{split}\partial_{t}n-|\nabla|h&=-\partial_{\alpha}\left[nR_{\alpha}h\right],\\
\partial_{t}\rho-|\nabla|g&=-\partial_{\alpha}\left[\rho R_{\alpha}g\right],\\
\partial_{t}h+|\nabla|^{-1}H_{\varepsilon}^{2}n-\varepsilon^{-1}|\nabla|^{-1}\rho&=-(1/2)|\nabla|\left[R_{\alpha}hR_{\alpha}h\right]-|\nabla|\left[(P_{e}^{1}/2)n^{2}+n^{2}C_{e}(n)\right],\\
\partial_{t}g-|\nabla|^{-1}n+|\nabla|^{-1}H_{1}^{2}\rho&=-(1/2)|\nabla|\left[R_{\alpha}gR_{\alpha}g\right]-|\nabla|\left[(P_{i}^{1}/2)\rho^{2}+\rho^{2}C_{i}(\rho)\right],\\
\end{split}$ (D.12) where $H_{1}$ and $H_{\varepsilon}$ are given in ( 3.1 ) and $C_{i}$ and $C_{e}$ are smooth functions related to $c_{i}$ and $c_{e}$ by $C_{i}(t)=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}s^{2}c_{i}(s)ds,\quad C_{e}(t)=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}s^{2}c_{e}(s)ds.$ In particular, they satisfy that $C_{i}(0)=C_{e}(0)=0$ . Defining $U_{e}$ and $U_{i}$ as in ( 3.8 ), we can recast this as $\begin{cases}\left(\partial_{t}+i\Lambda_{e}\right)U_{e}&=\mathcal{N}_{e}+\mathcal{N}_{e}^{\prime}\\
\left(\partial_{t}+i\Lambda_{i}\right)U_{i}&=\mathcal{N}_{i}+\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\prime}\end{cases}$ where (recall that $R$ is defined in ( 3.1 )) $\begin{split}&\mathcal{N}_{e}=\frac{\Lambda_{e}R_{\alpha}}{2\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}\varepsilon^{1/2}(nR_{\alpha}h)+i\left\{\frac{|\nabla|}{4\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}\varepsilon^{1/2}R_{\alpha}h\cdot R_{\alpha}h-R[R_{\alpha}g\cdot R_{\alpha}g]\right\},\\
&\mathcal{N}_{i}=\frac{-\Lambda_{i}R_{\alpha}}{2\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}\big[\varepsilon^{1/2}R(nR_{\alpha}h)+(\rho R_{\alpha}g)+i\left\{\frac{-|\nabla|}{4\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}\big[\varepsilon^{1/2}R[R_{\alpha}h\cdot R_{\alpha}h]+R_{\alpha}g\cdot R_{\alpha}g\right\}.\\
\end{split}$ (D.13) contains nonlinear terms already present in ( 1.1 ) and $\begin{split}\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\prime}&=-i\frac{|\nabla|}{4\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}\left[\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}P_{e}^{1}R(n\cdot n)+P_{i}^{1}\rho\cdot\rho\right]-i\frac{|\nabla|}{2\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}\left[\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}R[n\cdot n\cdot C_{e}(n)]+\rho\cdot\rho\cdot C_{i}(\rho)\right],\\
\mathcal{N}_{e}^{\prime}&=i\frac{|\nabla|}{4\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}\left[\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}P_{e}^{1}n\cdot n-P_{i}^{1}R(\rho\cdot\rho)\right]+i\frac{|\nabla|}{2\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}\left[\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}n\cdot n\cdot C_{e}(n)-R[\rho\cdot\rho\cdot C_{i}(\rho)]\right],\\
\end{split}$ (D.14) is a new nonlinearities coming from the new pressure. Since the formulas in ( D.14 ) involve Fourier multipliers with $\mathcal{S}^{100}$ Fourier symbols, we see that all the additional quadratic symbols are of the form given in ( 3.26 ). In addition, we have that the cubic terms are of the form $\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}^{3}(\xi,t)=\sum_{\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0},j\in\{1,2\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m^{3}_{\sigma;\mu,\nu,j}(\xi-\eta,\eta,\chi)\widehat{U}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta,t)\widehat{U}_{\nu}(\eta-\chi,t)\widehat{C_{j}}(\chi,t)d\eta d\chi$ where $m^{3}_{\sigma;\mu,\nu,j}(\xi,\eta,\chi)=|\xi|\cdot m(\xi-\eta,\eta,\chi)$ for some $m(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})$ a linear combination of functions of the form ( D.38 ) and $C_{i}=C_{i}(\rho),\quad C_{e}=C_{e}(\rho)$ satisfy ( D.39 ) in view of Lemma D.5 below. Thus, the new terms in the nonlinearity fall under the scope of Proposition 5.1 or under the scope of Proposition D.4 and can be handled. D.2. Relativistic model We refer to [ 6 , 20 , 25 ] for other references about relativistic fluids. D.2.1. Relativistic Euler-Maxwell model for the electron As a starter, we introduce a simpler one-fluid relativistic Euler-Maxwell model, namely the model for the electrons. This is the relativistic counterpart of the (classical) Euler-Maxwell model for electrons already discussed in [ 14 , 29 ] . We consider Minkowski space $(\mathbb{R}^{1+3},g_{\alpha\beta})$ with $g_{00}=-c^{2}$ , $g_{ij}=\delta_{ij}$ and $g_{0j}=g_{j0}=0$ . Its inverse is denoted $g^{\mu\nu}$ where $g^{00}=-c^{-2}$ , $g^{ij}=\delta_{ij}$ and $g^{0j}=0=g^{j0}$ . We use the Einstein convention that repeated up-down indices be summed and we raise and lower indices using the metric. Latin indices $i,j\dots$ vary from $1$ to $3$ , while greek indices $\mu,\nu\dots$ vary from $0$ to $3$ . We denote $\mathcal{T}^{d}(\mathbb{R}^{1+3})$ the set of contravariant $d$ -tensors on the Minkowski space.
We model the electron fluid by a scalar function $n_{e}\in\mathcal{T}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{1+3})$ and a velocity function $u=(u^{\alpha})_{0\leq\alpha\leq 3}\in\mathcal{T}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{1+3})$ normalized by 15 15 15 Some authors prefer to normalize the velocity of a particle of rest-mass $m$ by asking that $u^{\alpha}u_{\alpha}=-mc^{2}$ . Here, we prefer to incorporate the mass separately. $u^{\alpha}u_{\alpha}=-c^{2}.$ (D.15) Below, we will always note $\gamma_{e}=u^{0}$ so that $u^{\nu}=(\gamma_{e},v^{1},v^{2},v^{3})$ with $\gamma_{e}=\sqrt{1+c^{-2}|v|^{2}}$ . We will repeatedly switch between $u$ and $v$ as one uniquely defines the other thanks to ( D.15 ). In addition, we also consider an electromagnetic field $F=\{F^{\mu\nu}\}_{0\leq\mu,\nu\leq 3}\in\mathcal{T}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{1+3})$ . We assume that this field is skew-symmetric: $F^{\mu\nu}=-F^{\nu\mu}$ . Finally, we also assume the presence of a uniform flat positively charged background of density of charge $n_{0}e$ and four-velocity $\partial_{t}=(1,0,0,0)\in\mathcal{T}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{1+3})$ . We can introduce the energy-momentum tensor associated to the fluid under consideration: $T^{\mu\nu}=nh\frac{u^{\mu}u^{\nu}}{c^{2}}+pg^{\mu\nu}\in\mathcal{T}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{1+3}),$ where $p=p(n)$ is a function satisfying assumptions as in ( D.2 ) and $h=h(n)$ , the specific enthalpy is a function satisfying $h^{\prime}(x)=\frac{p^{\prime}(x)}{x},\quad h(0)=m_{e}c^{2},$ (D.16) where $m_{e}$ is the rest-mass of an electron. We can also consider the energy-momentum tensor of the electromagnetic field: $\mathcal{E}^{\mu\nu}=-(4\pi)^{-1}\left[F^{\mu\alpha}F^{\beta\nu}g_{\alpha\beta}+\frac{1}{4}F^{\alpha\beta}F_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\right]\in\mathcal{T}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{1+3}).$ The dynamics are then given by three equations: the Maxwell equations, the continuity of matter and the balance of energy-momentum. The Maxwell equations give: $\begin{split}\partial_{\mu}F^{\mu\nu}=\frac{4\pi}{c}J^{\nu}\qquad\partial_{\alpha}F_{\beta\gamma}+\partial_{\beta}F_{\gamma\alpha}+\partial_{\gamma}F_{\alpha\beta}=0.\end{split}$ (D.17) where the total relativistic current is defined by $J^{\nu}=en_{0}\partial_{t}^{\nu}-enu^{\nu}.$ (D.18) The continuity of matter gives $\partial_{\nu}(nu^{\nu})=0.$ (D.19) The balance of Energy-momentum is then $\begin{split}\partial_{\nu}T^{\mu\nu}=\frac{en}{c}u_{\alpha}F^{\mu\alpha}.\end{split}$ After using ( D.19 ), this reduces to $\frac{nu^{\nu}}{c^{2}}\partial_{\nu}\left[hu^{\mu}\right]+g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}p=\frac{en}{c}u_{\alpha}F^{\mu\alpha}.$ (D.20) Projecting onto the direction of $u$ gives $-nu^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}h+u^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}p=0$ which is always satisfied. Therefore, ( D.20 ) only contains three nontrivial equations which can be obtained by projecting onto $\mathbb{R}^{3}$ , the orthogonal of $\partial_{t}$ . Lorentz Covariance. Consider a Lorentz-transformation $L$ , i.e. a (fixed) $2$ -tensor $L$ satisfying $L_{\alpha\beta}L^{\alpha\gamma}=\delta_{\beta}^{\gamma}$ and define $\begin{split}&(X^{\prime})^{\alpha}=L^{\alpha\beta}X_{\beta},\quad n^{\prime}(X^{\prime})=n(X),\quad(u^{\prime})^{\alpha}(X^{\prime})=L^{\alpha\beta}u_{\beta}(X),\quad(F^{\prime})^{\alpha\beta}(X^{\prime})=L^{\alpha\gamma}L^{\beta\delta}F_{\gamma\delta}(X),\\
&(J^{\prime})^{\alpha}(X^{\prime})=L^{\alpha\beta}J_{\beta}(X)\end{split}$ Then, we see that $(n,u,J,F)$ satisfy ( D.17 )-( D.19 )-( D.2.1 ) if and only if $(n^{\prime},u^{\prime},J^{\prime},F^{\prime})$ does. Irrotational flows. We introduce the (generalized) vorticity defined by $\omega_{\alpha\beta}=\partial_{\alpha}(hu_{\beta})-\partial_{\beta}(hu_{\alpha})+ecF_{\alpha\beta}.$ This is transported by the flow in the following sense: $u^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}\omega_{\alpha\beta}=(\partial_{\alpha}u^{\nu})\omega_{\beta\nu}-(\partial_{\beta}u^{\nu})\omega_{\alpha\nu}.$ (D.21) Indeed, we may simply compute $\begin{split}u^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}\omega_{\alpha\beta}&=\partial_{\alpha}(u^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}(hu_{\beta}))-\partial_{\nu}(hu_{\beta})\partial_{\alpha}u^{\nu}-\partial_{\beta}(u^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}(hu_{\alpha}))+\partial_{\nu}(hu_{\alpha})\partial_{\beta}u^{\nu}+ecu^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}F_{\alpha\beta}\\
&=-\partial_{\alpha}(\frac{c^{2}}{n}\partial_{\beta}p-ceu_{\theta}F_{\beta\gamma}g^{\gamma\theta})+\partial_{\beta}(\frac{c^{2}}{n}\partial_{\alpha}p-ceu_{\theta}F_{\alpha\gamma}g^{\gamma\theta})-ecu^{\nu}(\partial_{\alpha}F_{\beta\nu}+\partial_{\beta}F_{\nu\alpha})\\
&\quad-(\partial_{\alpha}u^{\nu})\omega_{\nu\beta}-(\partial_{\alpha}u^{\nu})\partial_{\beta}(hu_{\nu})+ec(\partial_{\alpha}u^{\nu})F_{\nu\beta}+(\partial_{\beta}u^{\nu})\omega_{\nu\alpha}+(\partial_{\beta}u^{\nu})\partial_{\alpha}(hu_{\nu})-ec(\partial_{\beta}u^{\nu})F_{\nu\alpha}\\
&=ec\{\partial_{\alpha}(u^{\theta}F_{\beta\theta})-\partial_{\beta}(u^{\theta}F_{\alpha\theta})-u^{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}F_{\beta\nu}-u^{\nu}\partial_{\beta}F_{\nu\alpha}+(\partial_{\alpha}u^{\nu})F_{\nu\beta}-(\partial_{\beta}u^{\nu})F_{\nu\alpha}\}\\
&\quad-(\partial_{\alpha}u^{\nu})\omega_{\nu\beta}+(\partial_{\beta}u^{\nu})\omega_{\nu\alpha}-\{(\partial_{\alpha}u^{\nu})\partial_{\beta}(hu_{\nu})-(\partial_{\beta}u^{\nu})\partial_{\alpha}(hu_{\nu})\}\\
&=-(\partial_{\alpha}u^{\nu})\omega_{\nu\beta}+(\partial_{\beta}u^{\nu})\omega_{\nu\alpha},\\
\end{split}$ and hence as long as the solution is smooth irrotational initial data lead to solutions which remain irrotational. New Unknowns. We assume that the data are irrotational and we consider the unknowns 16 16 16 This choice of unknown is motivated from the choice of unknowns in the non relativistic case [ 29 ] . $\begin{split}\mu_{e}^{j}=c^{-2}hu^{j}=c^{-2}hv^{j},\quad E^{j}=ecF^{j0}.\end{split}$ (D.22) All the other unknowns can be recovered from the formula $ec^{-1}F^{jk}=\partial_{k}\mu_{e}^{j}-\partial_{j}\mu_{e}^{k},$ the first equation in ( D.17 ) $n\gamma_{e}=n_{0}-\frac{1}{4\pi e^{2}}\partial_{j}E^{j},$ and from the fact that the mapping $D:(n,v)\mapsto(n\gamma_{e},\mu_{e})=(n\sqrt{1+c^{-2}|v|^{2}},c^{-2}h(n)v)$ is invertible in an $L^{\infty}$ -neighborhood of $v\equiv 0$ and $n\equiv n_{0}$ , where $h\simeq h_{0}:=h(n_{0})$ . This latter point is easily seen from the Jacobian matrix $\nabla D=\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{e}&\gamma_{e}^{-1}c^{-2}nv^{T}\\
c^{-2}h^{\prime}(n)v&c^{-2}h(n)I_{3}\end{pmatrix},$ which also in particular implies that $\begin{split}\partial_{j}h=-\frac{h^{\prime}(n_{0})}{4\pi e^{2}}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}E^{k}+h.o.t.\end{split}$ (D.23) The dynamical equations then reduce to the following (from ( D.20 ) and the first equation of ( D.17 )) $\begin{split}\partial_{t}\mu_{e}^{j}+E^{j}+\frac{1}{\gamma}\partial_{j}h+\frac{c^{2}}{h\gamma_{e}}\partial_{j}\frac{|\mu_{e}|^{2}}{2}&=0\\
\partial_{t}E^{j}-c^{2}(\hbox{curl}\,\hbox{curl}(\mu_{e}))^{j}-4\pi e^{2}c^{2}\frac{n}{h}\mu_{e}^{j}&=0,\end{split}$ (D.24) where $(\hbox{curl}(v))^{i}:=\in^{ijk}\partial_{j}v_{k}.$ We can now choose scales appropriately so as to minimize the number of parameters in the linear system. Define 17 17 17 Here $\omega_{e}$ denotes the (nonrelativist) electron plasma frequency and $c_{s}$ denotes the (non relativist) sound velocity. $\begin{split}&\lambda=\sqrt{\frac{4\pi e^{2}n_{0}}{h_{0}}}\approx c\omega_{e},\qquad T=\frac{p_{0}^{\prime}}{h_{0}}\approx\left(\frac{c_{s}}{c}\right)^{2},\quad p_{0}^{\prime}=p^{\prime}(n_{0}),\quad\beta=\lambda c\\
&\mu_{e}(x,t)=\widetilde{\mu}(\lambda x,\beta t),\quad E(x,t)=\beta\widetilde{E}(\lambda x,\beta t),\\
&n=n_{0}(1+\widetilde{n}(\lambda x,\beta t)),\quad h_{0}\widetilde{h}=h(\widetilde{n}),\quad\widetilde{\gamma}=\sqrt{1+(c/h_{0})^{2}\widetilde{h}^{-2}|\widetilde{\mu}|^{2}}\\
\end{split}$ and introduce $Q=|\nabla|^{-1}\hbox{curl},\quad P=-\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\hbox{div},\quad P^{2}+Q^{2}=Id,\quad PQ=0,\quad P^{2}=P,\quad Q^{3}=Q.$ We can recast ( D.24 ) as $\begin{split}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{E}-T\Delta P\widetilde{E}&=\mathcal{N}_{1}\\
\partial_{t}\widetilde{E}+\Delta Q^{2}\widetilde{\mu}-\widetilde{\mu}&=\mathcal{N}_{2},\end{split}$ where $\begin{split}-\mathcal{N}_{1}&=\frac{c}{h_{0}}\nabla\frac{|\widetilde{\mu}|^{2}}{2}+\left\{T\Delta P\widetilde{E}+\frac{h_{0}}{\widetilde{\gamma}}\nabla\widetilde{h}\right\}+\frac{c}{h_{0}}\left\{\frac{1}{\widetilde{h}\widetilde{\gamma}}-1\right\}\nabla\frac{|\widetilde{\mu}|^{2}}{2}\\
-\mathcal{N}_{2}&=\left\{1-\frac{1+\widetilde{n}}{\widetilde{h}}\right\}\widetilde{\mu}^{j}.\end{split}$ We define $\begin{split}\Lambda_{e}^{2}:=1-T\Delta,\qquad\Lambda_{b}^{2}&:=1-\Delta\end{split}$ and we introduce the dispersive unknowns $\begin{split}U_{e}&:=P\widetilde{\mu}-i\Lambda_{e}P\widetilde{E}\\
U_{b}&:=Q\widetilde{\mu}-i\Lambda_{b}^{-1}Q\widetilde{E}\end{split}$ (D.25) which satisfy the system $\begin{split}\left(\partial_{t}+i\Lambda_{e}\right)U_{e}&=P\mathcal{N}_{1}-i\Lambda_{e}P\mathcal{N}_{2}\\
\left(\partial_{t}+i\Lambda_{b}\right)U_{b}&=Q\mathcal{N}_{1}-i\Lambda_{b}^{-1}Q\mathcal{N}_{2}.\end{split}$ (D.26) Now, it suffices to remark that $(\widetilde{E},\widetilde{h},\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{\gamma})$ are smooth invertible functions of $U_{e}$ and $U_{b}$ to see that the nonlinearities in ( D.26 ) correspond to a quadratic form that can be treated using the same techniques as in [ 29 ] plus a cubic term that can be treated using techniques similar to those of Subsection D.3 . This yields the following theorem: Theorem D.2 . Fix $h$ , $c$ , $T$ . Let $N_{0}=10^{4}$ and consider $\nu_{0}=n_{0}(1+\rho_{0})$ with $(\rho_{0},v_{0},F_{0})\in H^{N_{0}+1}$ satisfy $\partial_{j}F^{j0}_{0}=-4\pi c^{-1}\nu_{0}\sqrt{1+c^{-2}|v_{0}|^{2}}-n_{0},\qquad ec^{-1}F^{jk}_{0}=\partial_{k}(h(\nu_{0})v_{0}^{j})-\partial_{j}(h(\nu_{0})v_{0}^{k}),$ and $\begin{split}&\|(v_{0},F_{0})\|_{H^{N_{0}+2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|((1-\Delta)v_{0}^{j},(1-\Delta)F_{0}^{j0})\|_{Z}=\varepsilon_{0}\leq\overline{\varepsilon},\\
&\end{split}$ where $\overline{\varepsilon}>0$ is sufficiently small, and the $Z$ norm is defined in Definition 4.1 . Then there exists a unique global solution $(n,u,F)$ with $(n-n_{0},v,F)\in C([0,\infty):H^{N_{0}+1})$ of the system ( D.17 )-( D.18 )-( D.19 )-( D.20 ) with initial data $(n(0),v(0),F(0))=(\nu_{0},v_{0},F_{0})$ . Moreover, $\partial_{j}F^{j0}(t)=-4\pi c^{-1}[n(t)\sqrt{1+c^{-2}|v(t)|^{2}}-n_{0}],\quad ec^{-1}F_{jk}=\partial_{k}(hv_{j})-\partial_{j}(hv_{k}),\quad\text{ for any }t\in[0,\infty),$ and, with $\beta=1/100$ , $\sup_{t\in[0,\infty)}\|(v(t),F(t))\|_{H^{N_{0}+1}}+\sup_{t\in[0,\infty)}\sup_{|\rho|\leq 4}(1+t)^{1+\beta}\|(D^{\rho}_{x}v(t),D^{\rho}_{x}F(t))\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}.$ The proof follows the same strategy as in [ 29 ] . To find the high-order energies, we start from the conservation of energy: $\partial_{\nu}\left[T^{0\nu}+\mathcal{E}^{0\nu}\right]=0$ which implies that $\mathcal{E}_{0}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left[T^{00}+\mathcal{E}^{00}\right]dx$ is conserved. We then define the high order energies by $\mathcal{E}_{N}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left[h^{\prime}(n)|D^{N}n|^{2}+\frac{nh}{c^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}|D^{N}v^{j}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4\pi e^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}|D^{N}E^{j}|^{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{8\pi}\sum_{j,k=1}^{3}|D^{N}F^{jk}|^{2}\right]dx,$ from the Hessian of $\mathcal{E}_{0}$ . The semilinear analysis is a direct adaptation of [ 29 , Section 3 and 4] . Once we remark that $U_{e}(0)$ and $U_{b}(0)$ defined in terms of $(v_{0},F_{0})$ and $h$ as above satisfy $\|(U_{e}(0),U_{b}(0))\|_{H^{N_{0}+1}}+\|(U_{e}(0),U_{b}(0))\|_{Z}\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}},$ and ( D.26 ). D.2.2. Relativistic Euler-Maxwell model for two fluids Now we consider the relativistic analogue of ( 1.1 ). We consider again the standard Minkowski space $(\mathbb{R}^{1+3},g)$ defined as in the previous subsection. The main unknowns are two densities $n_{i}$ and $n_{e}$ , two velocity fields $v_{i}$ and $v_{e}$ (both of which satisfy ( D.15 )) and an electromagnetic field $F$ . We are also given pressure laws $p_{i}$ and $p_{e}$ and enthalpies $h_{i}$ and $h_{e}$ satisfying ( D.16 ), with $M_{i}$ , the rest-mass of an ion instead of $m_{e}$ for $p_{i}$ , $h_{i}$ . The Maxwell equations ( D.17 ) remain the same, with the relativistic current now defined as $J^{\nu}=Zen_{i}u_{i}^{\nu}-en_{e}u_{e}^{\nu}$ (D.27) instead of ( D.18 ). Both species are independently conserved so that $\partial_{\nu}(n_{i}u_{i}^{\nu})=0=\partial_{\nu}(n_{e}u_{e}^{\nu})$ (D.28) and we have two forms of balance of momentum: $\begin{split}\frac{n_{i}u_{i}^{\nu}}{c^{2}}\partial_{\nu}\left[h_{i}u_{i}^{\mu}\right]+g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}p_{i}=-Z\frac{en_{i}}{c}(u_{i})_{\alpha}F^{\mu\alpha}\\
\frac{n_{e}u_{e}^{\nu}}{c^{2}}\partial_{\nu}\left[h_{e}u_{e}^{\mu}\right]+g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}p_{e}=\frac{en_{e}}{c}(u_{e})_{\alpha}F^{\mu\alpha}.\end{split}$ (D.29) In particular, we recover the fact that the stress-energy tensor is divergence free 18 18 18 In general relativity, the stress energy tensor should be equated to a multiple of the Einstein tensor of space time. The fact that the stress energy tensor is divergence free is then a consequence of the Bianchi identity. $\begin{split}&\partial_{\nu}\left[T_{i}^{\mu\nu}+T_{e}^{\mu\nu}+\mathcal{E}^{\mu\nu}\right]=0,\\
&T_{i}^{\mu\nu}=n_{i}h_{i}\frac{u^{\mu}_{i}u^{\nu}_{i}}{c^{2}}+p_{i}g^{\mu\nu},\qquad T_{e}^{\mu\nu}=n_{e}h_{e}\frac{u^{\mu}_{e}u^{\nu}_{e}}{c^{2}}+p_{e}g^{\mu\nu}.\end{split}$ Again, we have two naturally transported (generalized) vorticities: $\begin{split}\omega^{i}_{\alpha\beta}&=\partial_{\alpha}\left[h_{i}(u_{i})_{\beta}\right]-\partial_{\beta}\left[h_{i}(u_{i})_{\alpha}\right]-ZecF_{\alpha\beta},\\
\omega^{e}_{\alpha\beta}&=\partial_{\alpha}\left[h_{e}(u_{e})_{\beta}\right]-\partial_{\beta}\left[h_{e}(u_{e})_{\alpha}\right]+ecF_{\alpha\beta},\\
\end{split}$ which satisfy that $\begin{split}u_{i}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}\omega^{i}_{\alpha\beta}&=-(\partial_{\alpha}u_{i}^{\nu})\omega^{i}_{\nu\beta}+(\partial_{\beta}u_{i}^{\nu})\omega^{i}_{\nu\alpha},\\
u_{e}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}\omega^{e}_{\alpha\beta}&=-(\partial_{\alpha}u_{e}^{\nu})\omega^{e}_{\nu\beta}+(\partial_{\beta}u_{e}^{\nu})\omega^{e}_{\nu\alpha}.\end{split}$ (D.30) We thus see that irrotational flows are well-defined and remain irrotational along the flow. We can easily see from ( D.16 ) that the component of ( D.29 ) parallel to the fluids under consideration are automatically satisfied. Thus to verify ( D.29 ), it suffices to verify it when $\mu=j$ varies between $1$ and $3$ . We now define the unknowns $\begin{split}&\mu_{i}^{j}=c^{-2}h_{i}u_{i}^{j},\quad\mu_{e}^{j}=c^{-2}h_{e}u_{e}^{j},\,\,1\leq j\leq 3,\\
&E^{j}=ecF^{j0},\quad 2B^{j}=-ec^{-1}\in^{jkl}F_{kl},\quad F^{jk}=-ce^{-1}\in^{jkl}B^{l}.\end{split}$ Since we only consider irrotational flows, $B=\nabla\times\mu_{e}=-Z^{-1}\nabla\times\mu_{i}.$ (D.31) at all times and we see that $B$ is uniquely determined by $\mu_{i}$ or $\mu_{e}$ . Now, we can rewrite our evolution system as $\begin{split}\partial_{t}(n_{i}\gamma_{i})+c^{2}\hbox{div}(\frac{n_{i}}{h_{i}}\mu_{i})&=0,\\
\partial_{t}\mu_{i}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}}\partial_{j}h_{i}-ZE^{j}+\frac{c^{2}}{\gamma_{i}h_{i}}\partial_{j}\frac{|\mu_{i}|^{2}}{2}&=0,\\
\partial_{t}(n_{e}\gamma_{e})+c^{2}\hbox{div}(\frac{n_{e}}{h_{e}}\mu_{e})&=0,\\
\partial_{t}\mu_{e}^{j}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{e}}\partial_{j}h_{e}+E^{j}+\frac{c^{2}}{h_{e}\gamma_{e}}\partial_{j}\frac{|\mu_{e}|^{2}}{2}&=0,\\
\partial_{t}E-c^{2}\hbox{curl}(B)+4\pi e^{2}c^{2}[Z\frac{n_{i}}{h_{i}}\mu_{i}-\frac{n_{e}}{h_{e}}\mu_{e}]&=0,\\
\partial_{t}B+\hbox{curl}(E)&=0.\\
\end{split}$ We now set $\begin{split}H_{i}=h_{i}(n_{0}/Z),\quad n_{0}P_{i}Z=p_{i}^{\prime}(n_{0}/Z),\quad H_{e}=h_{e}(n_{0}),\quad n_{0}P_{e}=p_{e}^{\prime}(n_{0})\\
\beta:=\sqrt{\frac{4\pi n_{0}Ze^{2}c^{2}}{H_{i}}},\qquad\lambda:=\sqrt{\frac{4\pi e^{2}}{P_{i}}},\qquad\mu:=\frac{\sqrt{n_{0}ZP_{i}H_{i}}}{c}\end{split}$ and $\varepsilon=\frac{ZH_{e}}{H_{i}},\qquad T=\frac{P_{e}}{P_{i}},\qquad C_{b}=\frac{H_{e}}{n_{0}P_{i}}$ (D.32) and use the rescaling $\begin{split}\gamma_{i}(x,t)=\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(\lambda x,\beta t),\qquad&\gamma_{e}(x,t)=\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}(\lambda x,\beta t),\\
n_{i}(x,t)\gamma_{i}(x,t)=(n_{0}/Z)[\rho(\lambda x,\beta t)+1],\qquad&n_{e}(x,t)\gamma_{e}(x,t)=n_{0}[n(\lambda x,\beta t)+1]\\
\mu_{i}(x,t)=\mu u(\lambda x,\beta t),\qquad&\mu_{e}(x,t)=(\varepsilon\mu/Z)v(\lambda x,\beta t)\\
E(x,t)=n_{0}\lambda P_{i}\widetilde{E}(\lambda x,\beta t),\qquad&B(x,t)=(\lambda\mu/Z)\widetilde{B}(\lambda x,\beta t)\\
h_{i}(n_{i}(x,t))=H_{i}\widetilde{h}_{i}(\widetilde{\rho}(\lambda x,\beta t)),\qquad&h_{i}^{\prime}(n_{i})=Z^{2}P_{i}q_{i}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\rho}),\qquad\widetilde{h}_{i}(0)=1=q_{i}^{\prime}(0)\\
h_{e}(n_{e}(x,t))=H_{e}\widetilde{h}_{e}(\widetilde{n}(\lambda x,\beta t)),\qquad&h_{e}^{\prime}(n_{e})=P_{e}q_{e}^{\prime}(\widetilde{n}),\qquad\widetilde{h}_{e}(0)=1=q_{e}^{\prime}(0)\\
\end{split}$ to obtain the system $\begin{split}\partial_{t}\rho+\hbox{div}[\frac{1+\rho}{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}\widetilde{h}_{i}}u]&=0,\\
\partial_{t}u^{j}-\widetilde{E}^{j}+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}}\partial_{j}q_{i}+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}\widetilde{h}_{i}}\partial_{j}\frac{|u|^{2}}{2}&=0,\\
\partial_{t}n+\hbox{div}[\frac{1+n}{\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}\widetilde{h}_{e}}v]&=0,\\
\varepsilon\{\partial_{t}v+\frac{1}{\widetilde{h}_{e}\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}}\partial_{j}\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}\}+\widetilde{E}^{j}+\frac{T}{\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}}\partial_{j}q_{e}&=0,\\
\partial_{t}\widetilde{E}^{j}-\frac{C_{b}}{\varepsilon}\hbox{curl}(\widetilde{B})+[\frac{1+\rho}{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}\widetilde{h}_{i}}u-\frac{1+n}{\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}\widetilde{h}_{e}}v]&=0\\
\partial_{t}\widetilde{B}+\hbox{curl}(\widetilde{E})&=0\\
\end{split}$ (D.33) which has a similar structure to ( 1.1 ). Indeed, we may Taylor expand to get $\begin{split}\frac{1+\rho}{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}\widetilde{h}_{i}}=1+r_{1}\rho+g_{1},\quad&q_{i}=\rho+r_{2}\rho^{2}+r_{2}^{\prime}|u|^{2}+h_{2},\\
\frac{1+n}{\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}\widetilde{h}_{e}}=1+r_{3}n+g_{3},\quad&q_{e}=n+r_{4}n^{2}+r_{4}^{\prime}|v|^{2}+h_{4},\\
\end{split}$ where $r_{1}$ , $r_{2}$ , $r_{2}^{\prime}$ , $r_{3}$ , $r_{4}$ and $r_{4}^{\prime}$ are constants and $g_{1},g_{3}$ are smooth functions of $(\rho,u,n,v)$ which vanish at the origin $(0,0,0,0)$ together with their gradient, and $h_{2},h_{4}$ are smooth functions of $(\rho,u,n,v)$ which vanish at the origin $(0,0,0,0)$ together with their first and second derivatives. We may thus rewrite ( D.33 ) as $\begin{split}\partial_{t}\rho+\hbox{div}[u]+r_{1}\hbox{div}[\rho u]&=-\hbox{div}[g_{1}],\\
\partial_{t}u^{j}-\widetilde{E}^{j}+\partial_{j}\rho+r_{2}\partial_{j}(\rho^{2})+(r_{2}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2})\partial_{j}|u|^{2}&=T_{2},\\
\partial_{t}n+\hbox{div}[v]+r_{3}\hbox{div}[nv]&=-\hbox{div}[g_{3}],\\
\partial_{t}v+\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{E}^{j}+\varepsilon^{-1}T\partial_{j}n+\varepsilon^{-1}Tr_{4}\partial_{j}(n^{2})+(\varepsilon^{-1}Tr_{r}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2})\partial_{j}|v|^{2}&=T_{4},\\
\partial_{t}\widetilde{B}+\hbox{curl}(\widetilde{E})&=0,\\
\partial_{t}\widetilde{E}-\frac{C_{b}}{\varepsilon}\hbox{curl}(\widetilde{B})+u-v+[r_{1}\rho u-r_{3}nv]&=-g_{1}u+g_{3}v,\end{split}$ (D.34) where $\begin{split}T_{2}&=-\{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}^{-1}-1\}\partial_{j}q_{i}-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}^{-1}\partial_{j}h_{2}-((\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}\widetilde{h}_{i})^{-1}-1)/2\cdot\partial_{j}|u|^{2}\\
T_{4}&=-\{\varepsilon^{-1}T\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}^{-1}-1\}\partial_{j}q_{e}-\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}^{-1}\partial_{j}h_{4}-((\widetilde{h}_{e}\widetilde{\gamma}_{e})^{-1}-1)/2\cdot\partial_{j}|v|^{2}\end{split}$ are simply smooth cubic (or higher order) terms in $(\rho,n,u,v)$ with no particle structure. We can directly observe that the linearization of ( D.34 ) coincides with the linearization of ( 1.1 ). Therefore we consider the same dispersion relations ( 3.12 ) and we define the dispersive unknowns as in ( 3.8 ): $\begin{split}&U_{i}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}\big[\varepsilon^{1/2}R|\nabla|^{-1}\Lambda_{i}n+|\nabla|^{-1}\Lambda_{i}\rho-i\varepsilon^{1/2}RR_{j}v^{j}-iR_{j}u^{j}\big],\\
&U_{e}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}\big[-\varepsilon^{1/2}|\nabla|^{-1}\Lambda_{e}n+R|\nabla|^{-1}\Lambda_{e}\rho+i\varepsilon^{1/2}R_{j}v^{j}-iRR_{j}u^{j}\big],\\
&2U_{b}:=\Lambda_{b}|\nabla|^{-1}Q\tilde{B}-iQ^{2}\tilde{E}\end{split}$ (D.35) with inverse transformation given by $\begin{split}&n=\frac{-|\nabla|\varepsilon^{-1/2}}{\sqrt{1+R^{2}}\Lambda_{e}}(U_{e}+\overline{U_{e}})+\frac{|\nabla|\varepsilon^{-1/2}R}{\sqrt{1+R^{2}}\Lambda_{i}}(U_{i}+\overline{U_{i}}),\\
&\rho=\frac{|\nabla|R}{\sqrt{1+R^{2}}\Lambda_{e}}(U_{e}+\overline{U_{e}})+\frac{|\nabla|}{\sqrt{1+R^{2}}\Lambda_{i}}(U_{i}+\overline{U_{i}}),\\
&v^{j}=R_{j}\left\{\frac{i\varepsilon^{-1/2}}{\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}(U_{e}-\overline{U_{e}})+\frac{-i\varepsilon^{-1/2}R}{\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}(U_{i}-\overline{U_{i}})\right\}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\hbox{Re}(U_{b}^{j}),\\
&u^{j}=R_{j}\left\{\frac{-iR}{\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}(U_{e}-\overline{U_{e}})+\frac{-i}{\sqrt{1+R^{2}}}(U_{i}-\overline{U_{i}})\right\}-2\Lambda_{b}^{-1}\hbox{Re}(U_{b}^{j}).\\
\end{split}$ (D.36) Using these formulas (and in particular the fact that $\partial_{t}n$ and $\partial_{t}\rho$ are exact spatial derivatives so as to counteract the singular relation at $0$ frequency in the definition of $U_{e}$ and to keep the derivative structure in the quadratic part of the nonlinearity $\mathcal{N}_{i}$ ), one quickly sees that $(U_{i},U_{e},U_{b})$ satisfy ( 3.19 ) with quadratic nonlinear terms of the form given in ( 3.20 ) and Lemma 3.3 and cubic nonlinear terms of the form handled by Proposition D.4 . Following the analysis in Section 5 - 8 , we may obtain Theorem D.3 . Fix $h_{i}$ , $h_{e}$ , $c$ , define $\varepsilon$ , $T$ , $C_{b}$ by ( D.32 ) and assume ( 1.9 ). Let $N_{0}=10^{4}$ and assume that $\begin{split}&\|(n^{0}-n_{0},v^{0},\rho^{0}-Z^{-1}n_{0},u^{0},F^{0})\|_{H^{N_{0+2}}}+\|(1-\Delta)(n^{0},v^{0},\rho^{0},u^{0},F)\|_{Z}=\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta},\\
&c\partial_{j}(F^{0})^{j0}+4\pi e[n^{0}\sqrt{1+c^{-2}|v^{0}|^{2}}-Z\rho^{0}\sqrt{1+c^{-2}|u^{0}|^{2}}]=0,\\
&ecF^{0}_{jk}=Z^{-1}\{\partial_{j}[h_{i}(\rho^{0})u^{0}_{k}]-\partial_{k}[h_{i}(\rho^{0})u^{0}_{j}]\}=\partial_{k}[h_{e}(n^{0})v^{0}_{j}]-\partial_{j}[h_{e}(n^{0})v^{0}_{k}],\end{split}$ where $\overline{\delta}>0$ is sufficiently small, and the $Z$ norm is defined in Definition 4.1 . Then there exists a unique global solution $(n_{e}-n_{0},u_{e},n_{i}-Z^{-1}n_{0},u_{i},F)\in C([0,\infty):{H^{N_{0}}})$ of the system ( D.17 )-( D.27 )-( D.28 )-( D.29 ) with initial data $(n_{e}(0),v_{e}(0),n_{i}(0),v_{i}(0),F(0))=(n^{0},v^{0},\rho^{0},u^{0},F^{0})$ . Moreover, for any $t\in[0,\infty)$ , $\begin{split}&ecF^{0}_{jk}=Z^{-1}\{\partial_{j}[h_{i}(v_{i})_{k}]-\partial_{k}[h_{i}(v_{i})_{j}]\}=\partial_{k}[h_{e}(v_{e})_{j}]-\partial_{j}[h_{e}(v_{e})_{k}],\end{split}$ and, with $\beta:=1/100$ , $\begin{split}&\|(n_{e}(t)-n_{0},v_{e}(t),n_{i}(t)-Z^{-1}n_{0},v_{i}(t),F(t))\|_{H^{N_{0}}}\\
&+\sup_{|\alpha|\leq 4}(1+t)^{1+\beta/2}\|D_{x}^{\alpha}(n_{e}(t)-n_{0},v_{e}(t),n_{i}(t)-Z^{-1}n_{0},v_{i}(t),F(t))\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\delta_{0}.\end{split}$ D.3. Cubic nonlinearities We consider an operator of the type $\begin{split}&\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f,g;h](x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}q_{m}(s)m(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})e^{i\Phi(s,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})}\widehat{f}(\xi_{1},s)\widehat{g}(\xi_{2},s)\widehat{h}(\xi_{3},s)dsd\xi_{1}d\xi_{2}d\xi_{3},\\
&\Phi(s,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})=x\cdot(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})+s\left[\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi_{1})-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\xi_{2})\right]\end{split}$ (D.37) where $m(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})=q_{0}(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})q_{1}(\xi_{1})q_{2}(\xi_{2})q_{3}(\xi_{3}),\quad\sup_{0\leq n\leq 3}\|q_{n}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{100}}\leq 1.$ (D.38) In particular, $\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f,g;h](\xi)\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}q_{m}(s)m(\xi-\eta,\eta-\theta,\theta)e^{is\left[\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi-\eta)-\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\nu}(\eta-\theta)\right]}\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta,s)\widehat{g}(\eta-\theta,s)\widehat{h}(\theta,s)dsd\eta d\theta.\\
\end{split}$ We will prove the following proposition Proposition D.4 . Assume that $\|f^{\mu}\|_{H^{N_{0}}\cap Z}+\|f^{\nu}\|_{H^{N_{0}}\cap Z}\leq 2$ and that $h$ satisfies for any $s\in[0,T_{0}]$ , and any $k\in\mathbb{Z}$ $\begin{split}\|P_{k}h(s)\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim\min(2^{-N_{0}k},2^{(1+\beta-\alpha)k}),\\
\|P_{k}h(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim\min(2^{-6k},2^{(1/2-\beta-\alpha)k})(1+s)^{-1-\beta}\end{split}$ (D.39) then there holds that $\sum_{(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J},k_{3}}(1+2^{k_{1}}+2^{k_{2}}+2^{k_{3}})\|\tilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k_{0}}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{B^{1}_{k,j}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{4}m}$ (D.40) for any choice of $\sigma\in\{i,e,b\},\quad\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{0},\quad(k,j)\in\mathcal{J},\quad m\in\{0,\dots,L+1\}$ Proof of Proposition D.37 . Proceeding as for Proposition 5.1 , from ( D.38 ), we may assume that $m(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})=1$ . Then, Plancherel theorem gives that $\begin{split}&\|\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[P_{k_{1}}f,P_{k_{2}}g;P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{m}\sup_{s\in[2^{m-4},2^{m+4}]}\min\{\|P_{k_{1}}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|e^{is\Lambda_{\nu}}P_{k_{2}}g(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P_{k_{3}}h(s)\|_{L^{\infty}},\\
&\|e^{is\Lambda_{\mu}}P_{k_{1}}f(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P_{k_{2}}g(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{3}}h(s)\|_{L^{\infty}},\|e^{is\Lambda_{\mu}}P_{k_{1}}f(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|e^{is\Lambda_{\nu}}P_{k_{2}}g(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P_{k_{3}}h(s)\|_{L^{2}}\},\end{split}$ (D.41) which will be used repeatedly. Let $k_{++}=\max(0,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})$ . We first claim that if $j\leq m/2+N_{0}^{\prime}k_{++}+D^{2}$ where $N_{0}^{\prime}$ is as in ( 5.24 ), then
( D.40 ) holds. We proceed as for Lemma 5.2 . It suffices to show that $\sum_{(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J},k_{3}}2^{k_{++}}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})2^{3j/2}2^{(1/2-\beta)\tilde{k}}\|\tilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{4}m}.$ Using ( 5.17 ), we first observe that $\begin{split}\|\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\|Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|Ef^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P_{k_{3}}h(s)\|_{L^{2}}ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\beta(|k_{1}|+|k_{2}|)}2^{-m}2^{-N_{0}k_{3}}\end{split}$ and therefore $\sum_{(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J},k_{3}\geq k_{++}-10}2^{k_{++}}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})2^{3j/2}2^{(1/2-\beta)\tilde{k}}\|\tilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{4}m}.$ On the other hand, if $k_{3}\leq k_{++}-10$ , then $\max(0,k_{1},k_{2})\geq k_{++}$ and we may proceed as in the proof of Lemma 5.2 using ( D.41 ). We now define three sets $\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{1}=\{(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J}:\,\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\geq j/N_{0}^{\prime}\},\\
&\mathcal{S}_{2}=\{(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J}:\,\min(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\leq-10j\},\quad\mathcal{S}_{3}=\{(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{J}:\,\max(j_{1},j_{2})\geq 10j\}\end{split}$ and claim that if $j\geq m/2+N_{0}^{\prime}k_{+}+D^{2}$ then, for $p=1,2,3$ , we have that $\sum_{(k_{1},j_{1}),(k_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{S}_{p},k_{3}}2^{k_{++}}\|\tilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{B^{1}_{k,j}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{2}m}.$ (D.42) This is done similarly to the proof of Lemma 5.3 . We can now consider each term in the remaining sum separately. More precisely, it suffices to prove that $2^{k_{++}}\|\tilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{B^{1}_{k,j}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{2}m}$ (D.43) provided that $j\geq m/2+N_{0}^{\prime}k_{++}+D^{2},\quad-10j\leq k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\leq j/N_{0}^{\prime},\quad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\leq 10j.$ (D.44) This is similar to the proofs of Lemma 5.5 and 5.6 but in an easier situation.
We first assume that $j+4k/3\leq 0.$ (D.45) In this case, as in Lemma 5.6 , it suffices to prove that $2^{k_{++}}2^{\alpha k}2^{(1+\beta)j}2^{3k/2}\|\mathcal{F}P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{4}(m+j)}.$ On the other hand, Cauchy-Schwartz inequality gives that, for any $\xi$ , $\begin{split}\left|\mathcal{F}P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h](\xi)\right|&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\|f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|(P_{k_{3}}h(s))\cdot f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\max(0,k_{3})}2^{-\beta m}\sup_{s\in[2^{m-2},2^{m+2}]}\|f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-k_{++}}2^{-\beta m}.\end{split}$ which suffices given ( D.45 ). Recall Definition 4.1 . We now show that, whenever $j\geq m/2+N_{0}^{\prime}k_{++}+D^{2},\quad-10j\leq k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\leq j/N_{0}^{\prime},\quad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\leq 10j,$ there holds that $2^{k_{++}}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})\|\mathcal{F}\left\{\tilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\right\}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta^{2}m}.$ (D.46) Indeed, for any $\xi$ , $\begin{split}\left|\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h](\xi)\right|&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\|\mathcal{F}\{Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\cdot f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\}\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{3}}h(s)\|_{L^{2}}ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-N_{0}k_{++}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\|Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-N_{0}k_{++}}2^{-\beta m},\end{split}$ if $\max(0,k_{3})\geq k_{++}$ , and $\begin{split}\left|\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h](\xi)\right|&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\|\mathcal{F}\{Ef^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\cdot P_{k_{3}}h(s)\}\|_{L^{2}}\|f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-N_{0}k_{++}}2^{-\beta m},\end{split}$ if $k_{2}\geq k_{++}$ , and similarly if $k_{1}\geq k_{++}$ . In every case, we easily deduce ( D.46 ). We now consider the $L^{2}$ -bound in Defintion 4.1 . We first assume that $k\geq-3/4j,\quad j\geq m,\quad\min(j_{1},j_{2})\leq(1-\beta^{2})j.$ (D.47) We may also assume that $j_{1}\leq j_{2}$ and $j_{1}\geq-k_{1}\geq-j/N_{0}^{\prime}$ . In this case, using that $\left|\nabla_{\xi_{1}}\left[x\cdot(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})+s\Lambda_{\sigma}(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})-s\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mu}(\xi_{1})-s\widetilde{\Lambda}(\xi_{2})\right]\right|\geq|x|-C2^{m}\geq 2^{j-10}$ we may use Lemma A.2 in ( D.37 ) with $K=2^{j}$ and $\epsilon=\min(2^{k},2^{-j_{1}})$ to see that $|\tilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}(x)\cdot P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};h](x)|\lesssim 2^{-100j}$ from which ( D.42 ) follows easily. Assume now that $j/N_{0}^{\prime}\geq k\geq-3j/4,\quad j\geq m,\quad\min(j_{1},j_{2})\geq(1-\beta^{2})j.$ (D.48) In this situation, using ( 5.18 ), we compute that, when $k_{1}\leq k_{2}$ , $\begin{split}\|P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\|\widehat{f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\mathcal{F}\left\{f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\cdot(P_{k_{3}}h(s))\right\}\|_{L^{2}}ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{3k_{1}/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{m}(s)\|f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\|P_{k_{3}}h(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\beta m}\cdot 2^{-k_{++}}\cdot 2^{3k_{1}/2}\min(1,2^{-10k_{1}})2^{-(1-\beta)(j_{1}+j_{2})},\end{split}$ from which we deduce that $2^{k_{++}}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})2^{(1+\beta)j}\|P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta j}.$ Together with ( D.46 ), this finishes the proof in this case. Finally, we may assume that $m/2+N_{0}^{\prime}k_{++}+D^{2}\leq j\leq m+D,\quad-10j\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq j/N_{0}^{\prime},\quad\max(j_{1},j_{2})\leq 10j.$ In this case, we may simply use ( D.41 ) to conclude that $\begin{split}\|P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim 2^{-(1+2\beta)m}2^{-(N_{0}-1)k_{++}}\end{split}$ and therefore $2^{k_{++}}(2^{\alpha k}+2^{10k})2^{(1+\beta)j}\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}_{j}\cdot P_{k}\tilde{T}^{\sigma;\mu,\nu}_{m}[f^{\mu}_{k_{1},j_{1}},f^{\nu}_{k_{2},j_{2}};P_{k_{3}}h]\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-\beta m}$ which suffices when combined with ( D.46 ).
∎ Lemma D.5 . Assume that $t\in\mathbb{R}$ and that $f\in C^{\infty}$ satisfies $f(0)=0$ and $\sup_{|x|\leq 1,\,\,j\leq N_{0}+3}|f^{(j)}(x)|\leq A.$ (D.49) Let $n\in H^{N_{0}}$ be such that $\|n\|_{H^{N_{0}}}+\|e^{-it\Lambda_{\sigma}}n\|_{Z}\leq 1$ (D.50) for some $\sigma\in\{i,e,b\}$ . Then $\begin{split}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}(2^{-(1+\alpha-\beta)k}+2^{N_{0}k})\|P_{k}(f(n))\|_{L^{2}}&\lesssim_{A,N_{0}}\|n\|_{H^{N_{0}}}+\|e^{-it\Lambda_{\sigma}}n\|_{Z},\\
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}(2^{-(1/2-\beta-\alpha)k}+2^{6k})(1+t)^{1+\beta}\|P_{k}(f(n))\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim_{A,N_{0}}\|n\|_{H^{N_{0}}}+\|e^{-it\Lambda_{\sigma}}n\|_{Z}.\end{split}$ Proof. We first assume that $\|n\|_{H^{N_{0}}}+\|e^{-it\Lambda_{\sigma}}n\|_{Z}=1.$ (D.51) When $k\geq 0$ , the lemma essentially follows from the following easy inequalities, $\begin{split}\|f(u)\|_{H^{N_{0}}}&\lesssim_{N_{0}}\|u\|_{H^{N_{0}}},\quad\hbox{ and }\quad\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f(u)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha}\|\partial^{\beta}u\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}$ This is sufficient for the first inequality; for the second, we first observe that $\begin{split}2^{6k}\|P_{k}(f(P_{\leq k}n))\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim 2^{-k}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 7}\|\partial^{\alpha}P_{k}(f(P_{\leq k}n))\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-k}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 7}\|\partial^{\alpha}f(P_{\leq k}n)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(1+s)^{-1-\beta},\end{split}$ while using that $f(n)-f(P_{\leq k}n)=\int_{0}^{1}f^{\prime}(P_{\leq k}n+sP_{\geq k+1}n)ds\cdot P_{\geq k+1}n$ we see that $2^{6k}\|P_{k}[f(n)-f(P_{\leq k}n)]\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{6k}\|P_{\geq k+1}n\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(1+s)^{-1-\beta}$ which is what we wanted.
If $k\leq 0$ , since $f$ is smooth, we can write $f(n)=C_{1}n+g(n),\quad C_{1}\in\mathbb{R},\,\,\lim_{n\to 0}\frac{g(n)}{n^{2}}\in\mathbb{R}.$ The linear part clearly verifies the appropriate bounds by ( 5.17 ). For the nonlinear part, we use that $\|P_{k}g(n)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}\|\widehat{g(n)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}\|g(n)\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}$ and $\begin{split}\|P_{k}g(n)\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim\|n\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\lesssim(1+s)^{-2(1+\beta)}\\
\|P_{k}g(n)\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim 2^{3k}\|\widehat{g(n)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3k}\end{split}$ and therefore, we can easily get the bounds when $k\leq 0$ . In the general case of ( D.50 ), we may simply write $n=\delta\tilde{n}$ where $\tilde{n}$ satisfy ( D.51 ). Then the Lemma follow by changing $f(x)\to\delta^{-1}f(\delta x)$ which still satisfies ( D.49 ).
